# MISTAKES — read before starting work; append when something goes wrong

Format: `- [phase/task] what went wrong → what to do instead`

- [P1 env] Raw TCP (ssh on :44033) is blocked from the sandbox → don't attempt direct SSH; use the deploy path in STATUS.md.
- [P1 env] Docker daemon is not running at container start → `(dockerd >/tmp/dockerd.log 2>&1 &)` first.
- [P1 env] There is no `postgres:19` tag yet → use `postgres:19beta4` as base.
- [P1-01 env] `docker build` can't clone GitHub (TLS-intercepting proxy) → build with `docker build --secret id=cacert,src=/root/.ccr/ca-bundle.crt -t manythreads/postgres:19 deploy/postgres`; compose reuses the existing image.
- [P1-01] pgvector v0.8.1 does not compile on PG19 (LWLock/slock_t headers moved) → use v0.8.7+ (Dockerfile default).
- [P1 review] One-off workflows with root SSH credentials left in the repo → delete them once used; prefer fixed-purpose workflows with validated inputs.
- [P1 parallel] A kernel agent's untracked files were deleted mid-run by a parallel agent's git operation → parallel agents must never run git stash/clean/checkout/reset; commit own paths early; use 'git pull --rebase --autostash' only.
- [P1 review] RLS helpers trusted session GUCs (app.actor_kind) that any SQL can set → system privilege must come from a real role (current_user), never a settable GUC.
- [P1 e2e] Running playwright from repo root loads two @playwright/test copies → run via 'pnpm e2e' (pnpm -C e2e exec playwright test).
- [P1-13 deploy] On CNPG the owner role is not a superuser, so kernel migrations failed (`permission denied to create extension vector`, then `permission denied to alter role`) → chart creates extensions via `postInitApplicationSQL` (postInitSQL runs in the `postgres` db, not `manythreads`) and makes `manythreads_owner` SUPERUSER like dev compose; the deploy action re-applies both idempotently for existing clusters. CNPG accepted the PG19 beta image, so `postgres.mode: statefulset` stays unused.
- [P1-13 deploy] Image prune sorted tags by name and deleted the freshly imported image (ImagePullBackOff) → prune only tags that are neither the new tag nor used by a pod.
- [P1-13 deploy] `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` failed in the image because tools/bench was missing from pnpm-lock.yaml → docker contexts exclude `tools/`; whoever owns the lockfile should re-run `pnpm install`.
- [P1 CI] Parallel test DBs race on cluster-wide ALTER ROLE (XX000 tuple concurrently updated); advisory locks are per-database so they don't help → retry XX000 in migrate.ts.
- [P1 exit] git push of tags is 403 from the sandbox proxy → tag via the release.yml workflow (dispatch with MCP actions_run_trigger).
- [P2] The old product name was used everywhere → the name is manythreads; never write the old name.
- [P2-10] UI copy mentioned build phases ("created in phase 3") → never expose plan/phase wording in product UI; reviewers check copy.
- [P2 review] Definer/RLS checks only tested 'is admin' → also guard owner rows, same-workspace references (triggers), and terminal states (revoked, accepted) as system-only.
- [P2 review] Inside SECURITY DEFINER functions owned by manythreads_system, app.is_system()/is_workspace_admin() are TRUE (current_user) → in definer code check the caller with app.lookup_workspace_role()/lookup_team_role(), never the is_* helpers. Get-or-create on shared entities (roles) can attach things the caller doesn't own → check ownership.
- [P2 review] trustProxy:true trusts the client-written leftmost X-Forwarded-For → trust exactly one hop (the ingress). Admin-supplied URLs fetched by the server need an SSRF guard (public https only, connect-time DNS check).
- [env] Container restarts kill background agents and the docker daemon → on resume: start dockerd, pnpm db:up, re-create the 5-min stuck-check cron, relaunch interrupted agents pointing them at their uncommitted partial files.
- [P2 screenshots] remote_sh runs the script from stdin (`bash -s`), so a bare `kubectl exec -i` inside it would swallow the rest of the script → give such commands their own stdin (`printf ... | kubectl exec -i`, see remote_set_password); secrets travel on stdin only, never in argv or logs.
- [P2 CI] Visual (W) baselines were generated in the sandbox, where Google Fonts is unreachable from the browser (fallback DejaVu Sans), while CI loaded the real Inter Tight → 8 baseline failures (1px height diffs, 2–3% pixels) → never load fonts from a CDN: the web client self-hosts Inter Tight + JetBrains Mono (`clients/web/public/fonts`, `styles/fonts.css`), and plate renders answer the prototype's Google Fonts requests with the same local files (`useLocalFonts` in tools/plates). Regenerate baselines only after fonts are local, and check a baseline image shows the real font.
- [P3-00] A hoisted read policy (`team_id = ANY ((SELECT app.readable_team_ids('read'))::uuid[])`) is evaluated once at statement start, so `INSERT ... RETURNING` of a row that only this statement makes visible (a new team) failed its own SELECT check → keep an explicit branch that does not look the row up (admins in `teams_select`), or do not RETURNING; and wrap `app.is_system()`/`app.workspace_id()` in `(SELECT ...)` too, a bare call is a per-row function call (10x on 300,000 rows).
- [P3-05 channels] `IF NOT app.lookup_can_team(...)` in a SECURITY DEFINER function did not raise for a non-member: the lookup answers NULL (not false) and `NOT NULL` is NULL, so any caller passed (found by a test that called the sync as Sameera on Marketing) → `coalesce(app.lookup_can_team(...), false)` in every definer-side check; never test a definer guard only with a member who is merely too weak, also test an outsider.
- [P3-05 channels] An RLS `WITH CHECK (EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM app.messages m WHERE m.id = thread_root_id))` compared `m.thread_root_id` with `m.id`: both tables have that column and the inner table wins → qualify the outer column (`thread_follows.thread_root_id`). Also: tables a definer function/trigger writes (channel_members, threads) still need a system policy (`FOR ALL USING (app.is_system())`): the system role is not BYPASSRLS, no policy means no rows.
- [P3-05 channels] A markdown-stripping regex with an open-ended `[^\]]+` ran 4 s on 20,000 `[` (quadratic) → bound every inner quantifier (`{1,500}`) and keep a hostile-input timing test next to the function.
- [P3-07 mentions] A mention lookup that joined `app.actors` from the caller's transaction found only the caller: `actors_select` shows a caller their own row → resolve people through a definer function that checks the caller itself (`app.channel_mention_candidates`), never join `actors` from app-role SQL.
- [P3-07 mentions] `INSERT ... SELECT $1, u.id ...` failed to type `$1` (text, not uuid) → cast parameters in a SELECT list (`$1::uuid`); only VALUES lists infer from the column.
- [P3-06 threads] The 0002 messages trigger made the root's author follow on EVERY reply, so unfollowing a thread you started was undone by the next reply → auto-follow the root author only on the thread's first reply (channels 0003); test follow semantics with an unfollow followed by someone else's reply.
- [P3-07 plain text] Swapping `markdownToPlain` for the shared `toPlainText` took 4.8 s on 20,000 `[` + backticks + `*` (and changed `[[x]]` output) → the shared parser needs the same hostile-input timing test and bounded quantifiers before it can feed `body_plain`; the channels plugin keeps its own function until then.
- [P3] Parallel e2e specs mutated the SHARED e2e server (created channels) and leaked into other specs → every spec that writes data uses its own stack/database (e2e/fixtures/stack.ts). Regex-heavy markdown stripping was super-linear (4.8 s hostile input) → linear scanners + hostile-input timing tests.
- [P3-10 search] A GIN trigram index was ignored behind a row level security policy (sequential scan, 2.3 s at 300,000 rows, even with `enable_seqscan = off`) → the planner may give an index only LEAKPROOF predicates once a policy is a security barrier, and pg_trgm's operators are not marked: the search migration marks `word_similarity_op`, `word_similarity_commutator_op`, `similarity_op` (superuser). Put the indexed column on the left (`col %> query`), and prove it with an EXPLAIN as a member (`enable_seqscan = off` must show the index).
- [P3-10 search] `m.id >= uuidv7(interval '-1 day')` inside a query is a per-row call (uuidv7 is volatile): the range could not use the index (880 ms) → compute it once into a variable (plpgsql parameter) first. Same for any volatile call you expect to be an index bound.
- [P3-10 search] Ranking every trigram match of a common word is the cost, not the index: 1.6 s for 40,000 matches at 1,000,000 messages (heap fetch plus comparison per row), and a four-word phrase pays 100 ms for the index scan alone → slice by recency (BitmapAnd with the id range), compare small sets row by row, and measure phrases, not only words. The planner also picks key-range scans for `%>` unless `enable_indexscan` is off in the function, and a bare `channel_id = ANY(...)` hidden behind the policy's `is_system() OR ...` is not an index qual: repeat it as a plain predicate.
- [P3-10 bench] A synthetic vocabulary of look-alike tokens (`tok119x`, `tok20x`) made every row similar to every query (998,000 of 1,000,000 matches at threshold 0.5) → generate distinct pseudo-words (md5 prefixes) when benchmarking fuzzy matching.
- [P3-08 server] Fastify's built-in text and JSON parsers consumed an upload with a `text/plain` content type before the handler saw the stream (stored empty blobs) → routes with `rawBody` live in their own context with `removeAllContentTypeParsers()` and a `*` parser; test an upload with `text/plain` and `application/json`.
- [P3-08 uploads] Iterating a Node request stream and stopping early destroys the socket, so the 413 never arrives → read with `iterator({ destroyOnReturn: false })`, refuse, then `resume()` to discard the rest. `fetch` with a streamed body keeps a half-sent connection open after an early answer and hangs `server.close()` in tests: use `node:http` for streamed bodies.
- [P3-08 e2e] An api spec granted Lena a channel on the shared e2e server and broke `messages/rls` (her directory was no longer empty) → no spec on the shared server changes what a persona can see (grants, team moves); test those in vitest with a world of their own, and in e2e assert only refusals.
- [P3-09 markup] The shared `toPlainText` was regex-per-construct and ran 1.3 to 4.5 s on 40,000-character hostile input (nested `[`, `*a ` / `_a ` runs, a long run of blanks: `[ \t]+$` unanchored at the start, a lazy `(.*?)` in the heading rule, `\[([^\]\n]+)\]` retried from every `[`) → rewritten as scanners with lookup tables and pointers (each `]` and each closer is judged once), byte-identical to the old output on 700,000 random strings; `test/markup/plain.test.ts` holds 40 hostile inputs at 50 ms. Any new markup regex needs an anchor or a bound, and a hostile case in that list. (`parseMarkup` still checks each claim against all earlier ones: 13,000 mentions cost 180 ms.)
- [P3-09 notifications] A system-side count of someone's unread rows included rows in channels they can no longer read, so the push said 12 while `GET /summary` said 11 → counts that cross RLS must be taken as the person (the routes do); the live push carries no count, and the consumer deletes a person's notifications when `channel.member.removed` takes their access.
- [P3-13 web] The message list anchored the reader's place on the first row to survive older pages being added in front, but the first row is the day divider, which stays first (same-day older messages land after it), so the view jumped and, stuck at scrollTop 0, no scroll event ever asked for the next page → anchor on the first *message* at the viewport top (`anchorable`), and ask for older pages after every commit that leaves the view near the top, not only from scroll events.
- [P3-13 web] A composer inside a hidden panel measured `scrollHeight` 0 and froze its textarea at 0 px; its `autoFocus` also ran while hidden → do not mount a composer until it can be shown, and never write a measured size of 0.
- [P3-13 e2e] `fixtures/stack-server.ts` proxied only HTTP, so the live socket never connected in stack-based visual specs ("Reconnecting…" banner in baselines) → it now proxies the `/ws` upgrade too; any new front for the app must do the same.
- [P3-13 web] Reading `scrollTop`/`offsetTop` in a layout effect after every React commit forced a layout per frame (1 s of 10 s of scrolling) → touch layout only when the rows changed or the view is glued to the bottom; keep offsets in an array shifted in place by a measured row instead of rebuilding 5,000 entries per frame.
- [P3 review] A `channel_members` row kept giving access to a private team channel after the person left the team (and their pushes kept coming) → a membership row only counts while the person is on the channel's team (channels 0004); any access derived from a membership table must also check the thing that membership was granted through, and removal needs a test that the access ends.
- [P3 review] `threads.title` was a copy of the root's text taken once, so a deleted or edited root stayed readable in the inbox, thread search and link resolver → derived copies of user text need a trigger that follows the source (and deletion must clear every copy: `body_plain`, thread title, attachment cards in `meta`).
- [P3 review] An open WebSocket outlived its session (sign-out, revoke, suspension, expiry) → the host re-checks every cookie-session socket against a read-only `sessions.live()` every 15 s and closes it; anything long-lived needs a revalidation path, not only a check at connect. Also cap frame size (`maxPayload`): `ws` takes 100 MiB per frame by default, from anonymous callers on a public route.
- [P3 review] A streamed upload holds the request's transaction (a pooled connection) for as long as the body takes, so slow senders could pin the pool → concurrency slots, an idle timeout and a total deadline on any handler that reads a body inside a transaction.
- [P3 review] LEAKPROOF on pg_trgm operators is only honest if no stored value can make them raise: measured, `word_similarity_op` fails with out of memory on a ~250 MB text (fine at 100 MB). Messages are capped at 40,000 characters, but `body_plain` and `threads.title` had no cap → search 0002 bounds them (100,000 and 200). Before marking a function LEAKPROOF, list every error it can raise and bound the inputs by constraint, not by application code.
- [P3 review] Channel membership outlived team membership (private channels) → visibility helpers must re-check team membership, not just channel_members. Derived columns (threads.title) must follow edits/deletes of their source.
- [P3-15 e2e] A Playwright beforeAll hook that started a per-file stack never ran before the first test: test-scoped fixtures such as `baseURL` are resolved first, so an overridden `baseURL` read `undefined` → start the stack lazily from the fixture (cache by `testInfo.file`) and stop it in `afterAll`; a spec's own `afterAll` runs after the helper's, so it must not call the stack it already stopped. Fixed ports (3100, 4173) made two Playwright runs collide → `support/ports.ts` picks free ports at config load and hands them to the workers in `MANYTHREADS_E2E_PORTS`.
- [P3-15 seed] Seed content that names its channels `#482`-style or ticket numbers with a hash trips the raw-hex lint rule (`#482`, `#4412`) → write "PR 482", "ticket 4412". A seed run in a Kubernetes Job cannot write the server's blob directory (a different pod's disk) → `--no-attachment`; never insert a `files` row without its bytes. A template sync that ran before the seed makes channels with random ids → look channels up by (team, name) after inserting, never assume the fixed id was used.
- [P3 deploy] The seed Job ran `--no-attachment` and the server kept blobs in the pod's own disk (uploads vanished on restart) → blobs live on the PVC `manythreads-blobs` (local-path, RWO, Recreate strategy, one replica); the seed Job mounts the same claim with a pod affinity to the server pod. A screenshot of an inbox must not open the thread first: the Followed tab auto-opens the first thread and reads it, use `?tab=unread` and a fresh stack per run.
- [P3-14 web] A sidebar section wrapped in a `div` hid its heading from the contract spec (`[data-landmark="sidebar"] > .sec` lists direct children only) and a "+ New" row styled `.it` would have been counted as a contract row → a section contributes its heading as a direct child of the sidebar; rows and buttons live in inner wrappers.
- [P3-14 e2e] `getByRole('button', { name: 'Back' })` matched a search hit whose text held "rollback" (accessible names match as case-insensitive substrings) and an `await click()` on a toggled-closed popover waited for the whole test timeout, reporting only a context-close error → use `exact: true` for short names, set `use({ actionTimeout })` in specs that drive popovers, and never click a toggle twice in a row without checking its state.
- [P3-14 e2e] Headless Chromium answers `Notification.permission === 'denied'` even with the context permission granted → drive "Allow browser alerts?" with an init-script stand-in for `window.Notification` (and `document.hasFocus = () => false`), and assert the alert title it received.
- [P3 gate vt] A plate clip at a fractional page offset painted every line a fraction of a pixel off the live screen's (one whole pixel row of difference on some rows, 9% instead of 4%), and a `fullPage` screenshot resized the viewport, which the virtual list answered by jumping to its end → move the plate frame onto the pixel grid before the clip, capture a region that fits the window without `fullPage`, give the start-of-channel header whole-pixel line heights, and paint masks on both images (the ratio counts only unmasked pixels).
- [P3 gate bench] A search benchmark that passed once (296 ms) failed the next run (302 ms): its p95 sat inside the distribution of one query, the four-word phrase, which paid the trigram index scan twice (a one-day slice, then the whole history) → find which query owns the p95 before trusting a pass, and take the budget line to a measured margin (182 ms) instead of rerunning until it is green.
- [P3 gate shell] Moving the bell into a band that is replaced when a thread loads remounted it and closed its popover mid-click (a flaky e2e that was a real flicker), and drawing a screen's own header band removed the shell's `header` landmark that three identity specs read → a control that must survive a screen's loading states lives in a fixed element that is not part of what loads, and the landmark moves with the band.
- [P3 gate mobile] The closed sidebar drawer (`translateX(-100%)`) still cast its `box-shadow` into the screen from beyond the left edge, visible as a grey strip on every 390 px baseline → a shadow belongs to the open state only.
- [P3 gate e2e] A new spec on the shared e2e server had Priya reply in a thread: she then followed it, and `threads/inbox.spec.ts` (which says "no other spec posts in threads she is in") failed about one run in four, in CI and locally → a spec that makes a persona follow, join or reply to something runs on a stack of its own (`startStack`), whatever the persona; a flaky spec after adding a spec means look at what the new one changed for shared personas first.
- [P4-00 realtime] A live-push loop (`for (person of audience) await pushToPerson(...)`) made a 2,000-member post 2,000 statements → push an audience with `ctx.realtime.pushToPeople` / `pushMany` (one statement); count statements in a test with a fake `tx`.
- [P4-00 markup] `parseMarkup` checked every candidate claim against all earlier claims (13,000 mentions: 180 ms) → each phase finds its candidates in ascending order, so sweep the sorted claimed list with two pointers and merge; keep a timing test (13,000 mentions < 30 ms) and compare against the old output on random strings when rewriting a parser.
- [P4-00 server] `app.close()` hung for 72 s (the keep-alive timeout) when the last streamed response finished just after Fastify's one-time idle-connection close → `close()` sweeps `server.closeIdleConnections()` every 100 ms until the server is down; a "hook timed out in afterAll" after a test that ends with a download means look here first.
- [P4-00 GC] A garbage collector needs to enumerate the store, not only the database: with no `list` on the blob interface, blobs of cascade-deleted rows and of uploads that failed to commit could never be found → the contract has `list`; a destructive job also needs a grace period (an upload's bytes exist before its row), a dry-run mode, a typo-means-dry-run default and a guard for "the database looks empty" (wrong database).
- [P4-13 s3] The AWS SDK's default flexible checksums (trailing CRC32 on every upload) are refused by older S3-compatible stores → `requestChecksumCalculation: 'WHEN_REQUIRED'`. MinIO no longer publishes images to Docker Hub or quay.io (the pull is 401 or "repository does not exist") → pin the mirror `ghcr.io/coollabsio/minio:RELEASE.2025-10-15T17-29-55Z`.
- [P4-03 git runner] A timeout killed the shell wrapper but its child kept the stdout pipe open, so `close` never fired and the call hung past its deadline → start git detached (own process group), kill the group and destroy the pipes. `spawn` with a working directory that does not exist reports ENOENT, which reads as "git is missing": fall back to the temp directory when the repo directory is absent, and let `resolve` answer null for a repo that is not there.
- [P4-04 index] A trigram GIN index on `repo_entries` was ignored on 3,000 rows (a full scan of the primary key costs less, even with `enable_seqscan = off`) → prove "the index serves a member under the policy" with about 100,000 rows and the natural planner, not by forcing it on a toy table.
- [P4-02 writer] Throwing from the commit route rolls back the whole request transaction, including a repository that request had just created (its first commit was already in git) → refusals (409 conflict, 422, 403) are returned as the error envelope, not thrown, so earlier work in the transaction commits; and the next write re-indexes any commit git holds that the index lacks (a rolled-back transaction must never lose history). Order commit rows by `(committed_at, seq)`: git dates have one-second resolution.
- [P4-05 guard] The audit event of a denied bot write is stored with the system actor in `events.actor_id` (the sink writes as system); the bot is `payload->>'actorId'` → assert on the payload. The bot also needs a `files.write` grant: the broker denies a bot with no allowlist entry even for `pages/`.
- [P4 tests] Four test suites at once exhausted Postgres connection slots and a first-time 20-parallel write answered 500 where it passes alone (4 of 4 reruns) → keep per-world pools small (8) and rerun a "flaky" concurrency test alone before changing the code.
- [P4-09 viewers] pdfjs-dist 6 (modern build) needs `Map.getOrInsertComputed`, which Chromium 141 (Playwright) and older WebViews lack, so every page failed with "this[#e].getOrInsertComputed is not a function" → import `pdfjs-dist/legacy/build/pdf.mjs` and its legacy worker (`?url`), never the modern build; pdf.js has no `PDFDocumentProxy.destroy()` in v6, destroy the loading task. An embedded app's refused `fetch` logs a console error by design: specs that assert "no console errors" skip messages whose location is `/repo/app/`.
- [P4-09 markdown] `marked` (inside @tiptap/markdown) takes 3.3 s for 15,000 characters of `*a *a ...` (quadratic emphasis) → pages above 200,000 characters open as raw text only, and an untouched page is never re-serialised (the viewer keeps the file's bytes until a user edit).
- [P3 gate vt] Plate 1 (thread) passed at 4.6% on the PR and failed at 6.85% on main with identical code (not reproducible locally, even at 12x CPU throttle): the shots were taken after `document.fonts.ready` (answers at once when layout has not yet asked for a face, so a slow runner screenshots the fallback font, on the plate page too) and a fixed 150 ms wait after the scroll (the virtual list was still measuring rows); the reply counters also carry a wall-clock time ("last 11:08") that was unmasked → `fontsLoaded()` loads every face explicitly, `scrollToFirstDay` polls until the day label has stayed put for 5 frames, `settle` waits for network idle, `.replies` is masked; never gate a pixel shot on `fonts.ready` alone or on a fixed sleep.
- [P4-08 diff] `git diff <empty tree sha> <commit> -- path` fails with "bad object 4b825dc..." when the repository has no empty-tree object, but only with `--end-of-options` → diff a root commit with `git diff-tree --root` (no first argument at all), never name the empty tree.
- [P4-07 pages] A body schema with `path: PagePath` (must start with `pages/`) answered 400 for `bots/x/BOT.md`, so the "four guarded paths are 403 with an audit event" requirement could never be met for `pages.write` → accept any valid repo path in the schema and refuse in the handler (403 guarded, 400 elsewhere); and the broker's path guard only covered `files.*` verbs, so a new path-writing capability must be added to `isFilesMutation` or a bot with its grant writes `bots/`.
- [P4-06 tree] A repo folder named `channels/` would collide with the virtual `channels/<name>/` attachment folders of the Files tree → the writer refuses `channels/` (any case) in the repo; whenever one tree is drawn over two stores, reserve the namespace the second store appears under.
- [P4-06 e2e] An isolated e2e stack seeded with `personas` has no template channels (the directory is empty), unlike the vitest world after `ensureChannels` → create the channel the spec needs through the API. A spec whose last test reads what earlier tests wrote needs `test.describe.configure({ mode: 'serial' })`: tests of one file may land on different workers, each with its own stack.

- [P4-13 seed] The v3 seed wrote its attachment straight into storage-local's directory under a key it derived itself, so it could only work with the local store (S3 got a row with no object) → seed bytes go through the `storage` provider (`put` chooses the key; `head` decides whether a re-run must put them again), and a test runs the seed against a fake non-local provider and, with `MANYTHREADS_TEST_S3=1`, MinIO. Seed text that contains a colour (an app's CSS) must use `rgb()`: a raw hex literal fails lint.
- [P4-10 token] A token in the query string would not reach an embedded app's sub-resources (`<script src="__manythreads.js">` resolves against the page's path and drops the query) → the per-open token is a path segment (`/repo/app/~mta.<token>/<folder>/...`). Tests of path traversal through `fetch` or Playwright prove nothing: the URL parser resolves `..` and `%2e%2e` before sending → send the raw request line with `node:http`. `repo.blob` on a folder answers 400 ("is a folder"), not 404.
- [P4 review C1] The embedded-app CSP and sandbox were added by a hook that tested the RAW `req.url` against `^/api/teams/[^/]+/repo/app/`, but find-my-way decodes the path before routing, so `/repo/%61pp/...` reached the app handler with no CSP and no sandbox (same-origin script execution for anyone who clicked a link to a member's file) → a security header must never depend on a second, hand-written URL match: the handler sets `embeddedAppHeaders()` itself, hooks key on the MATCHED route (`req.routeOptions.url`, `req.params`), and the app route has one canonical spelling (any other is 404). Test every encoding of the path (`%61pp`, `ap%70`, `%2561pp`, case, doubled slashes) against the real route.
- [P4 review M1] The writer read co-author trailers back out of the WHOLE commit message, which is user text, so a typed `Co-authored-by:` forged attribution and a shape-valid but non-UUID id made History answer 400 for every member (and a `-`×36 id raised 22P02 in reindex) → a value read back from stored text is a claim: build trailers only from the validated argument, neutralise attribution lines in user text, read only the final trailer block, validate every id with the shared `ActorId` before it reaches a uuid column or a response schema, take History's co-authors from the index, and test a poisoned commit made behind the writer's back.
- [P4-11 diff] The first line diff listed what came in before what went out (`+ new`, `- old`) because the walk preferred "add" on a tie → a unified diff lists deletions first; prefer `del` when the two choices are equal, and unit-test the order of a one-line change.
- [P4-11 e2e] `fixtures/stack-server.ts` served `.mjs` as `application/octet-stream`, so pdf.js's worker module never loaded and every PDF showed "could not be opened" in stack-based specs (the shared `vite preview` origin was fine) → every extension the built client loads needs a type in the stack's static table; a PDF that "cannot be opened" in a spec means look at the console's MIME error first, not at the PDF.
- [P4-11 e2e] Specs that start their own stack paid the 50 s build-and-three-servers start-up of `pnpm e2e` on every run → build the client once (`vite build`) and run with `MANYTHREADS_URL`, `MANYTHREADS_API_URL` (both any URL) and `MANYTHREADS_E2E_API_ONLY=1`: no shared servers, no sign-in states, 15 s per file. The seeded repo's files are made within the same second, so a list sorted "recent" is not stable between runs: sort a W baseline's list by name.
- [P4-11 web] A file open in the editor reloaded after every save (the screen refetched the row and built new content) and, worse, did not reload after a restore from History (the same object was kept) → the content object remembers the blob it read or wrote (`sha()`); it is rebuilt only when the row's blob is another one than that (a restore, a move), never after its own save.
- [P4-11 web] Hiding a hidden file and a missing one the same way (403 for both) made the panel say "Page not found" for a file the person may not read, and the generic not-found page for a missing file named a page → a 403 is "You do not have access", a 404 is "This file no longer exists"; never turn a 403 into null.
- [P4-11 plates] The prototype's tree is 220 px wide in the Files plate and 230 px in the repo plate, and its list table has six columns that overflow beside a 380 px panel (the plate clips them) → one width inside both 6 px bands (225), and the "Where" column goes first when the list is narrower than 720 px (container query), instead of copying a clipped table.

- P4: a screen stylesheet (files.css) redefined the shared `.linkish` class and added an always-present empty flex slot to the shell
  header; this broke the sign-in and phone channel W baselines. Prefix screen classes; hide empty slots (`:empty { display: none }`);
  run the whole visual suite before the gate, not only your area's specs.
- P4 tests: a raw `pg.Pool`/`pg.Client` in a test needs an `'error'` listener: `dropTestDatabase` runs `DROP DATABASE ... WITH (FORCE)`, which terminates any connection still open (57P01) and an unhandled 'error' event crashed a CI run ("Uncaught Exception" attributed to jobs.test.ts, all tests green) → use `testPool`/`testClient` from `@manythreads/test-utils`, never `new pg.Pool`/`new pg.Client` in tests; end every worker and pool before the drop.
- [windows-dev] `pnpm typecheck` passed on Linux but failed on Windows (TS1149/TS2305): with both `channels/markdown.ts` and `channels/Markdown.tsx` in one directory, tsc probed `Markdown.ts` first and the case-insensitive filesystem matched `markdown.ts`, so `import ... from './Markdown'` loaded the wrong module → in one directory no two files may have names equal only in case across `.ts`/`.tsx`; the components are `MessageMarkdown.tsx` / `SearchHighlight.tsx` and the lowercase parser/highlight util keeps its name (renamed in the Windows dev-env setup; see WINDOWS_DEV.md).
- [P5 password e2e] Minimum password length 12 → 8 updated every vitest fixture and snapshot but missed `e2e/identity/account.spec.ts`, which asserted the OLD boundary: it filled `'too-short'` (9 characters — valid at min 8) and expected the Update button disabled; CI's `End-to-end` step failed on `50f2fd1` while every vitest suite stayed green → when a product constant moves, grep the e2e specs for literals that sat just the other side of the OLD boundary (9–11 chars for min 12), and remember the devbox gate runs vitest only: `pnpm e2e <spec>` is a separate, explicit run.
- [P4 e2e CI] `e2e/files/embedded-app.spec.ts` failed on main (run 37017976239, 248 passed / 1 failed) waiting for `html[data-probed="1"]` inside the sandboxed app frame: the failure snapshot already showed the probe's JSON written, so the app had finished — the 15 s budget was simply gone before the frame finished loading on a runner sharing its CPU with the whole suite, while the same spec ran 12/12 green on the devbox → a wait whose subject only ever *gets slower* under load (iframe load before the probe) gets the generous budget (30 s) and, where possible, is started before the work that competes with it; never conclude "flaky, ignore" from a local green run alone — read the error snapshot, it says which side was late.
- [devbox e2e] `pnpm e2e` on the devbox failed at once with `Executable doesn't exist at /root/.cache/ms-playwright/...` — the devbox had never run Playwright; after `playwright install chromium` it failed host validation (missing system libs) → run both: `pnpm -C e2e exec playwright install chromium` **and** `pnpm -C e2e exec playwright install-deps chromium`; only then does a remote e2e run mean anything. Remote gates (`remote-gates.sh`) run vitest only, so this never showed up before.
- [P5-03 schema] `bots/0001_bots.sql` declared `thread_id uuid REFERENCES app.threads (id)` and the migration died with `column "id" referenced in foreign key constraint does not exist` (42703) — a migration runs at every server start, so 45 test files failed at once, far from the actual line → before writing a foreign key, read the referenced table's own definition: `app.threads` is keyed by `root_message_id` (one row per root message, channels 0002), not `id`. And migrate first with the smallest test that creates a database (one `createTestDatabase` file) before the whole gate: a schema error is one line, and the full suite only tells you so after a minute of noise.
- [P4 embedded-app e2e] The second embedded-app test failed at `expect(frame).toBeDefined()` while its own error snapshot showed the app **loaded and probed** inside the frame — the snapshot is captured *after* the assertion throws, so it records a state the assertion legitimately could not see. The real race: `expect(iframe).toBeAttached()` only proves the element exists, and `page.request.get(APP)` answering says nothing about the frame's navigation having committed, so `page.frames()` still held `about:blank` → an attached `<iframe>` is not a navigated frame: find the frame if it is already there, else `page.waitForEvent('framenavigated', { predicate })`, and never conclude "the code must be wrong, the picture shows it working" from a failure snapshot whose timing you have not checked.
- [kernel read-state] `markRead` validates `remaining` only after the monotonic early-return, so a test that passed
  a random uuid as the position flaked about one run in 160: when the random id sorted below the stored
  `last_read_id` the call answered the current row instead of throwing RangeError (CI red on a BUILD.md-only push)
  → never use a random id for a position a test asserts on; use a real message id (equal advances, `<=`).
- [actions] Every push to main fired three workflow runs (ci ~12 min, release + screenshots no-ops) because
  screenshots.yml triggered off every `release` completion: Actions minutes are limited, so nothing but build/deploy
  may trigger automatically — screenshots.yml is workflow_dispatch only, and phase/task screenshots run on the devbox
  over SSH instead (live.shots.ts via Remote.ps1, pictures synced down).
- [P5-04 deploy] Never wrap Ship.ps1 as `& ...\Ship.ps1 *>&1 | Tee-Object ...` under PowerShell 5.1 while
  `$ErrorActionPreference='Stop'` is in effect (Ship.ps1 sets it): ssh's stderr progress lines merge into the stream
  as terminating NativeCommandError records, so the first run died at remote-gates `db:up` reporting a native-command
  error instead of the real output — run Ship.ps1 plain.
- [P5-26 deploy, the same trap wearing a different hat] Redirecting the *whole script* is the same mistake: `.\Ship.ps1 >
  temp\ship.log 2>&1` also merges ssh's stderr (` Container compose-postgres-1 Running ` from `db:up`) into the
  NativeCommandError stream and aborts the run at the remote-gates stage — this time *after* Sync-Up and *before* the
  deploy, so the live site silently keeps the previous build while the local log looks like a gate failure. → run
  Ship.ps1 with no redirection at all (the harness captures both streams itself, which is not a PowerShell pipe); if a
  transcript is wanted, capture it on the devbox side (`Remote.ps1 'cmd > /tmp/x.log 2>&1'`) and pull the file.
- [P5-08 web tests] The first run of the new endpoint test reported "no request was sent" for all thirteen cases while
  the endpoint calls themselves resolved 200: the stubbed transport returned a canned reply but never recorded the
  request, so every assertion on method/url/body had nothing to look at. The failure was loud only because the helper
  that reads the capture throws when it is empty — indexing `seen[seen.length - 1]` would have been `undefined` and the
  assertions would have been vacuous, or worse, `expect(undefined).toBe(...)` on a case that happened to match nothing
  → when a test asserts on a *captured* interaction, make an empty capture a failure in one helper (`last()` throws)
  and route every assertion through it; and note that a transport that answers without recording is the failure mode,
  not a wrong URL — "no request was sent" means the stub, not the client.
- [P5-08 fake-llm] `tools/fake-llm` answered 401 to `POST /model/made/up` and the new test asserted 404, so the fake
  looked wrong; it was the assertion: a management path must refuse an unauthenticated caller *before* revealing whether
  the route exists, or a probe can enumerate the proxy's routes for free → when a fake and a test disagree, decide which
  one is modelling the real thing before "fixing" either; here the fake kept its 401-before-404 and the test now asserts
  both orders (401 anonymous, 404 keyed).
- [P5-04 loader] A loader test waited for the seed's `bots/coder/BOT.md` and timed out with the loader apparently dead: `createRepoWorld` calls `seedWorld(server.db)` **without** `repo: true`, so seed v4 never writes a repository at all (repo-git's own tests all make their own commits, which is why nothing noticed) → to test against the real fixture, call `seedRepo(server.db, { attachments: false })` in that file's `beforeAll`; never flip the flag inside the shared world helper (repo-git history tests assert their own exact commit sequences). Also: `RAISE ... ERRCODE = 'no_data_found'` surfaces as SQLSTATE **P0002**, not 02000 — assert what pg actually reports.
- [P5-01 Remote.ps1] Inner double quotes (or `=`) inside the Remote.ps1 command break PowerShell arg parsing (`accepts argument ...`) → keep remote one-liners free of inner double quotes; anything complex goes in a repo file, Sync-Up, then run by path.
- [P5-01 tsx] A scratch tsx server answered 401 to the dev-actor header: the header is honoured only with NODE_ENV=test (vitest sets it, plain tsx does not) → run scratch servers as `NODE_ENV=test pnpm exec tsx ...`.
- [P5-01 hermes] `Platform("manythreads")` raises ValueError until the real platform_registry holds the entry (`_missing_` checks `is_registered`); `hermes plugins doctor` needs `__init__.py` re-exporting `register` → prove adapters through the real registry + doctor, never a fake ctx. Hermes deps come from `uv run --project hermes-agent` (syncs the lockfile; `--no-sync` leaves an empty venv).
- [windows git] This Windows checkout had no git identity (past commits were made on the devbox) → set repo-local `user.name`/`user.email` once.
- [P5-01 shots] `page.request` shares the browser context's cookies: a public-route call went out WITH the sign-in session cookie and no CSRF header, so the host answered 403 (cookie-without-CSRF, by design — forged probes without cookies got the handler's 400/401). Public-door shots use a fresh anonymous context (the true caller shape); read the answer before closing it (`json()` after `close()` is "Response has been disposed").
- [P5-01 CRLF] A Windows tree with CRLF worktree files syncs CRLF to the devbox and breaks byte-sensitive tests there (csv.test.ts split on '\n' saw '\r'): keep `* text=auto eol=lf` in `.gitattributes` (LF blobs + LF checkouts, no per-OS conversion). Never `* text` bare — it marks PNGs/woff2 as text and corrupts them on checkout/renormalize. `git reset --hard` / `checkout-index -f` can skip stat-fresh files: delete + `git checkout -- <file>` forces the rewrite. `git add --renormalize` fixes CRLF blobs without touching binaries (only with `text=auto`).
- [P5-04 loader] A JSDoc one-liner describing the loader spelled the folder glob `bots/*/BOT.md`: the `*/` inside the block comment **closed it early**, so the rest of the line became code, a backtick opened a runaway template literal, and tsc reported TS1443/TS1434/TS1005 twenty lines away from the real cause → never write `*/` inside a block comment; describe a glob as `bots/*` (or use a `//` line comment). When parse errors point at code that looks fine, suspect an unterminated string/comment *above* them first.
- [P5-05 web] `useDefinition` built the edit draft in a `useMemo` **from** the loaded file instead of holding it in state, so `patch`'s `if (!d) return d` (the state was always null) dropped every keystroke: the Identity input reverted on fill, and Save committed a re-serialised *old* definition yet still said "Saved". `dirty` also compared the canonical render to the file's raw bytes, so a file the renderer reformats (YAML `[default]` → block) looked edited the moment it opened → the draft is `useState` seeded from the file by an effect, `reset` restores the loaded version, and "changed" is judged on the canonical render on both sides, never on the file bytes.
- [P5-05 bots] Rebuilding a stored event in a plugin must spread the payload next to the envelope (`{ ...row.payload, type, schemaVersion, workspaceId }`), not nest it under a `payload` key: `parseEvent` then finds none of the event's fields, throws inside the read handler and the whole roster/bot page answers 500. A plugin cannot import the kernel's `eventToRaw`; the spread is the copy that works (loader.test.ts could import it only because it is a test).
- [P5-05 bots] `toBot` now requires `name`/`role`, so the RLS mapper test's hand-written column list (`SELECT id, workspace_id, …`) produced `undefined` for both and the entity parse threw → any test that selects bot columns explicitly (or inserts rows without them) must carry the new columns; the migration defaults `''` keep the INSERT working, but the SELECT must name them.
- [P5-05 gates] Local `Check.ps1` failed inside a file this thread never touched (`clients/web/test/mock.test.ts`, an in-progress edit by whoever else shares the checkout — a stray `});` after an inserted test, plus a missing narrowing on a `session.person`/`session.role` union) → a shared working tree can carry another writer's half-saved file, and it blocks everyone's gates; repair only the broken hunk (parse error + the one type error), leave the rest of their edit intact, and say so. Do not revert their file.
- [P5-06 gateway-llm] `revokeBotKey` deleted the secret before the `llm_keys` row that references it (`secret_id uuid REFERENCES app.secrets`), so the delete died on `llm_keys_secret_id_fkey` — the test caught it, the design did not: when two rows reference each other, the deleter goes first and the referenced row last (the sign-in provider route deletes its row before `ctx.secrets.delete` for the same reason). Also: the sync-key job first guarded on a JOIN through `app.actors`, which is not what "the bot still exists" means — the loader creates the actor, so an actor-less bot row (every test fixture) silently skipped the mint; guard on the row you actually mean (`app.bots`).
- [P5-06 RLS] A member's `DELETE FROM app.llm_providers` did **not** raise `42501` — a missing USING clause filters the rows away and reports success, so the assertion "refused" must be `count` unchanged (an INSERT *does* raise 42501, through WITH CHECK, and a table without the DELETE grant raises 42501 as a privilege error) — assert the effect, not the SQLSTATE, whenever the operation is RLS-filtered rather than privilege-denied.
- [P5-05 bots] Seed v4 wrote the bot placeholder `bots/<slug>/BOT.md` as the team's **lead**, which is fine under lead-or-admin but breaks the moment `bots/` splits "a lead edits an existing bot" from "only an admin adds one": Marketing's lead (Tariq) is a workspace member, so a fresh seed got 403 → `repoSteps` now takes `botOwner` (a workspace admin) for that one step. When a seeding fixture writes through the real writer as a persona, a tightened write rule can invalidate the fixture, not the rule — check every seeded step's actor before shipping the rule.
- [P5-07 windows] `packages/shared/test/events/phase3-contracts.test.ts` walks the tree and only counts a directory as a test folder when `dir.endsWith('/test')`; `join()` on Windows yields `\test`, so the walker finds zero files and the test fails locally with `expected 0 to be greater than or equal to 8` while passing on the devbox — a path-walking test that fails only on Windows is a separator bug in the test, not a regression in the code; confirm on the devbox (Linux) before touching anything.
- [P5-07 tests] Two failed assertions in the new preflight suite were the test's own fault, not the code's: a sliding-window retry-after is recomputed from the clock, so two refusals seconds apart differ (assert it shrinks, never that it is equal), and `spendSummary` covers the whole workspace, so earlier tests' ledger lines are in the total (assert the bot's own row exactly and the total as a lower bound).
- [P5-06/P5-07 kernel] A server that boots N job workers held **one pooled connection per worker** (plus one for the outbox consumer and one for the realtime loop) because `listen()` called `pool.connect()` and kept the client for the listener's life; `startServer` builds its system pool with `createSystemPool(url)` = max 10, so once the queues of the tree (files, repo-git ×2, slack-compat, kms.rewrap, the gateway's three, the test plugin) plus the consumer reached the ceiling, the boot's *next* transaction (the scheduler's `schedule()` writes) waited forever for a connection nobody would release — the server hung before listening and, with no handles left, the process just exited 0. Every server-booting suite failed with `Hook timed out in 120000ms/180000ms`, 46 files at once, and it looked exactly like a busy shared box — but it reproduced on an idle box with zero leftover test databases, and `startTestServer({ jobWorkers: false })` booted in 1.4 s against the hung default: **bisect with the cheap option first (here, disable the resource), then count the resource the code takes for its whole life; a hang whose process exits 0 is a promise nothing will ever resolve.** Fixed by sharing one LISTEN per (pool, channel) in the process.
- [P5-07 tests] `NOTIFY` takes no bound parameters (the channel is an identifier, the payload a literal) — `NOTIFY channel, $1` is a syntax error; use `SELECT pg_notify($1, $2)` when the channel or payload comes from a variable. A DB-backed suite also needs an explicit per-test timeout: the 5 s default turned a working test into "Test timed out in 5000ms" while it waited on a NOTIFICATION.
- [P5-09 RLS] The gateway's source-log write failed with `new row violates row-level security policy for table "run_source_log"` although the INSERT policy's WITH CHECK (`run_id = app.run_id()`) passed: `INSERT ... RETURNING` also demands SELECT visibility of the row it returns, and a run's lines are readable with the run's *team* — a bot actor is not on it, so it cannot see the line it just wrote. The mirror trap: computing `max(seq)` in the caller's transaction then reads nothing, gives every line position 0, and the second line collides on the primary key. → when a write must read rows its actor cannot SELECT, put the read and the write behind ONE narrow definer function in the same transaction (bots 0004, owned by manythreads_system: `app.is_system()` is true inside it); and on an insert, "violates RLS" points at RETURNING/policies before the WITH CHECK.
- [P5-09 broker] A granted `pages.write` was refused as `needs a path to be authorized`, and every path-guarded mutation vanished from `tools/list`, because the gateway authorized with the tool name alone: the path guard's input is the call's own arguments, and a listing has no arguments. → the payload must reach the guard for a call (`authorize` with `{ path }` taken from the input, the same path the handler will write), while "may I offer this tool" is a different question from "may I do this" (`broker.check`, answered at grant level) — a listing that runs the call's guards hides exactly the tools it exists to offer.
- [P5-10 shared] `parseBotMd` returned the parsed **frontmatter only** — the body was computed by `splitBotMd` and then dropped — which was fine while the only caller was the loader and silently wrong the moment a runtime needed the definition's prose (SPEC §7.1: the body *is* the runtime's `SOUL.md`). → a parser that returns "what today's caller needs" is a parser that will be re-implemented tomorrow: return the whole document (frontmatter **and** body) and let each caller ignore what it does not use; when a new caller needs a field, check the parser before reading the raw bytes a second time.
- [P5-10 loader] The new assertion "the compiled allowlist is exactly the native list" failed on the first devbox run because the bot also carried a `tasks.handoff` grant from `handover.mayTag` — **two fields of one file are compiled by the same loader**, each owning different grants. → when asserting a compiled set, assert the whole set the loader owns, not the part the change was about; and prefer `ORDER BY` + `toEqual` over a containment check, because "the grant I expected is there" hid nothing here but would hide a stale one.
- [P5-10 runtime door] The SDK's `BotRuntimeScope.invoke` is documented as "call a capability the run may use, **through the
  broker**", but the router handed providers `ctx.runs.asBot(run, (tx) => ctx.runs.invoke(tx, capability, args))` — and
  `ctx.runs.invoke` is a raw handler call ("The broker is not involved — authorize first", sdk/types.ts). The broker was asked
  at the MCP door (`tools/call`) and nowhere else, so a definition with no `messages.post` grant could still post: the allowlist
  compiled from `capabilities.native` bound the model's tools but not the runtime's own calls, and `runtime-rules`' whole point
  (a bot's declared steps) ran unauthorized. The first database-backed run test caught it on the second case. → a door that
  takes a capability name must authorize it at the same place it invokes it (`ctx.runs.authorize` + path from the call's own
  arguments + `needsApproval` fails closed), and the reply the job posts must go through that same door rather than around it;
  when a helper's documented contract and its implementation disagree, the test that exercises the whole path is the one that
  says which one is real.
- [P5-10 test] The run test's stand-in recorded `process.env.HERMES_HOME` in the field it filtered on and the fixture compared
  it to the profile's *name* (`coder-<runId>`): every record was present and the filter matched none, so two run cases failed as
  "no process started" while the reply and the run row (the assertions above them) passed → when a fixture writes a directory
  for a child, record the bare name beside the absolute path (or compare on the last path segment); a filter that silently
  matches nothing looks exactly like a process that never started.
- [P5-10 hermes] The provider listened for its child's exit **inside the event generator** (`child.once('close', …)` ran on the
  first read), so a process that died before the job took its first event — a bad argv, no model, ENOENT — emitted `close`
  into the void: the run never saw an exit, never synthesised its failure `result`, and held a job worker forever. The new
  start/stop test (a stand-in child that ends before the stream is read) failed as a 5 s **timeout**, not an error, which is
  what "awaits an event that already happened" looks like. → register a child's exit listeners at spawn, in the same function
  that spawns it, and pass the promise to whoever reads the stream; a listener attached later than the event it waits for turns
  a failure into a hang. (A readline iterator over an *already-ended* stream does terminate — 0 lines — so the stream was never
  the suspect.)
- [P5 review] A page's "why is this broken" state was read from the newest `bots.bot.invalid` event, so a repaired BOT.md kept its red banner forever (and the loader's `status = 'invalid'` UPDATE turned a lead-disabled bot back on at the next good load); a pairing resolve that joined bots without `status = 'active'` let a disabled bot's token through; a `WITH CHECK` that tied `team_id` to the caller but not `bot_id` to `team_id` let a run name another team's bot → current state belongs in a column the writer sets AND clears in the same transaction as the status (events are alerts/history, never the page's state); every status CASE keeps `disabled` sticky; a credential-resolving function filters on the row's live status; a row that names two parents gets a composite FK, and an INSERT policy admits only the initial state (`status = 'running'`, `ended_at IS NULL`). Also: an editor that silently drops an edit the schema refuses (cleared Name) cannot be retyped → keep the draft, show the issue, disable Save.
- [P5-10 review / child env] The Hermes child was spawned with `env: process.env`, so the agent's shell tool (`env`, `/proc/self/environ`) could read the KMS key, the database URLs with their passwords, the LiteLLM master key and the test auth token — the one invariant the phase exists to keep ("a run's authority is its profile") broken by one innocent-looking option. → a child process gets an **allowlisted** environment (`childEnv`), never the parent's; add a test that puts secret-looking variables in the parent and asserts none reaches the child. The same review: a timeout that signals only the child leaves the processes it started (spawn `detached`, signal `-pid`); a profile directory holding tokens is 0700/0600 and removed on *every* exit path including `spawn()` throwing, plus a startup sweep for a server that died mid-run; a child's `stdin` needs an `'error'` listener or EPIPE takes the server down.
- [P5-10 review / un-awaited emit] `ctx.audit.emit(tx, …)` returns a promise and five call sites in `gateway-llm` dropped it, so the audit line could land after the transaction it described had committed, or fail unobserved. → `await` it; `@typescript-eslint/no-floating-promises` now runs on every `packages/**/src` file (type-aware, ~10 s, zero violations after the fix), so the next one fails lint instead of review.
- [P5-10 review / URLs are logged] The run's MCP token was a path segment (`/api/mcp/runs/<token>`), and a URL is written by every proxy and access log; `redactUrl` knew only `~mta.`. → a credential travels in a header (`Authorization: Bearer`) and the URL names the resource (`/api/mcp/runs/<runId>`, which must agree with the token's run); `redactUrl` masks anything but a uuid in that position as a backstop. And a **public** mint must not let the caller choose who the run is "for": the pairing-token holder named `askerActorId` and `trigger`, i.e. read as any teammate and unlock `person:*`. Authority a caller does not hold is never a request field.
- [P5-06 review / the proxy is not its docs] Written from documentation, the LiteLLM client assumed `POST /model/new` of an existing name fails ("already exists") and falls back to an update; the real proxy answers **200 and adds a second deployment**, deletes take `{ id }` (and answer 400, not 404, for an unknown one), `/config/update` of `router_settings.fallbacks` is refused when `litellm.yaml` itself names `fallbacks` (the shipped file did — every push would have failed), `/key/generate` of an existing key is 200, and `models: []` on a key means *every* model. A fake that mimics the docs passes the same wrong tests. → run the real thing once (`litellm.integration.test.ts`, env-gated), write what it did into the client's header and the fake, and give every object you create in an external system an id of your own so a repeated push is an update by id.
- [P5-10 review / scope of a credential] Each bot's gateway key was minted with *all* workspace aliases, once, at load: a bot could call aliases its definition never named, a key minted before any alias was bound would have `models: []` (= everything), and an edit to the BOT.md or the alias set never reached the key. → derive the grant from the definition (`bots.model_aliases`) ∩ what is bound, remember what the proxy was told (`llm_keys.models`), re-sync on every config push, and refuse to mint with nothing to grant. A preset's egress rule applies to the **whole fallback chain**, not the first alias.
- [P5-10 review / redaction must be used] The gateway's pre-call hook returned redacted text and the router ignored it, sending the raw prompt; the private-key rule redacted the header line and left the key. A check whose output nobody consumes is a log line: use the decision's `text`, and test through the whole path (the stand-in process records the prompt it received). Detectors that find a *start* (a PEM header) need a locator for the end, built so a hostile text of repeated headers stays linear (`indexOf` with a remembered result).
- [P5-10 review / a runtime that cannot run] Model checks (alias, `MANYTHREADS_LITELLM_URL`) sat in the router in front of provider dispatch, so every `rules` bot — which has no model — failed before it started; and `ruleInput` carried keys the strict `messages.post` schema refuses. Nothing ran a rules bot end to end. → checks belong to the provider that needs them (`usesGateway`), and every provider has a DB-backed end-to-end test, not only the one that was built first.
- [P5-10 review / `max(seq)+1`] `run_source_log_append` read the maximum and inserted the next position with no lock, so a model's parallel tool calls (one transaction each) collided on the primary key. → serialise per parent with `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(parent::text))` before the read, and test with `Promise.all` over separate transactions — a sequential test cannot see this.
- [P5-10 review / dedupe swallows] `dedupeKey` joins an enqueue to a job that is *ready or running*; an admin edit made while the apply-config job was mid-push was dropped because the running job had already read its rows. → a job that dedupes on a running twin fingerprints its inputs before and after and queues one follow-up under its own key when they moved; a sync keyed per input version (`mint:<bot>:<definition sha>`) never collides with a different version.
- [P5-10 review / a test env var vs an allowlist] After the child environment became an allowlist, the run test's stand-in lost its `FAKE_HERMES_SPOOL` variable and recorded nothing — green assertions above it, "no process started" below. → name test passthrough variables inside the allowlist (`HERMES_FAKE_SPOOL`), and when a stand-in goes quiet after a hardening change suspect what the hardening removed. Also: preflight with an alias outside the closed set threw a ZodError from inside the guard-event write (the test used `default`) — validate at the door and say why.
- [P5-10 review 2 / sweep by age alone] `sweepStaleProfiles` removed every directory older than two hours in `MANYTHREADS_RUNTIME_DIR` — a path an operator sets, which can be `/var/lib` or a shared volume. Age was the only test. → a janitor that deletes by itself proves **ownership**, not age: a name only this code produces *and* a marker file it writes first (and the directory it is pointed at is never assumed to be its own). Same review: "is anything left to signal" was asked of the leader's `exitCode`, so a leader that exited normally left its group unkilled — ask the group (`kill(-pid, 0)`, ESRCH = empty) and latch once it is gone so a recycled pid is never signalled; remove what the group could still read (the profile) only after it is gone. A column added with a default and no backfill (`model_aliases`) is a data migration that SQL cannot do when the truth lives in git — ship the job that re-reads the file in the same change, not "on the next commit". Two jobs that both read "no row" then insert is a mint race: take a per-key `pg_advisory_xact_lock` first and test with `Promise.all` over separate transactions. A pattern-only detector (`sk-…{16,}`) needs a second test on the match (a digit, or an unbroken run) or it redacts prose.
- [P5-20] A list joined `app.actors` for the requester's name; actors are readable only by themselves (`actors_select`), so the inner join silently dropped every row asked by anybody else and the inbox looked empty to everyone but the requester → names that a team-readable list shows are copied onto the row by the definer door that writes it (`requester_name`, `decider_name`); never join `actors` in a list, and test a list as a person who is *not* the row's author.
- [P5-20] Re-running an approved call failed because the capability handlers (`pages.write`, `repo.write`) re-ask the broker and refuse a grant that still says `needsApproval`; waiving it in the caller does nothing → the broker has to know the call was approved: `ctx.runs.asApproved(run, capability, fn)` puts `approvedCapability` on the in-memory actor and the broker waives the flag for exactly that capability (never a GUC, which SQL could set).
- [P5-20] A plugin can enqueue only onto its own queues (`ownQueue`), so the approvals plugin cannot re-queue `runtime-hermes.run` → a hand-back: approving emits `approvals.approval.decided`, the plugin that asked claims its stored payload (`ApprovalProvider.claim`, once) and enqueues its own job.
- [P5-20] `app.is_system()` is always true inside a SECURITY DEFINER function owned by `manythreads_system`, so "is the caller the system?" there must read `session_user` (the login role, not a setting) → `app.approvals_request` accepts `session_user = 'manythreads_system'` or the run's own bot (`app.actor()` + `app.run_id()`).
- [P5-20] `claim` ran an `UPDATE` as the request role and got `permission denied` (42501), not "no row": a table the role has no privilege on is an error, not a filtered result → check `tx.actor.kind === 'system'` before the statement when the contract says "anything else gets null".
- [P5-20 env] `pnpm install --offline` on the Mac cannot add a new workspace package (no metadata mirror) → copy a sibling plugin's `node_modules` (relative symlinks) and its `importers:` block in `pnpm-lock.yaml`; the devbox's `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` then accepts it.
- [P5-20] The first version of the e2e arranged approval rows as the owner without the denormalised name, so the screen said "asked by  ·" → test fixtures that insert straight into a table must fill every column the real door fills; assert the visible text, not only the row.
- [P5-20 review] The dedupe key of an approval was per (bot, key): a lead asking for the identical call as a member got the member's row — requester the member — and could approve it, so the call ran with the member's authority → a dedupe is per *asker* too (`bot_id, requester_id, dedupe_key`), and the conflict fallback looks up by requester; test it with two askers, not one asker twice.
- [P5-20 review] "Claim, then call, then settle in one job transaction" is not at-most-once: the call commits elsewhere, a later throw rolls the claim back, and the retry (5 attempts by default; the reaper requeues a dead worker's job whatever the queue's limit is) repeats an email → claim in a committed transaction of its own (a first job that queues the second), `maxAttempts: 1`, refuse any attempt after the first, and sweep what is left `running` to `failed`.
- [P5-20 review] A team-readable policy leaked the content of private channels and DMs through the summary, the preview and the DLP excerpt of an approval → a row made of a conversation is read with the conversation: `channel_id IS NULL OR channel_id = ANY (visible_channel_ids('read'))` in the policy, and the decide rule applies the same set (an approver who cannot read it cannot decide it; an invisible id answers `not_found` like a missing one).
- [P5-20 review] A per-row `app.approvals_block_reason(id)` in a list's SELECT list repeated the caller's whole identity lookup for every row → compute the caller's facts once and return reasons for an array of ids (`approvals_block_reasons`).
- [P5-20 review] `hermes.test.ts` "process group" tests hung for 60 s whenever other files ran beside them, and passed alone; a longer timeout did nothing → the provider created the stdout reader lazily, when the job first asked for an event; a process that had already exited had its stdout destroyed, and a `readline` attached to a destroyed stream never ends. Attach the reader (and take its async iterator) at spawn. A hang that a bigger budget does not cure is a race in the code, not a slow box.
- [P5-21 design] `hook.pre_egress` was a registry nobody could run: plugins could register a hook but there was no call that ran the chain, and the hook type (return a replacement input, or throw to block) had no way to say `require_approval` → a hook point needs both halves (`preEgress` registers, `runPreEgress` runs) and a verdict type with the four outcomes of SPEC §15; a chain that finds no hook answers `hooks: 0`, and a caller that must be guarded (the LLM gateway) refuses on that instead of waving the call through. A hook that throws blocks (fail closed).
- [P5-21] The first `runPreEgress` threw its "needs `hook.pre_egress` in `extends`" error synchronously, so `await expect(ctx.hooks.runPreEgress(...)).rejects.toThrow()` failed with the throw itself → a method that returns a promise is `async` all the way, including its guard clauses.
- [P5-11 regex] "Reject nested quantifiers" is not enough to make a user-supplied pattern safe with `new RegExp`: `\w*\w*\w*\w*x` has no nested quantifier and is still cubic on a long run of word characters; the owner's rule (linear-time text processing) can only be kept by not using the backtracking engine → gate patterns are parsed and run by a Thompson-style simulation in `shared/src/bot-md/gate.ts` (cost = characters × instructions), with a differential test against `RegExp` for every supported construct, size limits at BOT.md load, and hostile-input timing tests. The nested-repetition refusal stays as a portability lint, not as the defence.
- [P5-11 resume] A gate hold reused the DLP hold's `approvalId` on resume, which would have put the re-queued run in `approved` DLP mode — the person approved "this message may reach the model", not "let the secret in it through as written" → a separate field (`gateApprovalId`) and a marker in the stored hand-back (`gateHold`); the gate is skipped for either, the DLP check only for its own.
- [P5-11 classifier] The classifier call cannot use the bot's own virtual key: the key's model list is the bot's aliases, and `local` is usually not one of them → a platform call about a bot's message goes through `provider.llm.complete` (pre-call check, master key, ledger line), and says why it has no answer (`refused`, `unbound`, `unavailable`) instead of throwing, because the gate decides what "no answer" means (hold) and the caller must not guess.
- [P5-11] A `fail closed` gate that held every message while the classifier was down would also hold messages for a bot that had run out of budget — the run's own call fails on the same wall → a budget/rate refusal of the classifier stops the run without filing an approval; every other "cannot check" is a hold.
- [P5-21 env] `pnpm install --prefer-offline` on the Mac (registry reachable) adds a new workspace package's `importers:` block to `pnpm-lock.yaml` and links it; `--offline` fails with `ERR_PNPM_NO_OFFLINE_META` for any new package, as the P5-20 note says — do not copy `node_modules` by hand.
- [P5-21 env] A usage text kept in a JS template literal broke the CLI's parse (`Parsing error: ',' expected`) when a flag's description contained backticks → no backticks inside the `USAGE` template of `seed-cli.ts`. And BSD `sed -i` on the Mac needs `-i ''` and does not take GNU's one-line `a\` text: edit with a script (python) or the Edit tool.
- [P5-11 review] A size cap checked while *emitting* instructions is no cap when the body emits none: `(((((){100}){100}){100}){100}){100}` (37 characters) made the compiler run 100^5 empty copies for 38 seconds, inside a Zod `superRefine` that runs on every BOT.md parse, so one lead's commit could wedge the loader → compute the expanded size **before** expanding (saturating multiply, every node counts at least one, even an empty group), refuse a repeat of something that can only match nothing, and time hostile *compile* inputs, not only hostile match inputs.
- [P5-11 review] Stage one compared the message as typed: a zero-width space inside a word, full-width or Cyrillic look-alike letters, an Arabic tatweel or harakat all walked past a phrase list → read two spellings of the message (NFKD, lower-case, marks and tatweel stripped, look-alikes folded; format characters removed in one, turned into a space in the other) and compare the phrase in both; patterns run on the raw text as well. State the scope (leetspeak, spaced-out letters, translations are the classifier's) and test every evasion in the list.
- [P5-11 review] "The classifier runs on `local`" was checked as "`local` is bound", but an admin can bind `local` to a hosted provider (and its fallbacks can be hosted), and `air-gapped` judged the alias *name* → a guarantee about where bytes go is checked on the host of the alias and every fallback link (`localOnly`), never on a name.
- [P5-11 review] A class matched by `items.some(...)` makes the cost (characters × instructions × class size); bounding instructions alone left a hundred-item class × 200 instructions × 40,000 characters → precompile classes to merged ranges and bisect.
- [P5-11 review] A hook point's answer said how many hooks ran (`hooks > 0`) and a caller trusted that for "the message was redacted"; any other guard registered there would have satisfied it → the answer names the plugins that ran (`ran`), and a caller that relies on one checks for it.
- [P5-11 review] The gate was keyed to `usesGateway`, which says "talks to a model *through the LLM gateway*": a subscription runtime would have bypassed the gate by not using the gateway → its own provider flag, `gated`, set by every runtime that sends the message to a model.
- [P5-11 review] A stopped run with no approval card (budget, nobody able to decide) left the asker looking at a bot that never answers → say it in the thread, in words that name no gate entry, figure or message text.
- [P5-11 re-review] "Local" was inferred from a loopback host name, so on k3s (an in-cluster Service or a LAN address is where a self-hosted model really is) every gated phrase message was held as `classifier_unavailable` and `air-gapped` refused the real `local` alias — a guarantee made correct for the dev box and wrong for the deployment → where the platform cannot see the network, the admin says it (`llm_providers.local`, "Runs inside this deployment — never egress"), loopback stays inferred, nothing is guessed from `.svc`/`.cluster.local`/private ranges, and `EgressTarget.local` is the one answer that strict-egress, air-gapped and the classifier all read.
- [P5-11 re-review] A general `complete(…, { localOnly, followUp })` made the safety properties of the gate's call caller-supplied flags: any future caller could leave `localOnly` off or set `followUp` to dodge a rate limit → the surface is one purpose-built call (`classify`) with the alias, the local-only rule and the rate-limit accounting fixed inside (`followUp` derived from `piece`), and an optional `gated?` on a provider type became a required `gated: boolean` so forgetting it is a type error.
- [P5-11 re-review] Stripping `\p{Cf}` is not "removing what draws nothing": Hangul fillers, the Braille blank and C0/C1 controls got through, and two kinds of invisible character in one phrase (inside a word AND where the space was) beat both spellings → widen the removal set, and also search the message's words joined as one run for the phrase's words joined, aligned to word boundaries (accepting that `droptable` is held); scripts written without spaces are matched as substrings, and spacing combining marks (`\p{Mc}`) belong to the word.
- [P5-11 re-review] Defanging only `<…message` left `< /message>`, full-width and guillemet spellings → escape every angle bracket and its look-alikes inside the wrapped message; a wrapper keeps a message in its place, it does not stop a crafted message from steering the model (say so).
- [P5 e2e flakes] Three consecutive full `pnpm e2e` runs on the devbox failed on different visual and spec cases, each green alone; a failure "at 0 ms" in `dm`, `signin`, `composer` was the `beforeAll` that starts the file's stack, not the screen. Four causes, found by reading the error text of each run (`--reporter=line`, `sed` away the escapes, grep the `1)` blocks) and the Postgres log (`docker logs compose-postgres-1 | grep -E "remaining connection slots|too many clients"`), never by rerunning until green:
  (1) **Connection slots.** Every server holds an app pool of 10 and a system pool of 10; four per-spec stacks next to the three shared servers peaked at 98 of 100 connections (sampled with `pg_stat_activity` every 0.5 s) and the 98th client got "remaining connection slots are reserved" = a 500 on `GET /api/session` in a `beforeAll` (`MANYTHREADS_APP_POOL_MAX` / `MANYTHREADS_SYSTEM_POOL_MAX`, 5 each for every e2e server in `e2e/support/pools.ts`: peak 63, zero refusals in 6 runs). Count connections before adding a worker or a server to a run that shares one Postgres.
  (2) **`beforeAll` runs under the 30 s TEST timeout**, not under `startStack`'s own 120 s: a stack start passed 30 s under load ("beforeAll hook timeout of 30000ms exceeded", then the first test of the file is the one reported, at 0 ms or 30.0 s). `startStack` now raises the hook budget itself (`test.setTimeout`, works inside a hook; proven with a 35 s sleep after the start). Any helper with its own long budget must raise the Playwright budget it runs under.
  (3) **Scroll events are measured against the layout at dispatch, not at the scroll.** `VirtualList` pinned itself to the bottom, then the composer grew again in the same frame; the scroll event the pin produced saw the smaller viewport as "reader scrolled up" (`stuck=false`, "Jump to latest", 13% pixel diff in `composer-390`). Reproduced deterministically (grow, pin, grow in the next task) in `e2e/channels/list-pin.spec.ts`; the list now ignores a scroll event that lands exactly where it pinned. A document `keydown` listener registered in a passive `useEffect` can act on the previous render's state (Threads inbox `e` removed the neighbour of the thread the DOM showed selected): register it in `useLayoutEffect`.
  (4) **A frame-time gate measures the machine** (`perf-5000` p95 33 ms beside four other browsers, 18 ms alone) → specs named `perf-*` run in the `perf` project, which depends on the others and so runs alone. And an api spec that asserts "nothing unread" on the SHARED server (`read-state`) fails when any desktop spec posts in a channel that person belongs to → the file takes `useIsolatedStack()`.
  vitest: `afterAll(() => s.close())` timed out at the 10 s default under the full suite because `DROP DATABASE` forces an immediate checkpoint and Postgres runs them one at a time (1,400 forced checkpoints in 3 h on the devbox, up to 14 s each, as every test file drops a database): slow legitimately, so `hookTimeout: 120_000` in every project of `vitest.config.ts`, the budget of the `beforeAll` that starts the same server.
- [P5-12 compose] The Hindsight image has `python3` and no `curl`/`wget`, so a `curl` healthcheck left the container "unhealthy" while the API was up → healthcheck with `python3 -c "import urllib.request; …"`; check what an image ships (`docker run --entrypoint sh … -c 'which curl'`) before writing its healthcheck.
- [P5-12 memory] Assuming "team A gets nothing from B's bank" means an empty recall → the real engine always ranks the bank it was asked about and returns that bank's nearest facts at a near-zero score; assert that none of the OTHER bank's content is in the answer (the fake returns nothing on zero overlap, which hides this). Also: recall on a bank never created is a 404, not an empty list — create the bank first.
- [P5-12 env] A new workspace package (plugin or tool) is not linked until `pnpm install` runs on the devbox (and the lockfile comes back with `scp devbox:/root/manythreads/pnpm-lock.yaml .`); on the Mac, typecheck of a package without `node_modules` fails — copy a sibling plugin's `node_modules` symlinks (gitignored) rather than install.
- [P5-12 review] Retention closed only the `person` scope: a `channel` source line carries no public/private distinction (a private-channel or DM message logs as `channel`), so a run that read one could retain it into the team bank → decide "team-visible" from the data at retain time (public channel of the run's own team, resolved by id), treat everything unresolved as private, and refuse conversation / private-thread runs outright; a scope label is a claim, not a proof.
- [P5-12 review] A dev engine connected as the Postgres superuser with the LiteLLM master key, and a bank name that ignored the workspace → every new backing service gets its own least-privilege role/key from day one (own database owner, CONNECT revoked elsewhere, a virtual key limited to the alias it needs) and any name derived from a slug includes the scope that makes the slug unique.
- [P5-12 re-review] A safety rule that reads the run's own history (what it read) was bypassed by a path that re-runs the call in a new run without that history (approval resume) → a capability whose rule depends on run context cannot be approval-gated; refuse to file it and refuse to resume it, and anchor the rule on facts the new run still carries (its channel) as well.
- [P5-13 feed] The first cut queued the drain under ONE dedupe key (`feed:drain`), so a change that arrived while a drain was running joined that job, which had already read its list: the change waited for the minute cron and a test timed out at 30 s, passing alone → a "run after this" job needs a key per time bucket (a running job holds a bucket that has passed, so the next change gets a job of its own), and a follow-up for the earliest `not_before` under its own key per second; never a single key for "something changed".
