- [P5-13 feed] An outbox handler runs in the ONE transaction the consumer shares with every other plugin's handler for that event: an engine call (or a throw) there holds up or rolls back notifications and everything beside it → handlers only mark a work row and queue a job; the job talks to the engine, and its failures back off in the row. Row locks across an engine call would make the handler's next upsert wait for the engine, so the drain is single-flight by `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock`, and a settle deletes only `requested_at = <the value it read>` (compare the `::text` of the timestamp: a JS `Date` has milliseconds, the column microseconds).
- [P5-13 design] 0001's check in memory-hindsight only looked at lines labelled `channel`, so a private or DM message logged as `team` passed the retain rule → a message line is resolved to its channel whatever label it carries; a scope label is a claim, not a proof (the same lesson as P5-12, one level down).
- [P5-13 design] "What did the run read" is not enough for the text a bot posted without being asked to retain it: a reply in a public channel is team-visible where it stands, but the run that wrote it may have read a person source, and the bank would outlive and out-reach that → the bot's runs that were open at that instant decide (a run still open defers: its source log can still grow); a test with one bot for every scenario had earlier runs' two-second windows decide the "first refusal" → one bot per scenario and fixed historic instants.
- [P5-13 test] An assertion on the retain log right after the engine showed the fact was a race: the engine call is inside the drain's transaction and the log line commits after it → wait for the work row to be gone (`idle()`; it is deleted in the same transaction as the line) or poll the line; the engine fake alone says nothing about the log.
- [P5-13 test] Fixtures: `bots.definition_sha` must be hex (`repeat('m', 64)` fails `bots_definition_sha`), `files.folder_path` must end in `/` and be at least two characters, and a `w.system(...)` opened inside another `w.system(...)` cannot see the outer transaction's uncommitted rows (a grant for a bot inserted in the outer one fails its foreign key) → one transaction. BSD `sed -i` on the Mac needs `-i ''` (a multi-file edit without it fails with "invalid command code").
- [P5-13 review / denormalised copies] The thread document's head read `app.threads.title`, which is a copy of the root's first 120 characters made when it was posted: with the root withheld (a bot's message after a person-source read) and a person's reply, the head leaked the withheld text into a document that did not quote it → build a document only from the values it quotes; never from a stored copy of something that was decided separately (a title, a preview, a summary column). Test the exact shape: withheld root + retained reply, and assert the text appears in no engine call.
- [P5-13 review / a re-run forgets the history] Approving re-runs the call in a NEW run whose source log is empty, so a page written after a person-source read looked like the work of a run that read nothing → whatever a rule reads from a run's history must travel with the re-run (the approvals job now copies the stopped run's lines at open); the same lesson as `memory.retain` never being approval-gated, one door over.
- [P5-13 review / the newest line is not the state] "What the bank holds for a source" was the newest log line of any outcome, so a `not_configured` line (written while the engine was off) or a `failed` line (a forget that exhausted its attempts, settled and dropped) hid a document that was still in the bank, and nothing ever took it out → state is the newest of retained / forgotten / withheld only; a forget is never given up on (the row stays, a half hour apart); while the engine is off a change to a source the bank holds a document for is still queued. Attempt notes and state must be different kinds of line.
- [P5-13 review / the specific event is not the substrate event] `pages.page.written` comes only from `pages.write`; the Files screen saves through `POST repo/commit`, so a person's edit (removing a pasted secret) never replaced the old document and new pages never arrived → subscribe to the event every path emits (`repo.repo.committed`) and filter by path, not to the one a single route happens to add.
- [P5-13 e2e] `secret-readback.spec.ts` failed once in the full run and passed alone: the scanner refuses any key matching `hash|secret|token|…` and `MemoryRetainLogEntry.contentHash` appeared as soon as another spec's messages put a line in the log (an empty list has no keys) → an API shape carries no field named like a credential, whatever it holds (the hash is a column, not a field of the shape); and a scanner-driven spec depends on what other specs left behind, so a new list route needs a case with data in it.
- [P5-13 test] A run fixture took `ended_at` from the test process's `new Date()` and `started_at` from the database's `now()`: the process's clock reading was earlier than the transaction's, so `bot_runs_ended_after_started` fired intermittently (only under the full gate's timing) → derive both instants from one statement's `now()` (or pass both explicitly); never mix a client clock with a server clock in one row.
- [P5-13 re-review / a cap on a copy is a hole] The re-run's inherited source log was copied with `ORDER BY seq LIMIT 500`, row by row: a model that searched a dozen times has more lines than that, and a person line at 501 read as a clean run → a copy that a rule depends on is one set-based statement with no cap (`ctx.runs.inheritSources`), and when it cannot be made the call is not made; truncating and carrying on is the unsafe default.
- [P5-13 re-review / the column side of a comparison] `m.id::text = l.source_ref` read every message for every source line, on every recall, retain, summary and authored verdict; the first fix (a join with the regex and the cast inside the join clause) was still planned as "the channel's messages, then filter" — measured, not guessed: the planner had a hash join over 30,000 rows and, with joins forced to nested loops, a materialised scan of messages → the ref becomes a uuid in a subquery of its own and the join clause is `m.id = s.ref_uuid`; a plan test builds a table big enough, forces nested loops, EXPLAINs the real function and carries a negative control (the cast column) so it cannot pass vacuously.
- [P5-13 re-review / an informational line must not change state] A thread's exclusion block wrote `withheld` for a message whose own forget was failing; "newest of retained/forgotten/withheld" then said nothing was held and the document stayed for ever → state is the newest of retained/forgotten only (`withheld` is read for "the same refusal twice", and only when it is newer than the state), and the exclusion line is not written for a message the bank holds.
- [P5-13 re-review / a window is a promise about a duration] "A run that never closed covers two hours" assumed a run lasts two hours; `timeoutSeconds` has no maximum, so a reply at hour three of a run that read a person source was "outside any run" → an open run covers from its start until it ends, bounded only by the day the lookup reaches back (and the day a deferred source is given up).
- [P5-13 re-review / a hash of text is the text, to anyone who can guess] The document id and the log carried the first 16 hex of an unkeyed sha-256 of what they held, and a `forgotten` line is a deleted text → a keyed digest under a per-workspace random key that no route returns; the API shape has no hash field, and a bot's own retain is shown by its run, not by the derived id.
- [P5-13 re-review / a migration that runs as nobody] The legacy-row UPDATEs of approvals 0002 ran as the migrating role under FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY and the system-only UPDATE policy: zero rows, no error → `SET ROLE manythreads_system … RESET ROLE` (kernel 0007's pattern) and a test that seeds legacy rows and runs the block.
- [P5-13 re-review / the specific event, again] The cap of 200 paths and the "deletes only" filter were both ways of not hearing what a commit said; a catch-up of the index (git ahead of it) emitted nothing at all → every `pages/` path of every `repo.repo.committed`, set-based, and the index catch-up emits one event for its range; a commit time from a git author date is not the database's clock, so a page is judged at both its author date and `indexed_at`.
- [P5-13 final round / a process cache outlives a transaction] The workspace key was cached in the module the first time a drain read it, including a key that drain had just inserted: if the drain's transaction rolled back, this process kept a key the database never had, and two replicas named the same text differently → anything created inside a transaction is not cached past it; read it again in the next one (a per-drain map is enough).
- [P5-13 final round / a stale state with no owner] A run whose worker died stayed `running` for ever; every rule that waits for runs to end (the feed's deferral) waited with it and then withheld a day of a bot's output → whatever waits for an external state needs a janitor for the state it waits on (the bots plugin's hourly reaper), not only a give-up on the waiting side.
- [P5-13 final round / a default that stamps history] `ADD COLUMN indexed_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()` gave every existing commit the instant of the migration, so legacy bot pages would have been judged against whatever runs were in flight at the deploy → add the column nullable, set the default afterwards, and read `coalesce(new, old)`.
- [P5-13 final round / FK actions erase evidence] `approvals.run_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so an approval whose run was deleted looked like one that never had a run, and the re-run's inherited log was silently empty → record that a thing had a parent (`had_run`, by trigger) before the foreign key can forget it, and fail closed on the difference. Also: a fixture that deletes a row through a table with no DELETE policy deletes nothing, silently.
- [P5-13 final round / a catch-up is not a commit] The index catch-up emitted a `repo.repo.committed` that the bots loader would have applied, for a `bots/` path that reached git without the writer's lead/admin rule → a re-synthesised event names only what the writer would have let through (guarded paths out), in bounded chunks.
- [P5-13 final round / a precondition is a state, not a hope] `inheritSources` copied from a run that might still be logging; the approvals flow only worked because the run had usually stopped by then → the door states its preconditions (source ended, target live, same bot) and the caller waits for the state (a second job, ten seconds at a time) instead of failing or copying a prefix.
- [P5-13 delta review / waiting is not an answer for a run nobody owns] The re-run waited five minutes for the run that asked and then failed the call: an external client's MCP run (minted with a pairing token; `/close` is the client's option) is never closed by anything, so every approved call from one would have failed where it ran at once before → "still open" is never a reason to fail: end the run (its tokens revoked, so its log is final) — at once when no worker can hold it (no channel, no thread), after the wait when a router worker may — then inherit and go on. Test with the origin left open.
- [P5-13 delta review / closing is only real if every door agrees] A run's tokens were revoked on close, but a worker holding the in-process handle could still `asBot`, `authorize` and write to its source log; the reaper, the approval's re-run and a client's `/close` all assumed a closed run takes nothing more → every door that acts for a run reads its status first (RunEndedError / a denied decision), and the test calls each door on an ended run.
- [P5-13 delta review / a backfill that updates every row meets every constraint] 0003's `UPDATE app.approvals SET had_run = true` checked 0002's NOT VALID constraint on each row it touched — a final legacy `memory.retain` row failed the migration; found by seeding exactly that row in the test, not by reading → a backfill names what it touches, and the test seeds the awkward rows (the NULL FK, the row a constraint would refuse).
- [P5-13 delta review / state created in a transaction that may roll back] The digest key was made by the drain; the fix is not a better cache but making it elsewhere: the handler that marks a source makes the key in the transaction that commits the work row, and the drain only reads. An idempotent insert-if-absent in a transaction of its own (or one that commits with its reason for existing) is the only way to create something an external system will be told about.
- [P5-13 delta review / least privilege for a janitor] The reaper needed `ctx.runs.close` and got `tool.invoke` — open a run, act as any bot — because that was the only extension that exposed `ctx.runs` → a narrower door (`run.close`: `close` works, every other member throws naming `tool.invoke`), tested by calling each member.
- [P5-13 delta review / a NULL that means two things] `run_timeout_seconds` NULL meant both "the definition names no limit" and "loaded before the column": a daily job could not tell which to re-read, and the reaper used the default for both → a `…_known` flag beside the value, set by the loader and by the backfill.
- [P5-13 last delta / a flat timer for a state with its own clock] The approved call waited a flat five minutes for the run that asked while the run's budget was ten: a slow model's reply was turned into a refusal by the approval that was meant to unblock it → wait until the thing waited for is past its own deadline (the token's expiry), plus a grace, and make the sweep that rescues stuck rows aware of the same wait.
- [P5-13 last delta / an exception class that is only for the kernel] `RunEndedError` was thrown by the run service and caught by nobody outside it, so a run ended mid-request became a 500 for the client → export the error from the SDK and map it where each caller already has a vocabulary for it (a tool answer, a 401, `{ refused }`).
- [P5-13 last delta / a per-item loop with no per-item fence] A backfill that processed rows in one pass with one transaction let one unreadable row fail or starve the rest, every day → a savepoint per row, and an order that does not always start with the same unreadable ones.
- [P5-14 design / retention deletes the evidence] The feed's verdict about what a bot wrote is read from the bot's runs by time window, so pruning runs at 90 days turns "withheld: its run read a person source" into "written outside a run": a person's reply 91 days later would have put the withheld root into a thread document. → whatever a decision reads from a log that another job deletes needs a durable memo written BEFORE the delete, in the same transaction (`app.memory_memoize_runs`), and the reader takes live rows and memos as one set with the same window rules; a control test deletes the run without a memo and shows the verdict flips, so the pass cannot be vacuous.
- [P5-14 design / SET NULL is "no run"] `approvals.run_id` is SET NULL when the run goes, so a pruned run's approval read as "a run that read nothing" and would have been retained → the memo carries `approval_ids`, a run-less approval with `had_run` takes its verdict from it, and one with no memo is withheld (`run_gone`). "Absent" must never read as "clean".
- [P5-14 design / no delete policy] `bot_runs` and `run_source_log` had RLS forced and no DELETE policy, so no role could delete a run (a bot's removal only worked because FK cascades bypass RLS) and `memory_retain_log` is append-only for the system role → a destructive job needs its own explicit door: a DELETE policy for the system role on the run tables (retention's migration), and for the append-only log one SECURITY DEFINER function owned by the migrating role with SELECT/DELETE policies `TO manythreads_owner`; check `REVOKE`s and missing policies before writing the delete.
- [P5-14 design / a first-use write touches every repo] Writing `memory/index.md` and `log.md` on any team's first commit would have changed the commit sequence of every repo-git, files and e2e test (history counts, seeded teams, Files-tree baselines) and the live repos at deploy → the mirror is inert without an engine and writes only once a `memory/` path is committed; a plugin that writes into shared repositories must not do it on a deployment that did not ask for its feature.
- [P5-14 test] `memory/index.md` carries `okf_version` and no `type`, so `parseConcept` (rightly) refuses it as a concept; the test used the concept parser on the bundle root → use `splitConcept` for the root, `parseConcept` only for concepts. Also: the repo blob route answers from git, which has the commit a moment before the index row commits → wait for the `repo_commits` row before asserting on authorship or counts.
- [P5-14 test] A "linear time" ceiling of 500 ms failed at 516 ms while three workers shared the box → ceilings in unit tests that run beside DB suites are loose (1.5 s); the strict budget belongs to a dedicated hostile-input list (P5-24).
- [P5-14 types] `let refused: string | null = null` assigned inside a `visit` callback is narrowed to `null` by TypeScript at the use site (the later `if (refused !== null)` has type `never`) → hold such a result in an object (`const found = { reason: null as string | null }`).
- [P5-14 review / a delete is an UPDATE elsewhere] Deleting a run fires `approvals.run_id` SET NULL, which is an UPDATE of the approval row, and a NOT VALID CHECK is re-validated on UPDATE: one legacy `memory.retain` approval row would have raised 23514 and killed the whole 500-run step every night → a destructive job lists what its delete will UPDATE through foreign keys (and what their constraints say) before it ships, excludes what cannot take it, and falls back to run-by-run deletes in savepoints so one refusal cannot stall a chain; test with a seeded legacy row and with a deliberately poisoned one.
- [P5-14 review / the payload of a job is the request role's] `app.enqueue_job` is callable by the request role, so a destructive job that took its cutoff from its payload would delete everything under mode `on` for anyone who could enqueue → a job re-derives every authority-bearing value from the database (the workspace's own window, the bound's minimum) and re-asserts the whole eligibility predicate inside the DELETE; test a forged payload.
- [P5-14 review / "409" is two things] The writer answers 409 both for a file that exists (`conflicts` names it) and for a lost race for the branch (`ref_conflict`, no files, nothing written); the mirror treated every 409 as "the file exists", recorded the team with no root and told a journal that was never written it existed → read a refusal by what it carries, confirm with a look at the state before concluding, and retry the race.
- [P5-14 review / a column that outlives its first reader] The journal queries read `memory_retain_log` without `store = 'team_bank'`: harmless until P5-26 writes `person_file` lines, then a team repo file would count (and link) a person's memory → a query over a table with a discriminator filters it from day one, with a test that plants the other value.
- [P5-14 review / a memo that matches the common case is not a bound] `OR held` wrote a memo for every run whose summary was in the bank, i.e. almost every run, so "pruning" moved a run row to a memo row → ask what the reader needs the row for: the only reason (not forgetting a summary) is solved at the reader (`skip('gone')`), so the memo is conditional on what can change a verdict, with a matrix test of every combination.
- [P5-14 review / a dry run must not overwrite a real result] One `last_sweep` column made a preview erase what the last real sweep did, and `updated_at DEFAULT now()` made a row a sweep created look like an admin's change → one column per kind of result; a "changed at" is NULL until somebody changes something.
- [P5-14 delta review / the index heals later than the prune] The memo condition read "did the bot author something in the window" from `repo_commits`, but a bot's commit can be in git and not yet indexed (the reindex is lazy), so after the prune the page's time fell inside a window nothing remembered → a memo condition built on what an index knows now must also cover every run that left a footprint in the feed's own log (its summary or a message of it was withheld), because the feed's log is written when the index is not behind; test with a commit that is indexed after the prune.
- [P5-14 delta review / a fallback that catches everything] The run-by-run fallback caught every error, so a statement timeout or a deadlock would have been reported as "this run cannot be deleted" and skipped for good → a fallback catches the one class of failure it can get past (SQLSTATE class 23, an integrity refusal) and rethrows the rest for the job's own retry; test each class with a fake transaction.
- [P5-14 delta review / count what the statement returned] Counts of lines and tokens were taken from the ids a select proposed, before a delete that re-asserts its predicate and may decline some → counts come from the rows the DELETE RETURNING gave back, weighed before the cascade removes them.
- [P5-14 delta review / an upsert is not a no-op] The settings PUT upserted `updated_at` and `updated_by` on every call, so a request that changed nothing moved the stamp that is meant to name the last change → write only when a value changes, and test that the stamp stays.
- [P5-14 gate / a flat millisecond limit measures the machine] Ten tests failed in the full gate only: hostile-input cases asserting `< 50 ms` read 150 ms beside three busy workers, and 5 s default timeouts hit cases that start servers or hash passwords; all passed alone → a timing test of a "linear-time" claim times the same hostile construction at n and 4n in one process (best of five, large size warmed, a floor under the small time, ratio below ~10 where linear reads 4–8 and quadratic 16) plus a generous absolute ceiling (`expectLinear` in `@manythreads/test-utils`); first cut used ratio 8 and a 5 ms floor and read x8.2 on 7 ms measurements — a ratio of two timings under a few ms is noise, so it is floored. A test that does DB or server setup gets the budget of its `beforeAll` explicitly (`vi.setConfig({ testTimeout })`), never the 5 s default.
- [P5-14 gate / a helper in a package's main entry is loaded by everything that imports the package] The timing helper imported `vitest` and was exported from `@manythreads/test-utils`; the e2e web server and the seed load that entry at runtime, where vitest has no state: `pnpm e2e` could not start its web server while every unit suite was green → anything that needs the test framework lives behind a subpath export (`./blob-contract`, `./linear`), never in the main entry; a unit test walks the entry's imports and refuses `vitest`; and a change to a shared test package is verified by starting an e2e run, not only by the unit gate.
- [P5-16 review / a team-readable list of a private use] The bot page listed the bot's newest runs to the whole team, and `bot_runs_select` (written in P5-03, when every run happened somewhere the team could read) made no difference between a mention in a public channel and a person's private conversation with the bot: asker, thread, status and time of someone's private use leaked to teammates, the lead and the admin. → when a feature adds a *private* kind of row to a table whose policy says "the team reads it", change the policy (and any list that repeats it) in the same task, and add the negative test as a teammate, a lead and an admin with a positive control (they do read a run of another trigger); grep every reader of the table (`FROM app.<table>` outside the system role) before shipping a private variant. The same lesson for events: `events.team_id` is taken from the payload's `teamId`, so a team-scoped event is readable by every member — an event about one person's private act carries no `teamId` (DM events do not).
- [P5-16 review / new shared schemas need their snapshots] Ten new schemas (`Conversation…`, the started event) shipped with no `schema-compat/snapshots/*.json`: `vitest` writes a missing snapshot when it is not in CI and fails with `CI=true`, so the Mac/devbox gates were green and CI would have been red → generate snapshots on the devbox with `pnpm exec vitest run --project schema-compat -u`, copy them back, commit, and re-run under `CI=true` without `-u`; an added field to an existing response schema updates its snapshot too (`BotSummary`, `GetBotResponse`, `ListBotsResponse`).
- [P5-16 review / a control that stays in the bank] The conversation feed test posted its control note with the word `Heron`, which the next test used as the fact whose *disappearance* it waits for: the retained control note kept `factWith('Heron')` true for ever (deterministic timeout) and made "the other channel was untouched" vacuous → a marker word is unique per test, and a test that waits for something to be gone must not share its word with anything an earlier test leaves behind.
- [P5-16 review / a number a cast cannot hold] A cursor pattern of `\d{1,19}` accepted values above 2^63 and the `::bigint` cast raised 22003 (a 500) → bound the digits to what the type holds (16 digits for microseconds) and test the first number past it; "valid shape" is not "valid value".
- [P5-16 review / an offer the router will not honour] The roster showed Talk and `open` made the channel for a bot whose definition had no `conversation` trigger, while the router answers a conversation channel for that trigger alone: the person typed into a channel nothing would answer, and the Triggers tab claimed the opposite → every offer a UI makes ("Talk", "Ask") is gated by the same fact the server acts on (a field on the roster read from `bots.triggers`, a 409 from `open`), and copy about a rule is checked against the code of the rule.
- [P5-16 review / a nap is not a negative] A "nothing happened" assertion waited `sleep(2000)` after the thing that would have woken a run → green on a fast box by luck. → find the event that proves the decision was made (here: the conversation index row is written by the same outbox transaction as the router's job, so once it exists the router has decided) and assert the absence of the row it would have left (the `runtime-hermes.run` job for that bot and message), with a positive control for the same lookup.
- [P5-16 review / mobile layout was never run] The conversation spec said "both layouts" but skipped most flows on `mobile-web` and the empty state test was never executed there: on a 390 px screen with no thread open the message box was not rendered at all (`showMain = !narrow || thread !== null`), so nobody could start a conversation on a phone; the composer's autofocus also scrolled the list under a tap, so a Playwright tap on a tab silently did nothing (URL unchanged; a JS `.click()` worked) → run every flow of a new screen on `mobile-web` before calling it done, and when a tap "does nothing" with the element visible, look for a layout shift (autofocus, a composer growing) before suspecting the handler.
- [P5-18 a payload the platform vouches for must not be a tool argument] The obvious way to carry an Answer's payload (whom it was for, what it rests on) is a field on `messages.post`. That tool is what a bot's model can be granted and call with arguments of its own, so anything on it is the model's claim, however the answer plugin validates its own tool. → a value the platform stamps travels through code a model cannot reach (here the `messages` provider, `provider.messages`, and the message's `meta`), and a test calls the tool with the field to prove it is refused.
- [P5-18 copying a visibility rule] "Is this source readable by the asker?" was first considered (and not built) as a SQL function with the policies of messages, threads, files and runs written out again with an explicit person. Four copies of a rule drift from the original the first time a policy changes (bots 0011 did change `bot_runs_select` a day earlier). → ask the platform's own policies: an INVOKER function that switches the transaction's identity to the run's asker for the call, only for the caller's own live run, and back; and keep it narrow (no argument names a run or a person).
- [P5-18 test: two runs of one bot seconds apart] `app.memory_authored_verdict(bot, at)` reads every run of the bot that was open within two seconds of `at`; a test that ran after another test of the same bot got the earlier run's refusal (`conversation`) instead of its own (`private_source`). → assert the run's own `run_retain_verdict(run)` for the exact reason and only "not null" on the authored verdict, or leave a clear gap between the runs.
- [P5-18 test: a team search never reaches a DM] `messages.search` is filled with the run's team, so a direct message (no team) never entered the source log and the run was "team-visible" when the test needed it private. → arrange a private channel of the team the asker sits in; a DM is only cited, never searched.
- [P5-18 test: a fact that is true once per run] The RLS test for "a team the asker is not on has no page for them" used the run that an earlier test had already given an answer, so `answer_view` said `already` and the test failed with "no view". → a test that depends on a one-per-run state creates its own run.
- [P5-18 review / an audience check must test the readers of the reply, not a scope label] "May the reply's readers see this?" was `scope = 'team'` — a label about the *source* — so a team message cited into a channel a guest (ACL grant) or a person of another team could read was shown to them, with its first 120 characters, page path or file name. → ask who actually reads the place the text goes (`app.channel_audience` of the reply channel: the team, owners/admins, whoever an ACL entry names) and share only what every one of them may open; fail closed when the set cannot be established (the bot cannot read the channel, no readers), and write the negative tests with a guest grant, a grant to a person of another team, a private channel and a direct message, plus a positive control (a grant to the team itself is still the team's).
- [P5-18 review / check-then-act needs a lock] "One answer per run" read the thread for a prior answer and then posted: two parallel `answer.post` calls both saw nothing and both posted (the same shape as `max(seq)+1` in P5-10). → take `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('answer:' || run, 0))` before the read (held to commit, so the second call sees the first's committed message) and back it with a partial unique index so the guarantee does not depend on one caller; test with two calls at the same moment (`Promise.all` over separate transactions, and a stand-in that fires its tool calls in parallel), never one after the other. Also decide what "deleted" means once and use it in every reader (the tool, the index, the runtime): a deleted answer does not count.
- [P5-18 review / default-allow] `audience := private OR scope <> 'person'` let any scope word that was not `person` (`channel`, an unknown one) through for a line of a kind the function cannot resolve. → allow lists, not deny lists: `scope = 'team'` shows, anything else does not.
- [P5-18 review / a capability does not bound what is cited] The bot's allowlist said what it could *call*, not what it could *name*: any id the asker could read had its label copied into the answer, and the kept/removed counts were an existence oracle for ids and page paths. → an id a model names must be one its run read (the run's source log, plus the thread it was asked in), checked before anything is looked up; an id that was not reached is reported identically whether it exists, is hidden or is missing (`not_reached`). The contract of a logged source is a line type and a ref format (`page` = path, or `<teamId>:<path>`): a tool that opens pages writes those lines.
- [P5-18 review / a provider that trusts its meta] The `messages` provider validated the *shape* of `meta` and trusted its content: a plugin could post one run's answer as another's, as another bot's, or with a text that differs from the payload clients draw. → the provider compares the claim with the transaction (`app.run_id()`, the actor's bot, the message's own body) and each refusal has a test; "validated" is not "true".
- [P5-18 review / a weak negative] The smuggle test asserted only "no row has this body"; it passed when the call failed for any reason, including a typo in the scenario. → assert the refusal itself (status and code), and add a positive control that is the same call without the forbidden part and stores exactly one row.
- [P5-18 review / a flaky test that was a real race] `conversation.test.ts` "Channel mentions" saw a reply count of 2 under load: the fixture bot `quiet` had a `channel_message` trigger with no `source`, so the router fired it on every #dev message and it answered in the thread the test counted. → a fixture bot that must stay silent is bound to a channel nothing posts to, and a count that is about one author counts by author.
- [P5-18 review / a marker is not any bracket] Every `[0]`..`[999]` in an answer's text became a superscript (`arr[0]`). → a marker exists only for a number the payload has a place for; everything else is the text it was written as.
- [P5-18 delta review / a roster row is not membership] The reply-reader test said "on the team" with a bare `team_members` row, but kernel 0006 (`lookup_team_role`) counts a person only while they are an active, non-guest workspace member — a roster row survives a demotion to guest, and a guest with an ACL grant reads a channel without being the team. → when a rule re-implements "is a member", read the platform's own definition (`lookup_team_role`) and mirror every condition, and write the negative test that makes the condition matter (demote to guest, keep the roster row, add a person ACL on the channel; the kernel's `workspace_members_guard` refuses that demotion through every normal path, so the test lifts the trigger as the database owner for one statement and restores it). The same review: derive every "who reads this" answer, `asker` included, from the channel's readers, not from a column of the channel; and a test that waits with `setTimeout` is not a concurrency test — latch the first call, poll `pg_locks` for the second's not-granted advisory lock, then release.
- [P5-17 one rule, two callers] "Who will read this channel?" was a CASE inside `answer_reply_reach()`, written for a bot's reply; sharing a copy needs the same answer for a channel the sharer names, and a second copy would have drifted the first time a policy changed (it had already happened twice in P5-18). → the decision is one function of (channel, team, asker) over `app.channel_audience` (`answer_channel_reach`), and the reply's function became a wrapper, so the 29 existing reply-reader tests are the regression suite of the extraction; a new caller of an existing decision extracts it, never copies it.
- [P5-17 a copy is the sharer's message] The shared copy is authored by the person, and the edit route let its author change `body` while the Answer card kept drawing the payload's `meta.answer.body`: a sharer could have added words to what search and every client that does not draw the card show, after the copy was checked. → a message that carries an Answer is not editable (409: delete it and share again), and the `messages` provider verifies a copy against the stored original (`copyOf`: same run, bot and asker, text a subsequence, citations a subset by number and unchanged, no chips) so the guarantee does not depend on the one route that builds copies. When derived content has a stored twin, either the twin follows or the content is immutable.
- [P5-17 the preview is a promise] A preview and a share are two requests, and the readers of a channel can change between them (a guest is granted access). → the preview returns a digest of (target, answer, copy), the share takes it back and rebuilds the copy from the live readers first; a different digest is a 409 with nothing posted. Also: a request names claims by NUMBER and never carries text, so "can never add content" is by construction, not by validation.
- [P5-17 a nullable column in a unique key] The idempotency key of a share includes the target channel, which a page has none of; a unique index treats NULLs as different, so two saves of one answer would both have been recorded. → `channel_key` is a generated column (`coalesce(channel_id, nil uuid)`) and the unique index and `ON CONFLICT` use it.
- [P5-17 a union member breaks every reader] Adding `masked` to `AnswerCitation` made ten reads of `c.label` / `c.scope` fail typecheck (the web component, three plugin tests): code written for "every citation" had assumed all of them carry a label. TypeScript does not carry a narrowing past `if (a && b) return` → narrow with early returns in sequence (`if (!supported) return …; if (masked) return …;`), and when a shared union grows, typecheck the whole repo before the tests.
- [P5-17 manifests are pinned by events tests] Adding `answer.answer.shared` to the conversations manifest failed `conversations-events.test.ts` (it pins `emits` exactly), which only the `events` project runs; the unit run was green. → when a plugin's manifest changes, run its `--project events` and `--project rls` files too; and `people_guard` (like `workspace_members_guard`) refuses a status change through the app, so a test of "a suspended asker" lifts it as the database owner for one statement.
- [P5-17 review / sentence segmentation of stored model text must fail toward removing more] `removeClaims` cut per LINE and read a marker group after a stop and a space (`Revenue grew 4%. [2] Next.`) as the NEXT sentence's: the claim stayed in the copy and the innocent neighbour went; a hard-wrapped sentence or list item lost only its last line. The model is told to write markers anywhere, so every position happens. → the unit is a block (paragraph with its wrapped lines, list item, quote, table row), a marker group after a stop belongs to the sentence before it, a lower-case word, a digit or an abbreviation after a stop is no end, a removed marker that survives a cut takes its block, the preview lists the words that go, and the property test asserts "no removed marker survives outside fences" next to "the result is a subsequence". When a heuristic decides what is HIDDEN from readers, its errors must all fall on the side of hiding more.
- [P5-17 review / a "shared" marker is a promise to the team] The conversation was marked `shared` for any channel of its team, a private room included: every teammate then listed a dead row (a person, a time, no title) and learned that a share happened; deleting the copy never un-marked it. → mark only when the target is readable by the whole team (not private, the bot's own team), clear the mark with a trigger when the copy is deleted, and list a shared row only while its copy is live and readable. A flag that says "visible to X" is set only by something that proves X can see what it points to, and unset by whatever removes that thing.
- [P5-17 review / what is re-read must be re-read everywhere] Only the citation's label was read again at share time; the lineage's quote and "Sources reached" labels still came from the stored payload, so a source edited since leaked its old words through them, and the provider compared lineage by number alone. → every field derived from a source is re-read or dropped (the quote goes when the label moved), and the provider check compares each kept entry to the original and requires counts of what was left out to be at least the original's and the share note to be the one it computes.
- [P5-17 review / a message that cannot be edited must not offer Edit; a stored copy must not be a request] The server answered 409 to editing a copy, but the menu still offered Edit; and a posted copy of a model's text is an ordinary person's message, so an `@bot` a source planted in it fired that bot's mention trigger as the sharer. → the client reads the same fact the server acts on (`meta.answer`), and the router ignores person messages that carry an Answer for every trigger. A 409 that means "look again" keeps its words above the re-read preview (`refusalOf`); clearing the error while re-reading hid them.
- [P5-17 delta review / a property test must assert the claim side] The "no removed marker survives" property passed for the original blocker because the marker vanished together with the innocent neighbour while the claim stayed (`Revenue grew. [2] Costs fell.`): checking that the marker is gone says nothing about the claim. → generate sentences with UNIQUE tokens, mark which carry the removed citation, assert the token of every carrying sentence is ABSENT and the token of every other sentence (but the documented neighbour) is PRESENT, over every layout the model can write (markers before, after and tight to the stop, prefix, comma lists, ranges, full-width brackets, hard wraps, bullets, fences, inline code, CJK). A generator needs `Math.imul`, not `seed * 1103515245` (it leaves 2^53 and stops being a generator).
- [P5-17 delta review / "never touched" is a hole when the thing shields] Fenced code was never touched, so `Revenue 4.2M [2]` inside a fence kept its claim and a dangling marker the client prints literally, and a stray "```" with no closing line put every later line in the untouched set. → what is left alone must hold nothing that is being removed (a fence with a removed marker goes whole), and a construct only counts when it is complete (a fence with no closing line is prose). The same for "white space": a duplicated ASCII space where NBSP was meant made `.\u00a0[2]` bind to the next sentence; list the characters by code point.
- [P5-17 delta review / a flag recomputed from the latest event] Deleting the newest copy made the conversation private although an older copy was live. → derive the mark from the live copies (the share log), and make the door check that the copy belongs to the conversation (`answer_shares` says which answer it was a copy of); a test that asserts "nothing woke" must first wait for the delivery of the event it is about (the outbox row), not for a later one.
- [P5-17 third review / removal must treat every form the CLIENT renders as a marker; read the client parser, not the shared regex] `removeClaims` knew `[2]`, and the client's `parseInline` unescapes `\[` and `\]` before `withMarkers` looks for `\[(\d{1,3})\]`: `\[2\]`, `[2\]` and `\[2]` were drawn as citation markers in the copy and not removed. The shared scanner and the client parser had been written separately and agreed on the examples in the tests. → the guarantee is tested against the client's own parser (`renderedMarkers` in `clients/web/test/answer-share.test.ts` walks `parseMarkdown` and applies the marker regex to text nodes only, as `MessageMarkdown` does), over every form in a list read off `markdown.ts` end to end (escapes, emphasis, link labels, headings, quotes, list items, unsafe-href labels) and a fuzz; forms the client renders as text (HTML entities, a link labelled `2`) are pinned as untouched. Whenever a shared function must agree with what a screen draws, the test imports the screen's parser. Also: a line is a heading only by the client's own rule (`#{1,6} `), a table row only when it starts and ends with a pipe, and after a cut the rest is read again (taking words out can join `[` and `2]`).
- [P5-17 third review / a search per opener is quadratic in the number of distinct openers] The inline-code closer used `indexOf` of the run per backtick run: descending runs (`\`\`\`\``, `\`\`\``, ...) made it O(n·√n). → list the runs once and keep a forward cursor per length; add the descending case to the timing list. Also cut preview strings by code points, never between the halves of a surrogate pair, and guard a re-pointing UPDATE by the row's current value (`shared_root_id = NEW.id`).
- [P5-17 fourth review / a recognizer written for the bare form misses the decorated one in exactly the position the rule depends on] The "marker group after a stop and a space" rule looked for a literal bracket at the first character, so `. **[2]**`, `. ([2])`, `. (see [2])` bound to nothing: the claim stayed and the neighbour went; the decorated forms had been tested only mid-sentence, where the marker is simply found. → a rule that depends on a POSITION (after a stop) is tested with every form in THAT position, with claim-side assertions (the claim's words absent), and its recognizer reads the same decoration the client does.
- [P5-17 fifth review / five rounds of enumerating decorations is the wrong shape; use one positional rule] Each round found another way the model (or the client's renderer) could dress a marker next to a stop — `**[2]**`, `([2])`, `(see [2])`, `。（[2]）` — and each fix was a longer list (closers, openers, decorations) that the next form fell outside of. The rule that does not need a list is positional: a removed marker group belongs to the unit it sits in, and if no WORD character (letter, digit or mark of any script) lies between the start of the unit and the group, it is at a sentence boundary and the unit before goes too; only word characters are classified. When a heuristic must hide more rather than less, define it by the one property that matters (is there a word before it?) rather than by the set of things that can surround it, and fuzz it by composing random decoration around the position, with unique tokens per sentence and claim-side assertions.
- [P5-17 sixth review / "do not enumerate" applies to unit boundaries too] The sentence splitter rescued a stop from being a boundary through a list of seventeen abbreviations, so `according to Sen. Warren's office [2].` was cut in two at `Sen.` and the first half, with the claim's words, survived (`Gov.`, `Corp.`, `Mt.`, `ca. €3M` the same). The fifth round had taught the lesson for decoration and the same list shape was left standing one function away. → a split is never trusted: a unit that follows a doubtful split (a stop after a word of four letters or fewer in any script, after a token with a dot inside it, or before a character that is not a letter) is marked soft, and a soft unit that goes takes the unit before it, backwards. The list stays only as a way to avoid a split, never as the defence. Also: a boundary rule that works inside a block must also work across blocks (a paragraph that is only `[2]` takes the last unit of the block right before it). Test every "one thing may be a prefix or a suffix" rule with a pool of the things that look like the thing (abbreviations, initials, versions, file names), the unique token BEFORE it and the marker AFTER it, and add the same pool to the hostile-input timing list.
- [P5-17 seventh review / a second list is the first list again] The soft-split rule marked only a single `.` after a short or dotted word, so a quoted question (`"Can we ship Friday?" Dana asked [2].`), an exclamation in a name (`Yahoo! Finance`), an ellipsis and a stop closed by a quote still cut the claim in two: the rule had been written from the examples of the review. → define "doubtful" by what a stop CAN NOT prove (it is not a plain full stop followed by a letter), not by the shapes seen: anything but a single period, any run longer than one character, a number or short word or dotted token before it, anything but a letter after it. Letters are `\p{L}` and `\p{M}` by code point (a word may end in a combining mark). Pool-based claim-side tests get a new pool entry for every shape a review names, and a chain is pinned to stay inside its block.
- [P5-19 a phrase in a template's gate list silences the bot] The plates and SPEC §13 give each template bot a gate list of topics (`refund`, `credential rotation`), and a topic is a *phrase* entry — which the gate hands to the classifier on the `local` alias for every message that stage one did not match. A deployment with no `local` alias (the default one) answers `classifier_unavailable` and holds **every** message: the bot would never reach a model. → templates ship the topics as *patterns* (`/\brefund(s|ed|ing)?\b/i`), which stage one decides alone and which never need a classifier; a team that has bound `local` adds phrase topics beside them. A test refuses a phrase entry in a shipped definition, holds each list to its own sample messages, and refuses an example question of a bot that its own gate would hold (a prompt chip is sent as written). When copying a configuration from a design plate, read what the platform does when the thing it needs is absent.
- [P5-19 seating bots changes a number people read] The loader seats a bot as a `team_members` row, and `memberCount` (the Teams list: "3 people") counted rows, so a team made from a template went from "1 person" to "3 people" the moment its repository's first commit was loaded — a number correct in the response of the request that made the team and wrong in every later one. → a count a screen words as people comes from the roster function (`app.teams_roster`), not from the table that also holds bots; and a test that creates a team from a template reads the count *after* the loader ran (a test that asserts a raw `team_members` count right after creation passes by being early).
- [P5-19 a stored copy of what a repository now holds] The team keeps its template's definition (`template_definition`), and every team response carries it: adding the bot files to the template would have repeated about 25 KB of definitions in each response, next to the same files in the repository. → what the first commit carries is not also stored on the row (`templateDefinitionOf`), and a test pins both (pending files hold the files, the stored definition holds none).
- [P5-19 read-only is a list, so a hand-over is an act] The Brain plate has `handover.mayTag: [orchestrator, coder, …]`, but the loader compiles `mayTag` into a `tasks.handoff` grant — an act, in a bot whose whole claim is that nothing in its list writes. → Brain has no `handover`; a hand-off chip is the person's click, not the bot's grant. Before copying a field from a plate, read what the loader compiles it into; a test pins that Brain's compiled grants are exactly the file's list.
- [P5-19 a literal byte-order mark in a regex] The tool that wrote `/^\uFEFF/` put the character itself in the file, and eslint's `no-irregular-whitespace` was the only thing that saw it (a regex that looks right on screen). → write `\uFEFF` as an escape and scan new files for U+FEFF/U+00A0 before pushing, not after lint.
- [P5-22 web] `pnpm typecheck` failed on `packages/shared` (`Cannot find module 'yaml'`) on a clean HEAD too — stale Windows `node_modules`, not the change → `pnpm install` first, and prove pre-existing with `git stash -u` + filter typecheck before touching code.
- [P5-22 e2e] Two links per checklist step (title + Open button) tripped Playwright strict mode → scope to `.first()` or an aria-label. The mobile settings nav is a `<select>` (options are never "visible") → assert its values via `evaluateAll`, with the desktop sidebar link asserted only on desktop.
- [live shots] `getByText('Nothing waiting')` matched the sidebar hint AND the approvals empty state → scope live assertions to a testid (`approvals-empty`); sidebar copy duplicates screen copy by design.
- [live shots] A guest opening `/t/<team>/bots` gets `ForbiddenBody` ("You do not have access", QueryView 403 branch), not the channel-style `no-access` block → assert the OR of the two, and shoot whichever renders.
- [P5-refactor split const+type same-name zod schema] run-job.ts kept `const RunPayload` unexported while router-subscribe needs the value (safeParse) and holds needs the type → export the const AND the inferred type; a facade that re-exports only `type RunPayload` breaks value use. When a module is split, export both meanings and check value-vs-type use at each importer.
- [P5-refactor invented handle type] run-job split introduced `BotRunHandle` locally, but the real shape lives in SDK types.ts (`botId/botActorId/teamId/workspaceId/trigger/askerActorId/expiresAt`) and callers pass the full handle → import the SDK type, never redefine run handles per module.
- [P5-refactor type-only import of a value] `import { type RunPayload }` then `RunPayload.safeParse` fails TS2693 → import the value (covers both meanings in TS).
- [P5-refactor split dropped an export] claims/markers split left `MARKER_OPEN` unexported while segment.ts imports it (TS2459 on devbox only after sync) → when moving code, re-check every cross-module name is exported.
- [P5-26 schema-compat silently writes a missing snapshot, and the next sync deletes it] A new exported Zod schema (`OkfProposal`, `MemoryRecallFact`) adds a *test* to `packages/shared/test/schema-compat/compat.test.ts`, whose `it.each` walks every exported schema; with `CI` unset `toMatchFileSnapshot` WRITES the missing file and passes, so the full devbox gate reported "Snapshots 2 written" and the two files existed only on the devbox — the next `Sync-Up.ps1` (rsync `--delete`) removed them, and a later run would have silently written them again. → regenerate locally before syncing when a shared entity changes (`pnpm test:schema-compat` writes the missing file; commit it), and `tools/devbox/remote-gates.sh` now fails when `git status --porcelain packages/shared/test/schema-compat/snapshots` is not empty, so a written-but-uncommitted snapshot is loud instead of silent.
- [P5-26 authority is decided before content — the e2e caught what the unit tests could not] `repo-git`'s `write()` ran `plan()` before `authorizeWrite()`, and since P5-25 `plan()` validated every `memory/**` concept with `parseConcept`: a bot's `files.write` into `memory/` therefore answered **422 `okf_invalid`** in the parser's own words instead of the broker's audited **403** — a content verdict about a file the bot was never allowed to write, and **no `kernel.capability.denied` event at all** (PLAN criterion 16). The unit tests could not see it: they ask `checkBotWritePath` directly, and the person-facing 422 test passes either way. The repo's own path rules (`channels/`, `inbox/`) had the same shape — a bot got 400 before any audit. → `write()` is now *plan (paths) → authorize (403 + the audit event) → assertRepoPaths (400) → OKF (422) → size/binary*; the rule to carry forward is that authority is decided before content, always, and that a "denial" nobody records is not a denial yet.
- [P5-25 shipped a green unit suite and a stale e2e expectation] `e2e/api/seed/seed-v4.spec.ts` still expected `memory/` to hold only `facts/` and `journal/`, but since P5-25 the writer regenerates the root `index.md`/`log.md` and one `index.md` per folder that holds a concept — the spec was not re-run in that task and failed the first time P5-26's full `api` e2e ran. → when a change alters what the writer *writes*, run the e2e project that seeds repositories (`pnpm -C e2e exec playwright test --project=api`, about a minute), not just the unit suite; a passing unit suite says nothing about the tree a spec walked yesterday.
- [P5-26 a test asserting its own fixture] `write-run.test.ts` asserted `coAuthors` on the history entry (the field is `coAuthorIds`; the asker also appears in the message as a `Co-authored-by:` trailer) and asserted that the `memory.write` refusals wrote nothing by requiring every refused `memory/` path to 404 — but `memory/index.md` and `memory/log.md` exist by then (the earlier test committed a concept), so the assertion was about the fixture, not the code. → assert against a shape the API's Zod schema accepts (`GetRepoHistoryResponse.parse` already did the narrowing), and assert "nothing was written" as "the paths that did not exist still do not" + "the files that existed are byte-identical".
- [P5-26 gate flake: asserting a run's final state after its reply] `conversations/test/conversation.test.ts` read the run's row as soon as the bot's reply appeared, but the reply is posted by the runtime BEFORE the router closes the run: on a loaded devbox (655 s of tests, `--maxWorkers=3`) the row was still `status: 'running'` and the gate failed on a state the test never waited for (it passed alone, seconds later). → a test that asserts a FINAL state waits for that state (`until`), not for the event that precedes it; a reply tells you the run is about to end, not that it has.
- [P5-refactor duplicate helper in split modules] claims split left clipText/headOf defined in both text/truncate.ts and answer/boundary.ts → the split module imports from the shared helper and re-exports the name for its one importer (remove.ts).
- [P5-25 partial changed writer contract] repo-git P5-25 validates every concept path (422 okf_invalid), so mirror.test.ts "invalid concept is withheld by mirror" now fails at commit → updated the test to the new contract (422 at commit, nothing retained, no log line).
- [P5-25 a test helper typed by the success shape] `RepoWorld.commit` returns `ApiResult<{ sha, parentSha }>`, so a test that asserted a refusal body (`{ error: { message } }`, `{ paths }`) failed typecheck: the helper's type says the body is the success shape, and it also inferred `{ op: string }` for a change literal (not assignable to `RepoWriteChange`'s `'put'`). → cast a refusal body through `unknown` (`res.body as unknown as { error: { code: string } }`) and write the literal op (`op: 'put' as const`); when a helper types a response by its 2xx shape, every 4xx assertion in its tests needs that cast, so expect it and do not "fix" it by widening the helper.
- [P5-25 wrote the test from what the index should say, not what it renders] the new `bundle-tree.test.ts` expected a folder bullet `* [facts/](facts/)`; the renderer (and `parseIndex`, which reads `* [title](path)`) writes `* [facts](facts/)` — the trailing slash belongs to the link target, not the label. → for a new pure renderer, read the exact string the implementation produces (or fix the format first and change both) before writing the assertion; a link's text and its target are separate choices.
- [P5-28 / the phase-5 exit: a guard that compared a directory with itself] P5-28's two new route schemas (`RebuildConceptsPathParams`, `RebuildConceptsResponse`) added two schema-compat snapshot tests, and the devbox gate ran `pnpm test` with `CI` unset: vitest WROTE both files and passed, so the gate was green with two snapshots that existed only on the devbox. The before/after guard added in P5-26 could not see them either — they had already been written by an earlier run on that box, so before and after were equally dirty. The files were never committed, the next `Sync-Up` (rsync `--delete`) removed them, and the **`ci` run on the pushed commit failed at `pnpm test`**: phase 5 had been reported done with its "e2e green headless in CI" gate red on `main`, and the steps after that one (rls, events, schema-compat, e2e) never ran at all. → the devbox gate now runs the suite with `CI=true`, so a missing snapshot fails exactly where CI fails and the gate can no longer pass on a file that lives only on that machine, and the rule to carry forward is that a check proving "this run wrote nothing" must compare against the **committed** tree, never against the state it found the machine in. `pnpm test:schema-compat` writes a missing snapshot locally; run it before syncing whenever a shared schema changes.
- [phase exit: the tag is made by a workflow that reads the commit SUBJECT] `release.yml` tags `phase-N` / `v0.N.0` when the head commit of a push to `main` reads `Phase N · …` (that is how phases 1–4 were tagged, on their PR merge commits). Phase 5's closing commit was titled `Phase 5: the live verification evidence carries the final deployment` — a colon, where the `sed` wants the separator — so the release run **succeeded in 6 s having done nothing**, and phase 5 sat "done" with no tag while every earlier phase had one; a green run that released nothing looks exactly like a green run that released something. → a phase's closing push is either titled to match the release workflow or the release is dispatched by hand (`gh workflow run release.yml -f phase=N`), and the exit check is the tag itself (`git ls-remote --tags origin | grep phase-N`), not the run's colour.
- [git a forced branch switch, with a dirty tree] *(recovered from the devbox's own history, `517a9ba`, which never reached `main`; kept here so the lesson lives with the others.)* `git checkout -f -B main origin/main` reverted every uncommitted edit to tracked files (eight of them: the fake-llm/fake-hindsight CLIs and servers, their README, `deploy.sh`, `values.yaml`) and left a tree whose `git status` looked clean enough to commit on top; two files became part of a commit in their reverted state. → snapshot before the switch (`git ls-files -co --exclude-standard | xargs md5sum > /tmp/tree-before.md5`) and compare after it, and recover the source from the image built out of that tree (`docker create` + `docker cp`, byte-checked against the snapshot) instead of rewriting it by hand.
- [ci] **A GitHub runner has no browser, and it is the wrong place for the suite anyway.** The `ci` run died at "Install Playwright Chromium": Playwright 1.56.1 refuses to install Chromium on `ubuntu26.04-x64`, and `ubuntu-latest` now resolves to that image — the job failed before a single project check ran, and the run before it (the phase-5 exit) failed in the browser step itself. Two separate faults with one answer: the runner only carries the code to the devbox, and the devbox builds, tests and deploys (`tools/devbox/ci.sh`); the browser tests run against a release in the devbox's own CI namespace, where Chromium is already installed. A green Actions run that tested nothing is worse than a red one.
- [devbox] **The devbox checkout belongs to whoever is working in it.** `Sync-Up.ps1` (rsync `--delete`) over `/root/manythreads` while another agent was building a plugin there overwrote that agent's uncommitted edits to shared files (`packages/shared/src/ids.ts`, the event registry, the test-utils database helper), and nothing in git could recover them: no stash, no staged copy, no dangling object. The pipeline works in its own checkout (`/root/ci`, replaced on every run) for the same reason — a sync is only safe over a tree nobody else is writing in.
- [chart] **A flag in a chart is not a feature until the code reads it.** The chart set `MANYTHREADS_ALLOW_TEST_AUTH_IN_PRODUCTION=1` next to the test sign-in token, and `values.yaml` documented it as the way to run the test sign-in route in a test environment — but `resolveTestAuthToken` threw on *any* production process with the token set, so the CI release crash-looped with "the test sign-in endpoint must never run on a real deployment". → the code honours the flag now, and a test pins it: the escape hatch a deployment needs has to be read by the code, and a documented flag nobody reads is a crash loop waiting for the next environment that needs it.
- [P6-01 `readable_team_ids('write')` is leads only] Every member action in the new tasks schema (create, claim, complete, rework, handoff) was policed with `readable_team_ids('write')`, and `write` ranks above `post`, so `member_team_ids` returns only leads for it: the first full run had **every** card create answered 403 "You do not have permission to do that" for Nadia (a member) while Omar (lead) passed, and the suite read like a plugin bug. → a member-level table policy uses `'post'` (read and post are every member, `manage`/`write` are leads and admins); test a new team-scoped door as a **member**, not only as the lead who writes the fixture.
- [P6-02 `destructive: true` means "can never be granted to a bot"] The tasks manifest declared `tasks.claim`/`tasks.complete`/`tasks.handoff`/`tasks.deploy` destructive thinking "it changes state", but the broker's rule is literal — `destructive` is reserved for capabilities no bot may ever hold (`files.delete`) — so the first template load failed with "Allowlist rejected: tasks.claim is destructive and can never be granted" and took `bots/test/templates.test.ts` with it. → before setting the flag, read what the broker does with it (`broker.ts` denies a destructive capability before any grant check); a tool a `BOT.md` names must be non-destructive, and its real danger is bounded by approval/environment rules instead.
- [P6-02 a schema-qualified scalar function's column is not `app_<name>`] `SELECT app.tasks_handoff_pending(...)` comes back to node-postgres under the column name `tasks_handoff_pending`, not `app_tasks_handoff_pending`; the route read `rows[0]?.app_tasks_handoff_pending`, got `undefined`, and answered the very misleading 409 "Could not file the handoff" although the row was written. The same shape was hiding in the release route, where an unknown outcome silently skipped every refusal (`outcome === 'not_owner'` never matched). → always alias a scalar function (`… AS handoff_id`) and read the alias, and never let a route fall through an unrecognized outcome — a refusal the code did not recognise must be a 409, not a 200.
- [P6-04 a refusal that records its reason must not throw] `tasks_complete` writes `blocker_reason` on the card before returning `stale_base`, but the route threw an `HttpError` for the 409: the request transaction rolled back and the reason the plan wants the board to show was gone (the API message was right, the card was blank). → when a refusal's *effect* is data (a reason, an attempt count, a claim audit), the handler returns the 409 as a response so the transaction commits; a thrown error is for refusals that must leave nothing behind.
- [P6-03 `app.actors` is own-row-only under RLS] A handover resolves another actor (a bot by slug, a person by id), and the first version joined `app.actors`/`app.people` directly: a person sees only their own actor row, so every target was "No such actor" while the fixture existed, and `mayTag` would have refused every target for the same reason. → resolve another actor through a system-owned door (`app.tasks_actor_of`, `app.tasks_bot_of`), and let `mayTag`, the route and the tool ask that one door; the kernel's 0011 comment says the same thing about `actors` for every plugin.
- [P6-08 the cron parser with names rejected the numeric fields] `parseField` used the name table whenever a field had one, so `0 0 30 2 *` and a bare `7` in day-of-week threw "Invalid cron month field" and the "never fires" unit test failed on a parser that looked correct. → a field with names still accepts numbers (`/^\d+$/` before the name lookup), and test the mixed forms (`mon-fri` and `1-5`, `0` and `sun`) — the two grammars have to be one grammar.
- [P6-07 a claim is not a label: it starts the card] The new board spec claimed a card, then expected the "Move to" select to offer `in_progress` and the history to gain `Open → In progress`. `app.tasks_claim` sets the card to `in_progress` in the same door (and records only `claimed`), so the option was already gone and `selectOption` waited 30 s; the run reported "Test timeout" from the `finally { context.close() }`, which hid the line for three runs. → read what a door *does* before asserting a state after it (the migration's `UPDATE … state = CASE …` is the contract), and in a Playwright spec put a short `timeout` on an assertion whose element may legitimately be absent so the failure names the assertion rather than the teardown; when a timeout points at `context.close()`, look at the `Error:` line and its call log, not the stack.
- [live Hermes / MCP params are not only the tool] The gateway parsed `tools/call` params with a strict `{ name, arguments }`, and Hermes sends the protocol's reserved `_meta` on every call (`"params": {"name": …, "arguments": …, "_meta": {}}`). The refusal was a JSON-RPC `invalidParams` — "tools/call needs a tool name and object arguments" — so the agent's own words in the thread were "messages.search failed on every attempt … then went unreachable after 3 consecutive failures", and it answered from memory with no tool use at all. Every unit test passed: they built params the way we wrote them, not the way a client does. → a protocol field the spec reserves is part of the wire even when we never send it: accept and ignore `_meta` (`packages/shared/src/entities/mcp.ts`), and when the one test double is a script we wrote (`e2e/conversations/fake-hermes.mjs` speaks no MCP), capture one real client's bytes before believing the shape is right.
- [live Hermes / a definition named an alias no workspace may bind] The seeded placeholder bots' `BOT.md` asked for `model.aliases: [default]`. A definition's alias list is free-form, but the workspace's alias set is closed (`smart`, `fast`, `code`, `local`, `embed`, `vision`, `image`, `transcribe`) and only a bound alias mints a key — so those bots could never call a real gateway, and the seeded world only looked healthy because the e2e stand-in Hermes ignores the key entirely. → a definition that names a model is only as valid as the alias a workspace can actually bind: keep seeds and templates on the closed set, and prove the promise against a real model path (the live check in `tools/devbox/hermes-live.mjs`) rather than a stand-in that answers without one.
- [P6-09 a socket's first frames arrive before the page has loaded] The live route awaited the room's load (a repo read, a snapshot, the update tail) before attaching `socket.on('message')`, and a browser sends its Yjs sync step the moment the socket opens: the step was emitted with no listener and dropped, the server never answered with the document, and both clients sat on an empty page while the socket stayed open (the test timed out on "both editors to receive the page"; nothing closed, so there was no error to read). → attach the handler before the await and buffer frames until the room is ready (then replay them), and test a socket route's handshake against a client that sends immediately, not after a pause the product does not have.
- [P6-09 one registry, two keys] `rooms` was keyed by `<teamId>:<path>` but `openRoom` looked it up by `<slug>:<path>` and the pending-load map by `<slug>:<path>`: every socket for the same page created its own room, so two editors could not converge no matter what the protocol did. → key every lookup in one function from the values the room is stored with (`room.key`), and assert the registry's identity in a test that opens two sockets (the merge test) rather than only one.
- [P6-09 a fixture path containing the word in an assertion] The live tests waited for a commit whose message included `live`, and the page path was `pages/live/...`: the initial `Create page pages/live/...` commit matched, so the idle test read the file before the session had committed and failed on `quiet` vs `still editing`. → assert the commit's exact shape (`/^Edit .+ live/` on the subject line), never a substring that a fixture path can supply; and expect the writer's message to carry the `Co-authored-by` trailers (it stores the full message), so compare the first line where only the subject is meant.
- [P6-12 a runtime value the spec, the migration and the runtime do not have] An editing pass added `hermes-paper` and `ask` to `BotRuntime` and to the CHECK of the **already-applied** `bots/0001_bots.sql`, and to the shared type, backed by a new test file that asserted the new values parse. Nothing else in the tree knows them: there is no provider for either, `SPEC §7.1` says `hermes | rules`, and the migration runner refuses an applied file whose checksum changed — so on any existing database the server would not start, and on a fresh one a team could save a definition no runtime answers. The run-job guard meant to catch it (`bot.runtime !== 'hermes' && provider.id === 'hermes'`) could never be true, because the provider is looked up *by* the runtime. → a vocabulary change is a migration (a new file with a new CHECK and a backfill), a spec line, and a provider, in that order; and a guard whose two conditions cannot both hold is dead code that reads like safety — prove it by naming the case that reaches it. The pass also left two test files with joined lines (`…canary-secret-value;      process.env…`), an `if (!fake) return` that silently skipped a budget test, and `if (run) expect(...)` around a value the `until` helper already guarantees: when an edit lands a hunk that weakens an assertion, the typecheck and the diff are the place to catch it, not a green test run.
- [P6-02 a role's first act must be a tool that exists] The engineering Orchestrator's whole job is to split a goal into cards and hand them out, and `tasks.create` was a route with no capability: the write side of the tool was simply absent, so a template bot written from the spec would have had a body telling it to do something the model was never offered. It only surfaced when the template bot was written and its `capabilities.native` was checked against what the manifest actually declares. → before writing a bot (or any template) around a tool, check the capability registry, not the routes; a route is for a person, and a tool is the same door declared a second time.
- [Windows / a filesystem path split on '/' only] `startServer` picks the one storage plugin by the last segment of each discovered plugin's directory (`s.dir.split('/').pop()`), and on Windows the separator is `\`: the "last segment" was the whole path, matched neither `storage-local` nor `storage-s3`, and **both** plugins loaded — `storage-s3` then stopped every DB test in `s3ConfigFromEnv` ("MANYTHREADS_STORAGE=s3 needs MANYTHREADS_S3_BUCKET") before the first assertion, though the test server had asked for `local` explicitly. → split filesystem paths on `/[\/]/` (or use `node:path`), and when a filter looks right on the devbox and wrong on the laptop, check the separator before the logic; this one made the whole DB-backed suite unrunnable on the platform the repo documents for local development.
- [nginx / a socket route under `/api/` is not `/ws`] The web image's nginx gave `/ws` (the kernel's own hub) `proxy_http_version 1.1` and the `Upgrade`/`Connection` headers, and left `location /api/` at the default: HTTP/1.0, no upgrade. Live pages are a *plugin* socket route under `/api/teams/:slug/pages/live`, so the public site forwarded the handshake as an ordinary GET, `@fastify/websocket` called `reply.callNotFound()`, the server logged a 404 at 5 ms and the editor showed "Offline — changes stay here until the connection is back". Every unit test talks to the server directly, so nothing could see it. → an upgrade path is defined by the proxy, not the route: when a feature is a socket, curl the *deployed* URL with `Upgrade: websocket` (a 401/101 proves the route, a 404 proves the proxy), and give every location that can carry one the upgrade headers — a location that sets any `proxy_set_header` inherits none from the server block, so repeat them.
- [P6-09 presence sent the person id where the row needed the actor] The browser's awareness state carried `session.person.id` (the only id the session payload has), the socket refused any state whose `actorId` was not `caller.actorId`, and `page_awareness.actor_id` is a foreign key to `actors` — so every cursor frame from every browser was dropped and two editors never saw each other, though the *text* converged (different frame type) and the unit test passed because it sent the actor id. Finding it took two browsers on the live site and `document.querySelector('[data-testid="live-people"]')` returning null in both. → when a wire field is an id, say in the type which table it names and test with the value the client actually has (the session payload), not the one the server happens to prefer; and a guard that silently `return`s is a guard with no observable failure — make the refused frame a close with a reason, or assert the row it should have written.
- [devbox / Docker's copies filled the disk and the kubelet deleted the live images] Every CI run builds a new `manythreads/server` and `manythreads/web` image; `deploy.sh` pruned the k3s copies but never Docker's own, nor the build cache. On 2026-10-24 the root disk reached 91 %, the node was tainted `disk-pressure`, every pod was evicted, and the kubelet's image garbage collection removed `manythreads/postgres:19`, `server` and `web` from containerd — the pods came back as `ImagePullBackOff` (there is no registry to pull from). Recovery: `docker builder prune` + `docker image prune --filter until=24h` (52 GB), then `docker save <img> | k3s ctr images import -` for each image the pods name. `deploy.sh` now removes old Docker server/web tags and caps the build cache at 8 GB on every deploy. Check `df -h /` before a long build session; images exist only on this machine.
- [P6-06 Hermes: an empty tool list is not "no tools"] `platform_toolsets.cli: []` in a profile gave the agent terminal, file, web and sub-agent tools (Hermes treats an unset or empty list as its full default; only a non-empty list is authoritative). A first draft of the grant mapping rendered `[]` for a bot with no `capabilities.runtime` → render a harmless toolset (`todo`) and put every guarded toolset in `agent.disabled_toolsets`, then prove it against the real CLI (`hermes chat --oneshot --format stream-json`: the tool_use events and the tools the agent lists), not against the YAML.
- [P6-06 Hermes docker: `terminal.cwd: /workspace` turns the mount off] With `backend: docker` and `docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace: true`, naming `/workspace` as `cwd` makes Hermes treat the path as already inside the container and skip the bind mount: commands ran in an empty container directory and files never reached the host. `cwd: "."` plus the runner's process directory (the team workspace) mounts it. Hermes also leaves its sandbox container running after it exits (`sleep infinity`) → label it (`docker_extra_args: ["--label", …]`) and remove it by label when the job ends.
- [P6-06 a socket's first frame beats the token lookup] Same shape as the live-pages mistake: the runner says `hello` the instant the socket opens, before the bearer token has been resolved in the database. The handler attaches its `message` listener first, queues the frames, and replays them in order once authenticated.
- [P6-06 test: a stand-in script inside a template literal] The e2e stand-in for Hermes is a script held in a JS template string, where `\s` loses its backslash and `/runs\/[^/]+/` becomes a regex syntax error: the "agent" crashed with exit 1 and every run in the file failed with no message. Avoid regex literals with escapes in such scripts (use `split`/`indexOf`), and when a run "fails", print the run's own reason before debugging the test.
- [live / a key row is not a key at the proxy] `syncBotKey` returned `unchanged` for a live row without asking LiteLLM, so when the live proxy was replaced every bot's key existed only in our table and every run died on the proxy's 401. → a sync confirms the key at the proxy (update, and register the same value again on 404); a boot heal and a 401 report trigger it.
- [live / a failed run was silent] `runtime-hermes` closed a failed or refused run and posted nothing, so people waited forever. → one fixed-text notice per run, a category only (never the error text, which carried a key prefix and hash), through the reply's own `messages.post` door.
