- [live / a fake more permissive than the proxy hid a rejection] The real LiteLLM answers 401 "workspace_id is not allowed in request body" to any body carrying a `workspace_id` key, even nested in `metadata`; our key mint and gate calls sent exactly that, and every bot key mint failed live while all tests passed, because `tools/fake-llm` accepted any body. → a test double must refuse what the real service refuses once we have seen it refuse: the fake now returns the same 401 for `workspace_id` anywhere in a `/key/generate`, `/key/update` or `/chat/completions` body (with a test), and our metadata names are `manythreads_workspace` / `manythreads_bot`. When a live call fails and the fake passed, fix the fake first.
- [search test / a random word whose typo is itself] `word()` drew 10 random letters and the ranking test dropped the 5th; when letters 5 and 6 matched (about 1 run in 25) the "typo" was the exact word, and `expected 1 to be greater than 1` failed once in the full suite and never alone. → random test words never repeat a neighbouring letter; when a test builds a variant of a random value, make sure the variant differs.
- [live Hermes / the role that creates cards could not pass them on] `tasks_handoff` and `tasks_handoff_pending` allowed only the owner or a lead, but the Orchestrator creates cards unowned and must hand each over without ever claiming it, so its first `tasks.handoff` was refused. The spec's role and the door's rule were never tried together. → a handover is also allowed to the card's creator while nobody owns it (tasks migration 0004, a new file: 0001 was already applied); the route and the tool share the two SQL functions, so one change covers both. Check a new rule against the template bot's actual first call sequence.
- [devbox / a "scratch" Hermes home shared the live install's source] A research run made a throwaway Hermes home under the scratchpad but used the live install's source tree (`/root/.hermes/hermes-agent`). Hermes regenerates its launcher (`hermes-agent/.hermes/bin/hermes`) for the home it bootstraps, so the shared launcher was rewritten to the scratch Python, and systemd's `hermes-gateway.service` (which execs that launcher, `Restart=always`) came back on an interpreter inside a temp directory. Repaired by pointing both launchers back at `/root/.hermes/tools/python-…` and restarting the service. A scratch Hermes needs its own copy of the source (or the official installer into its own home), never the live tree; check `systemctl status hermes-gateway` and `grep scratchpad /root/.hermes/hermes-agent/.hermes/bin/*` after any Hermes experiment.
- [hermes-pods] A Secret mounted with mode 0400 is owned by root, so the Hermes container (uid 1000) got EACCES and crash-looped → mount the profile Secret 0444 (only the Hermes container mounts it) and do NOT set a pod `fsGroup` (on local-path it re-owned the whole workspace volume to group 1000 and loosened the deployer kubeconfig to 0660); and make a volume's sub-directories writable for uid 1000 with a root init container (the kubelet creates them owned by root).
- [hermes-pods] A bot-run `git-receive-pack` reads the bot's own git config, so a bot could switch the protecting `update` hook off (`core.hooksPath`) → bots push through `sudo -u repo mt-receive-pack.sh` (system config `remote.origin.receivepack`) and the repository of record is writable by the `repo` user only.
- [hermes-pods] `tar | kubectl exec` kept the staging folder's 0600 modes, so nginx (not root) answered 403 for the screenshot gallery → chmod a+rX after extracting.
- [devbox disk] Importing a 4.7 GB Hermes image into k3s while Docker keeps its own copy and the Playwright base pushed `/` to 87% → drop Docker's copies of images that live in k3s (`docker rmi`), and `docker builder prune -af`, before importing big images.
- [devbox disk, again 2026-10-27] The lesson above was applied by hand, not in the scripts: every deploy left Docker copies of the server, web and pod images that k3s already held, the 4 GB workbench rebuild tipped `/` to 90%, the kubelet evicted the live database, Hindsight, LiteLLM and both team pods and garbage-collected the hermes-pod image, and the team pods then sat in ImagePullBackOff (the deploy had skipped the build because the image was still present when it checked) → `deploy.sh` skips an image k3s already has and `docker rmi`s each image once it is in k3s, `ci.sh` drops its copies after importing; after a deploy check `kubectl get pods -n manythreads-bots`, not only `/readyz`.
- [ENG-01 a handover note is not a channel] `tasks.handoff` cuts its note at 1000 characters and a bot sees another bot's thread posts only as search snippets, so the plan reviewer's fifth objection never arrived and the two bots waited on each other. → what the next bot must read goes on the card (`tasks.describe`, 8000 characters, the holder writes, the next reads); the note is a short pointer.
- [ENG-01 a handover left the new owner unable to claim] A handover releases the claims and sets the owner, and `tasks_claim` answered `already_owned` even to that owner, so `tasks.describe` (needs a live claim) answered `claim_expired` for every bot that was handed a card. Found only when real bots ran the chain: the tool and the door were tested apart. → tasks 0013 lets the owner claim again; test a rule through the sequence a bot actually follows (handover, then claim, then describe).
- [ENG-01 a running gateway lists its tools once] A Hermes profile connects to the MCP server and lists tools when it starts; a new grant (`tasks.describe`) never reached a running bot, and the profile file did not change, so the pod's restart check (a hash of config and env) saw nothing. → the profile carries a digest of the granted native tools, so a changed grant changes the file and the pod restarts that gateway.
- [ENG-01 one key-like string ends a run, then every later run] The model gateway's guard blocks a whole request that carries a key-like string, and the string stays in the Hermes session of the thread, so every later wake of that bot failed in a second. → a run that fails before any output with "Content blocked" starts again on a fresh session (the stale-session path in `gateway.ts`); bots filter command output through a redaction `sed` and a `tail`, and never print `.env`, fixtures or whole logs. Check the failed run's own error in the pod log before assuming the bot is wrong.
- [ENG-01 a deploy target belongs to one team] `deployTargets` gave `test-manythreads` to Delivery (agent bridge), so the Engineering deployer on the managed pod had no context for it and could not deploy; the template told it the namespace without anyone checking the team had it. → a bot instruction that names a resource is only true if the platform grants it to that team: check the chart values, not the README. The target is Engineering's now.
- [ENG-01 a whole-monorepo suite does not fit a bot's run] The tester's runs on the full suite ran past 2400 s and ended with no hand-over. → the tester runs lint, typecheck, build and the test projects that cover the change, each under `timeout`; the project's CI runs everything after the merge. A pod without a database says so plainly instead of pulling one.
- [ENG-01 sync a template into a live team] Team bot copies are made at creation; `tools/devbox/sync-engineering-bots.mjs <team>` moves only the chain (keeps the team's placement unless `TEMPLATE_PLACEMENT=1`) and renames the old orchestrator, reviewer and browser-tester. Existing teams were on the Claude bridge, not the managed pod, until it was run with `TEMPLATE_PLACEMENT=1`.
- [devbox memory, 2026-10-28] The host hit a global OOM and rebooted: two host-side `vitest` workers held ~8.4 GiB of 15 GiB (each 4.2 GiB; no pod limit reaches host processes), and the live server, web, Postgres, mailpit, cert-manager, the CNPG operator and Traefik had no memory limit, so nothing was capped before the kernel picked victims. → every pod gets a memory limit (values-live.yaml; deploy.sh for cert-manager and CNPG); cap a vitest run (`--maxWorkers=2`, `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=2048`) and do not run it beside a deploy or a bot's full-suite run.
- [ENG-02 publish] `mt-publish.sh` read the open pull request's head straight after pushing a second commit to its branch, and GitHub had not moved it yet (seen on real GitHub, up to ~15 s), so a good push exited 4 → ask again for up to a minute before calling the pull request "at another commit"; the test double of GitHub moves the head at once, so only a run against real GitHub (a throwaway branch, closed and deleted after) shows such lags.
- [P10-09 backup scripts] `find -regex` uses emacs syntax by default, where `{8}` is not a repeat count: the "keep the newest N sets" prune matched nothing and every set stayed → give `-regextype posix-extended`; the test that runs the script three times with retention 2 caught it. A dump taken while the server runs and row counts read afterwards disagree by whatever was written in between → read the counts in the same repeatable-read session that `pg_dump --snapshot` joins. `DROP DATABASE` also drops `ALTER ROLE ... IN DATABASE ... SET search_path` settings, which a dump does not carry → the backup saves them as `db-<name>.settings.sql` and the restore replays them. Nothing in the scripts could be run against the cluster from the coding agent (the shell classifier refuses `exec`/`create namespace` on the shared cluster): the first live drill is the real test.
- [P10-09 review: checks against the real image, not the fakes] Run against the pinned database image in Docker, the scripts held, but the reading of them found what the stand-in `kubectl` could not: an unquoted heredoc that builds a pod spec expanded `$(ls …)` and `$PATH` on the operator's machine (escape them, and assert the applied YAML keeps them verbatim); `initdb -D` on the root of a fresh `emptyDir` fails as uid 999 (`could not change permissions`), so use a sub-directory; `initdb` with no locale makes a `SQL_ASCII` cluster (`-E UTF8 --locale=C.UTF-8`); a `DROP DATABASE` / `CREATE DATABASE` restore loses the database's privileges (the chart's `REVOKE CONNECT … FROM PUBLIC` that keeps the memory engine's login out of the app database), so the backup saves them in `db-<name>.settings.sql`; a restore that drops the live database before `pg_restore` finishes loses it on a bad dump, so it restores into `<name>_restoring` and renames; a role created `NOLOGIN` stays so (the memory engine's setup only sets its password). A WaitForFirstConsumer claim used only by a CronJob is Pending until the first run, which `helm --wait` waits on.
- [P10-10 metrics page] A `/metrics` page is the one route that must start closed: with no `MANYTHREADS_METRICS_TOKEN` (or a short one) it answers 404, with a wrong one 401, and the web pod's nginx never passes it on. A new exported Zod schema also needs its schema-compat snapshot file committed (the devbox gate writes it silently), and a check that reads a failed query must keep its last verdict rather than read the gap as "fine" (each section runs in a savepoint and is `null` when it fails).
- [P10-10 review: a health number counts the rows that are still waiting, not every row not marked done] The outbox lag and backlog read `done_at IS NULL`, but a row the outbox gives up on gets `dead_at` and keeps `done_at` empty, so one dead-lettered row would have held the "outbox lag" check tripped for ever and aged without bound; the unit test answered from a fake and could not see it → read the consumer's own pending predicate (`done_at IS NULL AND dead_at IS NULL`, the `outbox_pending` index) and pin it, and add a database-backed case with a dead row. Also: a job that mails people must not take its words from its payload (the request role can call `app.enqueue_job`): rebuild the text from the check's name and send only when the state the watcher stored agrees; and read a cached count (`DeadJobCounter`) before the section transaction opens, not inside it, so one pass never holds two connections at once.
- [P14-09 notifications: levels, keywords, push] Three things to keep. (1) Web Push needs no library: VAPID (ES256 JWT, `ieee-p1363` signature) and the `aes128gcm` payload encryption are about 150 lines of `node:crypto`, and the one way to know they are right is the test vector of RFC 8291 appendix A, byte for byte (a ciphertext recalled from memory was wrong in the test, the code matched the RFC). (2) A push endpoint is a URL the browser hands us and the server then calls: it is allowed only at https port 443, no user name, on a known push service host (`MANYTHREADS_PUSH_HOSTS` adds more), with redirects off and an 8 second limit, or the route is a way to make the server call any address. (3) A push body shows the message text only for a public team channel; a private channel, a direct message and a group conversation always get the fixed line, and a test pins it, because the push service and the lock screen are not the place for private text. The network call runs in a detached job, never inside the consumer's transaction. New exported Zod schemas and the changed `Notification` need their schema-compat snapshot files committed (`git status packages/shared/test/schema-compat/snapshots`); the gate writes missing ones silently. A stylelint rule here wants `@media (width <= 600px)`, not `max-width`.
- [P14-14 webhooks: what the database and the plugin guard really accept] Three things found by running it, not by reading. (1) `app.permission_rank` knows only `read`, `post` and `manage`: a policy written with `readable_team_ids('write')` compiles and silently shows no row to anyone (the create answered, the next read said "no such webhook"). Use `'manage'` for a lead-only table. (2) The plugin SQL guard rejects `set_config`, so a system transaction cannot switch the acting actor by itself; a webhook posts as its own author through a definer function (`app.webhooks_act_as`, system role only, one live webhook of the transaction's workspace), the same pattern the answer plugin uses. The system role also needs `EXECUTE` on `app.message_authors` when a post runs in a system transaction, because the post names the author for the live push. (3) A Slack link `<url|label>` looks like a tag to the HTML stripper: convert Slack syntax first, strip HTML second, and break `@` mentions last, or the link silently disappears and a mention still pings. The events table column is `occurred_at`, not `at`. New schemas need their schema-compat snapshot files committed.
- [P14-14 review: three holes in a public secret door] (1) The secret sits in the URL path (`/hooks/<id>/<secret>`), and the server's request log (and nginx's access log) wrote the whole address: `log-redact.ts` now replaces the secret segment, and the web pod's nginx has `access_log off` for `/hooks/`; any new credential-in-URL route must be added to `redactUrl` with a test. (2) The mention breaker only knew ASCII (`@(?=[A-Za-z0-9_])`) while the parser reads a handle in any script, so `@éric` or `@عمر` in a payload still pinged someone: test "no mention survives" with `parseMarkup`, not with a hand-written regex. (3) A JSON value nested 200,000 deep parses but cannot be printed (stack overflow), which was a 500 for anyone holding the address: wrap a payload reader so any throw is a 400. A header button taller than the band (a plain `.btn` is 31 px, the band holds 28) grew every channel header by 4 px; size header controls like `.icon-btn` and give them an icon below 600 px, or the title is cut to "# q…" on a phone. The e2e test front must also proxy a new public path (`/hooks`).
- [P14-14 webhooks live push had no author] `app.message_authors` joins the messages to `app.visible_channel_ids('read')`, which is empty for the system role, so a message posted in a system transaction (a webhook) was pushed live without `author` and the open channel showed "Someone" until reload; the reads in the tests passed because they run as a person → when a code path pushes a message from a system transaction, assert on the frame a socket receives, not only on a later list. Fixed by webhooks migration 0002 (system role names the authors of the messages it asks about).
- [P14-12 message actions: a feature that adds a button to every row changes a test that counted buttons] The "More" menu used to exist only for a message you could edit or delete, and `e2e/channels/post-and-read.spec.ts` asserted that Nadia saw no "More actions" button on Rafi's message; adding "Copy link" and "Forward…" for everyone made that assertion false → assert on the menu items (no Edit, no Delete…) instead of on the button. Three design points to keep: (1) a forwarded message stores only a reference (`meta.forward`), and the card is built for each reader under that reader's own row level security, so the live push of a forward carries no body (one push goes to everybody; each client fetches its own copy); (2) a history of old texts is a second copy of text that was edited away, so deleting the message drops it in the same statement and the author can clear it; (3) a link to a message is checked by asking for the message first (403 shows a "cannot see" page with no text), never by trusting that the channel lookup passed.
- [P14-12 message actions: two things only a browser run found] (1) Each row of the message list is `contain: layout`, which makes it a stacking context, so the row below painted over the open "More actions" menu of the row above and Playwright's click on the third item hit "<p>third note</p>"; the two-item menu had hidden it → raise the row that holds an open menu (`.vrow:has(.acts .menu)`), and click every item of a new menu in a spec, not only the first. (2) A checkbox placed above the composer never toggled: pressing it moved focus out of the composer, the composer folded its toolbar, the footer shrank and the box slid from under the pointer between press and release, so the click landed on the panel → anything added next to the composer must not change the composer's height when it takes focus (`.rpf:focus-within` keeps the toolbar open). Also: a header/bar that appears in every channel moves every visual baseline; show a bar only when it has content and put the empty-state control in the header's action slot.
- [P14-12 review: a copy of a message is a message to every subscriber] "Also send to channel" posts the same words twice, and the bot router reads the body of every posted message (not the mention rows), so `@bot do X` in a thread reply started two runs. Skipping the mention rows in the copy was not enough → when a feature posts a second message, check every subscriber of `channel.message.posted`; the copy carries `meta.alsoSentFrom` and the router ignores it (test in `runtime-hermes/test/run.test.ts`, checked to fail without the fix). The server also refuses the flag in a conversation with a bot.
- [P14-12 live: a popover near the end of a virtual list] The five-item "More" menu and the edit-history popover open below their anchor; for the newest messages that is past the end of the list, where the scroll area cuts them off and the composer lies over Edit and Delete…, and a `.vpop` was also painted under the rows below it (each row is a stacking context, and only the open-menu row was raised) → `usePopoverAbove` (`shell/usePopoverSide.ts`) measures from geometry on open and on resize and opens upward when there is no room below and more above; every row that holds an open popover is raised (`.vrow:has(.vpop)`), and the e2e puts the message at the end of a filled list and checks each item with `elementFromPoint`, not only that it is visible. A status line that belongs to "the latest" action (Marked unread from here) must be cleared by the next one, or two rows show it.
- [P14-12 CI red: an enum test nobody ran] The coder and reviewer ran the channels, rls, schema-compat and e2e suites but not `packages/kernel/test/schema-enums.test.ts`, which fails when a new SQL CHECK enum column (`channel_bookmarks.kind`) is not mapped to its shared Zod enum → a new CHECK-constrained column needs an entry in `ENUM_COLUMNS`; before shipping run the whole `pnpm test` (all unit files), not only the touched packages.
- [P14-10 quick switcher: a slice that lived only in the team pod] The bot chain's `feat/quick-switcher-slice-1` existed only in the pod's repository, so the working tree had none of it; it was recovered with `git diff <merge-base> <branch>` over `kubectl exec` into a patch and `git apply`. Look in the pod repository before assuming a bot task left nothing, and never trust a spec that "never ran": the Caps Lock fix (`e.key.toLowerCase()`) and the spec both came from running it.
- [P14-10 e2e: read-state specs need their own stack] Esc and Shift+Esc mark the persona's channels read, which other specs on the shared server watch; the spec uses `startStack`. A sidebar link's accessible name starts with `#`, so locate it by `a[href$="/c/<name>"]`, and `getByRole('button', { name: 'Link' })` also matches "Link a channel", so pass `exact: true`.
- [P14-02 composer: a focus-dependent toolbar must keep its popovers inside `.composer`] The toolbar folds when focus leaves the composer; a picker rendered outside it, or a click on its search box, folded the toolbar and moved the box. The emoji picker is a sibling inside `.cbox`, `useDismiss` is told it is "inside", and `.ctool` is `flex: none` with the phone toolbar scrolling sideways instead of wrapping.
- [P14-11 search parity: what to remember] (1) `actors` shows a caller only their own row, so `from:nadia` cannot be resolved by joining it from the app role; a definer function (`app.search_resolve_people`, owned by the system role) answers, checking the caller with `lookup_workspace_role()` (a guest resolves only themself). A name that matches nothing must find nothing, never be searched as text, and an unreadable private channel must answer the same "no channel called x that you can see" as a channel that does not exist. (2) A hit count is a leak when it is taken before the policy runs: every list is built from rows the policy already let through, and "there is more" is `limit + 1` rows of the same readable set, never a separate count. Test it with a second person who has 25 hidden matches: the answer must equal the answer for a word nobody wrote. (3) Changing a SQL function's argument list needs `DROP FUNCTION` of the old signature (a new default argument is a new function), then `REVOKE … FROM PUBLIC` and the `GRANT`s again; give the new arguments defaults so the capabilities and older tests keep their three-argument calls. (4) Filter text inlined into dynamic SQL goes through `format('%L')` as a typed literal, because the planner needs the real values to choose the channel and trigram indexes; the policy still applies on top. (5) Run the whole `pnpm test` before handing over: a new exported Zod schema needs its schema-compat snapshot committed.
- [P7-01 proposals: a rule in the writer breaks every test that reaches it by another door] Making the repo writer refuse a person's direct write to `bots/`, `TEAM.md`, `skills/`, `routines/` turned 100 tests red at once: the shared `RepoWorld.commit` helper, the seed (`seed-repo`, `seed-work`), the BOT.md loader tests and the hermes run tests all set those files up with a plain commit. Only the packages first touched were run, so the first full `pnpm test` found them. → run the whole suite after changing a writer rule; give tests an honest door (`world.commit` sends guarded paths through a proposal applied at once; `world.commitRaw` writes through the service for tests whose subject is something else, such as a broken BOT.md); the seed passes `proposalId: 'seed'`.
- [P7-01 proposals: three database details] (1) A new CHECK enum column (`proposals.status`, `op`, `author_kind`) needs its entry in `ENUM_COLUMNS` of `packages/kernel/test/schema-enums.test.ts` and a shared enum; the schema-compat snapshots are written silently when `CI` is unset, so `git status packages/shared/test/schema-compat/snapshots` must show them before committing. (2) Inside a SECURITY DEFINER function owned by the system role the `is_*` helpers are always true, so the caller is checked with `lookup_*`/`app.actor()`; one function (`proposals_why_not`) holds the "may this person apply it" rule, used by the view and the decision. (3) A proposal's rls test needs the channels and bots migration sources too, because the bots table needs `app.threads`.
- [P7-01 proposals: a check message must not repeat the field] The BOT.md parser's `message` already names the field path (`role: Invalid input ...`); prefixing it again with `fieldPath` printed the path twice. Show the loader's own text as it is.
- [P14-11 CI red: a shots script edited after the last typecheck] `e2e/screens/task.p14-11.shots.ts` walked `parentElement` with a `HTMLElement | null` variable and failed `tsc` (an `SVGElement` can be a parent); the agent edited the file after its last `pnpm typecheck` → a live-shots file is part of the e2e project: run `pnpm typecheck` once more after the last edit of ANY file, including `*.shots.ts`.
- [CI red at midnight UTC: the daily journal test spans two days] `packages/plugins/memory-git-mirror/test/mirror.test.ts` ("the daily journal ... written once") waits for the journal file of one day while the run crossed 00:00 UTC (2026-10-29 → 10-30) and timed out; it passes before and after midnight → compute the day once at the start of the test (or fake the clock); re-run by hand when a red run straddles midnight.
- [P14 e2e flake: channel opened at the top] `message-actions.spec.ts` "stay reachable" failed ~1 run in 4 under load: the channel opened at its FIRST message with "Jump to latest" and a stray "New" line. `useQuery` keeps the last answer while a new key loads, so the roster read `ok` with an empty list while the team slug was still unknown; `self` was null, all 40 of the reader's own messages counted as unread, and the "open on first unread" effect scrolled to the top. → `People.settled` says the roster is the answer for THIS team (the answer carries its slug), and the unread decision waits for it; the spec delays the roster 1.5 s on purpose. A "loaded" flag on a stale-while-revalidate hook must be tied to the key it answers.
- [P7-01 review / a commit cannot be rolled back with the row that names it] Applying a proposal commits to git and only then marks the row `applied`; an apply racing a decline (or a double click) both passed the "is it open" check, the decline won the row, the apply's transaction rolled back, and the file had changed while the proposal said "declined" → whatever decides one record AND does something a rollback cannot undo takes a per-record `pg_advisory_xact_lock` first and reads the state after it (`lockProposal`), and the race has a test that runs both at once (it fails without the lock).
- [P7-01 review / tools that commit to a guarded place] Closing the direct-commit door left four operator scripts (`tools/devbox/sync-engineering-bots.mjs`, `push-template-bots.mjs`, `hermes-live.mjs`, `demo-journeys.mjs`) and the counting bench POSTing to `repo/commit` for `bots/` and `TEAM.md`: they would have answered 403 on the next run → when a door closes, grep `tools/` and `e2e/screens` for the old route, not only the app and the specs; each now posts a proposal with `applyNow` (one file each).
- [P7-08 a granted capability is not a call] The first free-mode judge counted a "forbidden" call as made whenever BOT.md granted the capability, which failed Brain's own shipped set (Brain is granted `messages.post` and must still not post in the case that forbids it) → a definition only proves what it cannot do: judge `toolCalls.required` from the grants, report everything that needs the bot's behaviour (forbidden calls, words, citations) as "not checked", and never count a skipped assertion as passed.
- [P7-08 two jobs for one change] A commit that touches BOT.md and its set file (or a guarded path applied one file at a time) queues two eval jobs; with concurrency 2 both found no row and made two runs, and a BOT.md-only commit made a failed run ("the set file does not exist") that a test then mistook for the real one → give the job a `serialKey` per bot so the second sees the first's row, look the row up by (bot, trigger, commit) before inserting, and make tests wait for the job queue to be idle instead of for the first row.
- [P7-08 an RLS test that inserts through SELECT proves nothing] `INSERT ... SELECT ... FROM app.bots` run as a persona who cannot read the bot inserts zero rows and raises nothing, so "refused" looked like "allowed" → read the ids as the system role and insert with explicit values when a test asserts a refusal.
- [P7-08 review / a read-then-insert guard is not a lock; a pass is not a full pass] The route's "is a run open" check and its INSERT were two statements, so two requests at once could both start a run: take `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the bot before the check (the race could not be reproduced through the test server, so the test is a guard, not a proof). A case that passes on `toolCalls.required` while its `mentions` were skipped counted as a plain pass in "Eval 2 of 2 passed"; the tab now says "only partly checked". A model run multiplies the BOT.md body (up to 256 KB) by up to 100 cases: cap the total prompt before the first call. A run whose job exhausts its attempts in `begin` stayed `running` for an hour (409 for ten minutes): end it on the last attempt. The placeholder set name `-` of a run that has not read BOT.md must never reach the API's `set` or the tab.
- [P7-08 review / the 9 full-run failures were load, not the eval change] notifications/levels-keywords-push, runtime-hermes/run, test-utils/seed-repo and seed-work failed once in a full run and passed alone; the full `CI=true pnpm test --maxWorkers=3` with the whole P7-08 change in place ran clean (329 files passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed), and the change touches none of their seeds, ports or row counts. Treat a lone full-run failure that passes alone as load on the shared dev DB; re-run before blaming the change.
- [P10-05 the subscribe wrapper dropped extra handler arguments] The kernel wrapped each plugin event handler as `(event, tx) => handler(...)`, so a new third argument (event meta: id, time, actor, team) arrived as undefined and the audit subscriber silently wrote no rows at all → when a handler signature grows, check every wrapper between the outbox and the plugin (`context.ts` subscribe), and make an integration test assert that at least one row appears before testing the details.
- [P10-05 events carry an actor id, not a person id] `events.actor_id` and payload `ActorId` fields are `app.actors.id`, while `personId` fields and the web's member ids are `people.id` (= `actors.ref_id`) → store and filter by the person or bot id (resolve the actor first), or filters on "who" match nothing.
- [P10-05 Postgres has no `max(uuid)`, and one transaction runs one statement at a time] A backfill bound used `max(id)` on a uuid and failed; `Promise.all` over one tx client printed a pg deprecation warning → use `ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1` and run a tx's queries in sequence.
- [P10-05 every new exported Zod schema needs a snapshot] The schema-compat suite writes missing snapshots silently when `CI` is unset and fails when it is set → generate with `-u` and commit the files, or the full run fails in CI only.
- [P10-05 a second password sign-in on the same e2e client gets 403] The first sign-in leaves a session cookie, and the next write then needs its CSRF header → use a fresh request context per sign-in; the test-only session route emits no sign-in event, so an e2e that needs a sign-in row signs in for real.
- [P10-05 review / a bound the database cannot hold is a 500, not a 400] `from=0000-01-01T00:00:00Z` (Postgres has no year 0) and a `targetId` with a NUL (text cannot hold one) passed the Zod schema and reached SQL as 500s; the date input in the web app also let a person type year `0099` (JavaScript read it as 1999) or `9999-12-31` (its next day is year 10000 and made the request schema throw inside an effect) → give every filter a schema that matches what the column can hold (years 0001 to 9999, an id charset), probe the extremes through the real route, and build web dates with `setFullYear` and a check on the ISO result.
- [P10-05 review / one transaction, many subscribers: a row the table refuses poisons everyone] All plugin subscribers of an event run in one transaction (`host.dispatch`), so the audit subscriber failing on a CHECK (a 300-character team name copied unclipped into `team_name`, or a clip that split an emoji and left a lone surrogate the JSON could not carry) rolled back and retried every other plugin's work for that event → clip every copied text to its column, surrogate-safe, and test the awkward value through the real subscriber, not only the pure mapper.
- [P10-05 review / a path is content] The mappers copied a request path (`capability.denied`) and a proposal path as labels, and the canary tests never put a canary in them, so "content-blind" held only for the fields the tests chose → a path shows whole only for the guarded definitions (`TEAM.md`, `bots/`, `skills/`, `routines/`), otherwise its top folder; when a content-blind claim is tested with canaries, every field the mapper reads that is not an id or enum needs one.
- [P10-05 review / a mark kept as a JavaScript Date loses microseconds] The memory sweep saved its position with `toISOString()` (milliseconds), so the position sat before its own line and re-read it on every tick (harmless only because the insert is idempotent) → keep a position as the database's own `created_at::text`.
- [P10-05 review / an expensive route needs a gate besides the per-minute limit] 30 exports a minute of up to 20,000 rows each could be queued at once → one export at a time per workspace (429 for the second), released in `finally`.
- [P10-14 / a public route counted per actor is not counted per address] The server keyed every limit on the signed-in actor and fell back to the client address only for anonymous callers, so sign-in, reset and invitation routes handed an attacker holding any one real session a second, separate counter → a route where nobody is signed in sets `rateLimitBy: 'address'`; the address is `req.ip` (trusted `X-Forwarded-For` hops only), never the header.
- [P10-14 / a limit nobody listed is a limit nobody tests] Sign-out, sign-out-everywhere and session revoke had no limit at all, and three public routes had 20 to 120 requests a minute for a link that a person opens once → one list (`ABUSE_ROUTES`) that the routes read, the burst test walks and the docs table must match; add a credential route there first.
- [P10-14 / a lock that can be tripped from anywhere can shut an owner out] A per-account lock keyed on the email alone lets a stranger lock out an admin → the account lock is higher (25) than the email + address lock (5), temporary (15 minutes), not extended by attempts made while it holds, and a completed password reset lifts it; test all four.
- [P10-14 / the route limit answers before the lock] A lock that trips at the same count as the route's per-minute limit cannot be seen from outside inside one minute (the limiter's 429 wins) → test a lock by moving the limiter's clock past its window (the test server takes a `limiter` built on the test clock) and by checking the message and a `retry-after` of minutes, not seconds.
- [P10-14 / in-flight counting is wrong for a busy sender] `Lockout.begin` counts attempts still being verified as failures-in-waiting; for the webhook door that would lock a sender posting 30 good calls at once → the door uses `check` + `fail` (failures only); use `begin` only where each attempt is a guess.
- [P10-14 / a hop count trusts what a direct caller writes] With `MANYTHREADS_TRUST_PROXY=2` the server believes the second entry from the right; a caller that reaches the server Service directly (no proxies append) can write one entry and choose its own address → keep the server Service unreachable from untrusted pods; the ingress only routes to the web pod.
- [P10-14 / a tighter limit breaks old tests that sign in a lot from one address] Test files that sign in more than 20 times a minute from the single test address (identity, OIDC) started to answer 429 once sign-in dropped from 60 to 20 a minute; the limit is proven in `packages/server/test/abuse.test.ts`, so such files start the server with `limiter: relaxedRateLimiter()` (test-utils) instead of weakening the route. A new cooldown that reads the injected clock (one reset mail per account per minute) needs the test to advance that same clock.
- [P10-14 review / a hop count is only as good as the address the first proxy saw] Every limit and lock keys on `req.ip`, and the "2 hops (3 behind Cloudflare)" setting was only checked with injected headers; on the live k3s node Traefik's Service is `externalTrafficPolicy: Cluster`, which rewrites every source address to the bridge `10.42.0.1`, so nginx in the web pod logged `X-Forwarded-For: 10.42.0.1` for requests that came through Cloudflare: all visitors shared ONE counter (30 sign-ins a minute for the whole company, one stranger lock for every webhook sender) → prove the derived address on the live ingress (the web pod's nginx access log prints the incoming `X-Forwarded-For` as its last field), not in a unit test; behind Cloudflare the chart sets `MANYTHREADS_CLIENT_IP_HEADER=cf-connecting-ip`, elsewhere set `externalTrafficPolicy: Local`.
- [P10-14 review / a lock keyed by address must not refuse a right secret] The webhook door answered 429 to every call from a locked address, so one sender posting to a rotated address (30 pushes in five minutes) silenced every other webhook that shares its address (GitHub's, a company's CI) → the lock changes only the answer of a call that fails anyway (429 instead of 404); a right secret always passes.
- [P10-14 review / an IPv6 address is not one client] Keying on the full IPv6 address gives a guesser a fresh counter per request (a /64 holds 2^64 of them) → key on `addressKey(ip)` (IPv6 as its /64, IPv4-mapped as IPv4) wherever a limit or lock is keyed on an address.
- [P10-14 review / a limit below the morning rush is a lockout for an office] 20 sign-ins and 20 SSO starts a minute per address would 429 a 40-person office signing in after the 30-minute idle timeout → 30 a minute; the lockouts, not the per-minute number, stop guessing.

- **A test that counts rows right after a job-driven setup must wait for the jobs first (found in CI on P10-14).** `evals.test.ts` "the same commit delivered twice" read the first run row before the first delivery had finished; under the full-suite load a second row landed and the count was 2. Call `jobsIdle()` before taking the "first" snapshot of anything a job writes.
- [P10-04 hook reads a table] a pre_egress hook that started reading the policy tables broke every test that used a fake transaction (its `query` rejected) or a database built without the plugin's migration, and failed closed → give every test database that loads the hook the plugin's migration source (`packages/test-utils/src/db.ts`), and make fake transactions resolve `{ rows: [] }`.
- [P10-04 span scan] the first span finder kept the match marks in `const` arrays while the live thread lists swapped each step, so marks and threads drifted apart → anything stepped alongside a Thompson thread list must swap with it; and a rescan after every match can go quadratic, so cap the work and turn the rest into one span.
- [P10-04 new test imports] a plugin whose tests newly import `pg` or `@manythreads/test-utils` needs them in its own `devDependencies` (then `pnpm install --offline`), or typecheck fails with "Cannot find module" → add them with the first RLS test.
- [P10-04 secrets in tests] a key-shaped string in a test file is still a committed secret to a scanner → build it from two halves at run time.
- [P10-04 review / linear per text is not bounded per call] The custom-rule matcher was linear in one text, but 20 rules over a 2 MB page write (class-start patterns try every position) cost seconds of synchronous time, and a call with thousands of small values multiplies it → give a user-supplied automaton a work budget shared by the whole call (steps, not time), skip positions that cannot start a match for free, and when it runs out fail closed with a named finding (`scan-limit`) that always blocks; a finding name missing from the ordered rule list silently turns into "allow", so add every system finding name to it.
- [P10-04 review / a pattern is not text with its invisible parts] A zero-width space or joiner pushed into a key, card number or private-key header defeated every detector while reading the same to a person → fold format characters (`\p{Cf}` and the blank letters) out before scanning and map the offsets back so the redaction covers them; test with the splitter inside, at the start and after an emoji.
- [P10-04 review / names are text too] The gateway read only values, so `{"<key>": 1}` carried a secret out in a field name, and a name cannot be redacted without changing the call → read the distinct names (one hook call, joined by newlines) and refuse the call on a finding.
- [P10-04 review / a count in the route is not a cap] "At most 20 rules" lived only in a route's count, so two requests at once or any other writer passed it → back every cap with a BEFORE INSERT trigger that takes a per-workspace advisory lock; a row's workspace and its parent's workspace must also agree in the WITH CHECK (`team_egress` accepted a foreign team under the writer's workspace).
- [P10-04 review / a screen must not promise what nothing enforces] The host-list text said a switched-on team "can reach only the hosts below" while no sender passes the team yet → write the copy for what happens today and say what is not wired; reserved names shown elsewhere (`api-key`, `scan-limit`, `unnamed`) cannot be rule names, or a rule looks like a built-in in the audit log.
- [P10-01 HttpError codes] `HttpError` takes a closed set of codes (`validation_failed`, `not_found`, `forbidden`, `rate_limited`, `conflict`, ...); a custom code (`not_testable`, `not_launched`) fails typecheck → use `conflict` for "not allowed in this state" and put the reason in the message.
- [P10-01 a checklist step must be a fact] The old Models step turned green when a provider existed, so a mistyped key passed → a step is done only from something the system observed after the last change (a test call that answered, a team, Brain on it); a button may re-read facts but never tick a step, a stored result must say whether it is still current (compare its start time with the alias's and provider's update time), and a fact always beats a recorded "set aside".
- [P10-01 test the thing, not its rescuer] A test call through an alias with fallbacks reports whichever alias answered, so a broken alias looked fine → send the call with fallbacks switched off and say which model answered.
- [P10-01 tag results by the key that made them] `useQuery` keeps the previous `ok` data while a new key loads, so the checklist briefly showed the first team's facts for the second team → carry the key in the result and treat a mismatched key as loading.
- [P10-01 events are append-only] A test that deleted `app.events` rows to count from zero failed on the table's rules → note the time before the act and count events since then.
- [P10-01 a new sidebar item moves every baseline] A card in the rail changes every screen picture for admins → the e2e test servers write the item as already put away (`seedFinishedSetup`, not `createPersonas`), and the specs that want a fresh install delete that row.
- [P10-01 enum order] A CHECK and its `z.enum` must list values in the same order; removing a cause from one and not the other (or leaving a word that nothing produces) fails `schema-enums` and leaves a dead branch → add and remove a word in the migration, the shared enum, the mapper and the screen in one pass.
- [P10-01 the browser assembles what the plugin may not read] A plugin cannot read another plugin's tables, so a cross-plugin checklist reads each owner's own route in the browser and the server stores only decisions; say so in the docs, because the server can then check a launch exists but not that the message was posted.
- [P10-01 changing a provider's key was refused] `PATCH /providers/:id` with `apiKey` deleted the old secret before the row stopped pointing at it, so the foreign key answered 409 and a wrong key could never be fixed from the screen; no test rotated a key → write the new secret, update the row, then delete the old one, and give every "replace a stored value" route a test that does exactly that.
- [P10-01 RLS tests] an UPDATE or DELETE by a role with no such privilege raises 42501; it does not return zero rows. Do not run it bare as a "this is allowed" setup step.
- [P10-01 review / a test that runs before the push reaches the proxy] A save is applied to the model proxy by a background job a moment later, so a test started right after a save went through the OLD key or model and read as current for the new one → the test route refuses (409, nothing sent) for ten seconds after an alias or provider save; tests and e2e move `updated_at` into the past instead of waiting.
- [P10-01 review / a seed switch inside a shared seeder reaches production] `createPersonas` also runs in `pnpm seed --demo` on the live site, so "start the seeded world as already set up" would have hidden the checklist from the one place the owner looks at it → test-only world switches live in a separate function that only the e2e servers call.
- [P10-01 review / a step must check what the thing it unlocks needs] The Models step turned done when any alias answered, while the shipped Brain asks for `smart` and `fast` → derive a step from the aliases the next step actually uses.
- [P10-01 review / a message must not promise a log nothing writes] The "other" cause said "the server log has the detail" while the test route logs nothing (by design: provider text must not reach logs) → write only what the code does.
- [P8-07 a new reference to a stored blob must reach the blob GC] The files blob GC treats a blob as referenced only when a `files` row holds its key, so the originals of knowledge documents (same store) would have been reaped as soon as GC was switched on → whoever stores a blob outside `files` adds its table to the GC's "referenced" check in the same change, with a test that runs the GC against an aged blob only that table points at.
- [P8-07 pdfjs] `getDocument(...)` returns a loading task, and `destroy()` lives on the task, not on the document (`doc.destroy is not a function`); `isEvalSupported` is not in the typings of the legacy build. Read PDFs in a worker thread with a host-side timeout, never on the request path, and pin `pdfjs-dist` to the exact version the web client already resolves so the install needs no download (`pnpm install --prefer-offline`).
- [P8-07 row types for `tx.query`] `PluginTx.query<R extends Record<string, unknown>>` rejects an `interface` row type ("Index signature is missing") → declare row shapes with `type` aliases.
- [P8-08 a coverage floor must fit how people ask] The first search kept a passage only if it held half of the question's words; "how long do refunds take" (words: long, refund, take) found nothing in a refund policy → the floor is a third, the score still rises with coverage, and a test asks the question the way a person would, not with the document's own words.
- [P8-08 search only the current version] Passages of older versions stay for citations; a search that does not join `c.version = d.version` returns the old text beside the new → every search joins on the current version, and an old passage is reached only by its id and says the source changed since.
- [P8-08 a bot cannot read its own row or its run log] `app.bots` and `run_source_log` are not readable by the bot actor, so anything the tool needs from them (its `knowledge` list, whether it read a passage yet) goes through a security definer function that answers only for `app.run_id()` of a live run; a test that calls such a function must set both `app.run_id` and `app.actor_id`, or the function sees nobody.
- [P8-07 repo commits do not roll back] The converted copy goes through the repo writer, which commits outside the database transaction; a failed write after the index was made must not undo the index → write the repo copy last, inside a savepoint wrapper, and put a refusal in the document's `reason` instead of failing the upload.
- [P8-07 same bytes, same result] Re-uploading identical bytes must change nothing (no version bump, no event, no new passages); compare the sha256 against the indexed document, and a failed document is retried, not skipped.
- [P8-32 lint] A literal byte order mark inside a regex trips `no-irregular-whitespace` → write `﻿`.
- [P8-07 a cap test must not assume names] The RLS test for the 20-base cap looked up `fill-0`, which does not exist when the team already holds other bases → derive the slug from the count it started from.
- [P8-07 review / buffers are not heap] A worker thread's `resourceLimits.maxOldGenerationSizeMb` does not count the `ArrayBuffer`s a PDF's compressed streams unpack into: a 200 KB PDF with a 200 MB flate stream grew the server by about 750 MB (the live pod limit is 1536 Mi, steady 760). → the host watches the process's resident memory while a file is read and stops the thread past a growth limit (`MANYTHREADS_KNOWLEDGE_EXTRACT_MB`, 500); any other decoder of hostile compressed input needs the same watch, with a test that builds the bomb.
- [P8-07 review / definer owner] A new `SECURITY DEFINER` function is owned by whoever ran the migration (`manythreads_owner`, a superuser), not by `manythreads_system` → every definer function ends with `GRANT CREATE ON SCHEMA app TO manythreads_system; ALTER FUNCTION … OWNER TO manythreads_system; REVOKE CREATE …`, and a test reads `pg_proc` for owner, search path and grants.
- [P8-07 review / a copy must not overwrite a teammate's file] The converted copy of an upload was written to `knowledge/<base>/<slug>.md` with last-write-wins, so a Markdown file someone had just put there (not yet read by Re-sync) was replaced silently → ask the repo for the folder first and take the next free `<slug>-2.md`; a document keeps the path it was given.
- [P8-07 review / NUL from a PDF] PDF text can carry control characters; a NUL makes Postgres refuse the passage (a 500 on upload instead of a plain reason) → the PDF extractor turns control characters into blanks like the text extractor does.
- [P8-07 review / a limit in the database needs one in the schema] `bots.knowledge` has a CHECK of at most 50 entries, but BOT.md allowed any number, so a longer list would throw in the loader and the outbox would retry it forever → the frontmatter schema says `max(50)` with a plain message (and the JSON schema and its snapshot move with it).
- [item 7 / self-host docs] The chart's default `postgres.image` and `domain`, and `seed.demo: true`, only suit the project's own devbox (private registry, project host, demo workspace that blocks the first-admin link); a stranger following docs/deploy.md would stall → a self-host page must name every value a stranger overrides (`postgres.image`, `domain`, `seed.demo=false`, `mail.*`, `publicUrl` for plain HTTP) and say plainly that `tools/devbox/deploy.sh` is not the install path.
- [item 7 / docs claim a path that is not built] architecture.md called docker compose a self-host path while the compose file itself says it comes in phase 10 → before writing "supported install", check the file's own header and STATUS.md; list unbuilt paths under "not ready yet".
- [item 7 / UI words] A guide step written from memory ("profile menu → Do not disturb") named a screen that does not exist in `clients/web/src` (only the presence store does) → grep the web client for every button and tab name before putting it in a guide; leave out what you cannot find.
- [item 7 / self-host docs review] The first draft quoted code and chart behaviour from memory: it said the compose file "says so in its header" (the note is a comment near the end), named a section of docs/deploy.md that does not exist, said a Teams invite box invites "the lead" (it invites members), put the DLP preset on the Data protection page (it is under LLM > Guard), and described the team egress list backwards → for each sentence about a screen, header or section, open the file and copy the exact label or heading; a "not enforced" note must say which side is missing.
- [item 7 / a guide must not hide what the install lacks] The install page left out what a stranger needs before the first useful day: Hindsight (team memory), the pod images for managed Hermes pods, `backup.databases` with Hindsight, the Cloudflare secret, and the k3s Traefik `externalTrafficPolicy` that makes every visitor share one lockout counter outside Cloudflare → walk deploy.sh and values-live.yaml line by line and list every step the script does that the page does not.
- [P14-15 brief] The task said the sidebar had no unread bold label or count, but `Sidebar.tsx` and `DirectMessages.tsx` already drew both from the read state and `quick-switcher.spec.ts` already asserted the class → read the screen's code and the neighbouring specs before building from a docs-review gap; the real gap was that a conversation set to "nothing" (P14-09) still showed loud, so the work was `unreadLook` (grey count, no bold) plus a spec that proves the whole path.
- [P14-15 web] A button that always ends in 403 for most people is clutter, and the web client had no "can create a channel" fact → mirror the server's rule from facts the client already holds (`team.myRole === 'lead'` or a workspace admin, team not archived) in one tested function (`canCreateChannel`), keep the server as the only authority, and give every closed-set refusal (409 taken or archived, 400, 403, 404, 429) one plain sentence in `createErrorText`.
- [P14-15 test] A fixture for a shared Zod response copied from memory (`createdBy`, `updatedAt`) failed with "response this app does not understand" → build response fixtures from the schema in `packages/shared` (`Channel` has `dmKey`, `botId`, no `createdBy`).
- [P14-15 review] Three small slips found by a second reader: a hint class `.nc-bad` in `discover.css` lost to `.dlg-hint` in `files.css` (same specificity, later file), so the bad-name hint stayed grey; `createErrorText` told "team is archived" for any 409 whose text held the word "archived" (a taken name like `archived-docs`); and a directory refresh asked for while another was running was dropped, so a just-made channel could be missing from the sidebar until the next push → give a state class the compound selector (`.dlg-hint.nc-bad`), match the whole server phrase, and run one more fetch when a refresh is asked for during a fetch.
- [P14-06 brief] The plan row lists ten built-ins and bot-declared commands arriving as a `command` trigger, but `BOT.md` has no `commands` field, no `command` trigger exists, and most of the listed commands need rules that are not built (polls, Do not disturb, reminders) → build only the commands that end in an existing route or an ordinary message, say in the docs which are left and why, and do not invent a bot contract on the client.
- [P14-06 escape rule] "`//` escapes a slash" taken literally would eat one slash of every pasted code comment (`// TODO`) → the escape applies only when `//` is followed by a letter (`//ask`), anything else is posted exactly as typed; the same test file holds both cases.
- [P14-06 a command must not swallow text] A message that fails a command's own check (no bot, no title, files attached) must stay in the box with one sentence, and a message that is not a known command (`/xyz`, `/etc/hosts`, a leading space) goes out untouched → `parseSlash` returns plain text for everything but a known word at character 0, and `planSlash` answers `say` (nothing sent, nothing cleared) instead of an error.
- [P14-06 one menu, two sources] The composer's `@`/`#` picker and the slash menu share one list, so a text-based trigger can match inside a slash line (`/ask @co`) → the slash context wins when it exists, every item carries the range it replaces, and "dismissed with Esc" is remembered by the word's start so it resets when the box is emptied.
- [P14-06 stylelint] Generic descendant selectors in a shared stylesheet (`.cnotice a`, `.shelp li`, `.x span`) collide with older rules (`no-descending-specificity`) → give new parts their own class (`.pk-name`, `.shelp-row`) instead of styling bare elements.
- [P14-06 test world] The only guest in the seeds (Lena) has a read grant and gets no message box, so a disabled `/task` cannot be seen in a browser → the permission table is covered by unit tests of `availability`, and the browser spec checks that she has no box and that the server answers her a card with 403/404.
- [P14-06 Enter on a finished word] With the menu open, Enter always picked the highlighted row, so `/ask coder` + Enter inserted "coder " instead of saying a question was missing, and `/help` needed two Enters → when the word typed already equals the highlighted row (and nothing needs completing), Enter falls through to send; a command that needs words is still completed first. The browser spec drives exactly these keys.
- [P7-11 who says a run is a rehearsal] A "dry run" flag that travels with the request (a body field, a tool argument, a message tag) can be forged or dropped → the Test route files the question itself in a table the router reads, the run job writes the flag into the run's own row, and the gateway reads it back from that row on every call; the request body is strict and carries the question only.
- [P7-11 what a rehearsal cannot hold back] The gateway is the only door a rehearsal controls; a Hermes process with `capabilities.runtime` (shell, files, browser) acts outside it, and on a gateway or pod profile those tools are per profile, not per run → refuse the test for such a bot, in the route and again in the run job, and say which grant is the reason, instead of promising "nothing is done".
- [P7-11 unknown tool means write] A hold-back that lists the tools it blocks lets a tool added later through → decide by "not a read, not a stub, not the reply" (`rehearsalSafe` is opt-in, only `answer.post`), so a new or undescribed tool is held back until someone says otherwise.
- [P7-11 approvals in a rehearsal] A grant with needs-approval filed a real approval for a call that is never going to be made → the hold-back comes before the approval branch, files nothing, and records `would_wait` so the page can say a real call would have waited.
- [P7-11 notes added twice] A run held for an approval stores the payload it was given and is parsed and prepared again on resume, so a prompt prefix added in the job doubles → make the prefix function idempotent (skip when the prompt already starts with it) and test it.
- [P7-11 review / "running" starts at the question] The "one test at a time" check read `bot_runs`, but the router opens the run later, behind the bot's queue, so two presses in that gap both went through → a test counts as running from the moment its question is filed (a recent `rehearsal_questions` row with no run yet), and an advisory lock per person and bot makes two simultaneous presses take turns; the test presses twice at once with another lead, because the route's ten-a-minute limit counts refused requests too.
- [P7-11 review / the safe tool still has a gate] Marking the reply (`answer.post`) as safe skipped the hold-back, so a reply whose grant needs approval fell into the approval branch and filed a real card from a test → a "safe" tool passes only when it would go straight out; anything that would wait for a person is held back like a write.
- [P14-04 brief] The task said "extend an existing plugin or a tiny one only if conventions say so", but PLAN P14-04 names `plugins/reminders` and Appendix A.11 names the tables → read the plan row and the appendix for the plugin's name before choosing a home; the reminders plugin only emits an event and the notifications plugin turns it into a row, so neither imports the other.
- [P14-04 notification key] A reminder on a message that also mentions the person would have collided with the mention on the unique key `(person_id, ref_type, ref_id)` (one row per message), and the upsert keeps the stronger kind → a reminder gets its own `ref_type = 'reminder'` (with the message as `ref_id`), so both rows exist, the open-message path still works, and the deleted-message cleanup removes both kinds.
- [P14-04 re-arm only when read] A plain upsert for a reminder row would turn a replayed event into a second alert → the conflict branch updates only where the earlier reminder was already read (`WHERE n.read_at IS NOT NULL`), so a replay changes nothing and announces nothing.
- [P14-04 a cap trigger and ON CONFLICT] A BEFORE INSERT trigger that refuses the 201st row also fires for an `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` that would only update, so a person with a full list could not change a reminder → the route reads first and updates an existing item, inserts only a new one.
- [P14-04 no rowCount] `tx.query` returns `{ rows }` only, so `result.rowCount` is a type error and would be undefined → write `RETURNING id` and test `rows.length`.
- [P14-04 do not hide what was lost] Filtering the list by what the caller can read would make a deleted message or a lost channel vanish silently, and a person would wonder where the item went → the item keeps its id and times and the message part is `null` ("This message is no longer available"); the text, author and channel name are never sent, and a test greps the whole response for the canary words.
- [P14-04 times and zones] "Tomorrow morning" computed on the server would need the person's zone, which the server does not know → the client computes every preset in the browser's zone and sends an exact instant; the server only checks the window (a minute of slack in the past, a year ahead), and one shared function (`checkRemindAt`) is used by both sides so the custom-time field says the same words.
- [P14-04 reminders ignore levels] A reminder is something the person asked for, so the P14-09 channel level (even "nothing"), the per-kind switch and a muted channel must not swallow it; Do not disturb still holds it, because that is the one setting meant to stop all interruptions → `levelAllows` is true for `reminder` at every level, and tests cover level "nothing" and Do not disturb with its release.
- [P14-04 test / deleting a channel] A cascade test that deleted the channel as the system role passed 0 rows without an error (nothing in the app may delete a channel, so the system role has no such policy) and the "items go with the channel" check failed on the leftover row → delete the channel as the owner role (`testClient` on `db.ownerUrl`) and assert `rowCount` is 1 before checking the cascade.
- [P14-04 schema-compat] Widening `NotificationKind` and `NotificationRefType` changed six existing JSON snapshots (inbox response, push, entity, created event) besides the new ones → run `pnpm test:schema-compat` after a shared enum change, read the diff of the old files (only the added value may appear), and leave the written files for the commit.
- [P14-04 review / a screen's keys outlive its tab] The Threads inbox keeps one global key handler (j, k, e) for the thread tabs; on the Later tab `selected` is a saved item's id, so pressing e would have marked a thread with that id as read (a bogus request) → a tab that reuses the screen's `selected` must switch the thread handler off (`tab === 'later'`), and a link to a tab whose plugin is absent (`?tab=later` on a server without reminders) falls back to the first tab instead of showing an empty, unhighlighted one.
- [P14-01b brief] The docs said the Do not disturb backend existed and "the web app has no screen", and the client already held `presence/store.ts`, `format.ts` and `hooks.ts` that nothing imported (the heartbeat is never started, so no card is ever fetched) → before building, grep who imports the existing client module; the store was reused, but only for the person's own settings (`loadMe`, no heartbeat and no card list) so the screen did not switch on a 30-second poll of everyone's cards that no screen reads yet.
- [P14-01b a schedule has no zone of its own] The server reads a weekly schedule in the saved time zone, or in UTC when none is saved, so a person who types "22:00 to 07:00" in Karachi without a saved zone gets quiet hours five hours off → the schedule editor shows the zone it will be read in, and saving the schedule first saves the device's zone when none is saved; the unit test checks the rows against the server's own `dndState` for a Friday night in Karachi.
- [P14-01b end of a pause] A pause that ends by the clock sends no event to the browser, so a moon on the bell would stay until the next poll → the client works the state out itself from the saved settings with the shared `dndState` and wakes at the end time (`useMyDnd`), and the minute-by-minute read only catches changes made on another device.
- [P14-01b older read, newer save] A read of the person's settings that was on its way while they pressed "Resume now" came back after the save and put the pause back on the screen → the store counts the person's own writes and drops a read that started before the latest one; the unit test resolves a slow read after a save.
- [P14-01b quiet hours are not a pause] Offering "Resume now" while a weekly quiet period runs would need a "skip tonight" the settings do not have (the only way is to switch the schedule off, which also changes tomorrow) → the paused sentence says which of three it is (a hand-set Do not disturb, a pause, quiet hours); only the first two get "Resume now", quiet hours get "Change" to the schedule.
- [P14-01b e2e: shared persona state] Do not disturb is per person and global, so a spec that pauses Nadia on the shared e2e origin would silence her in every other spec running at the same time → the spec starts its own stack (`startStack`), and every test puts the settings back in a `finally`.
- [P14-01b live region] An `aria-live` region inside the sidebar is not announced on a phone, where the closed drawer is `inert`, and two regions (sidebar and bell) would announce the same sentence twice → one polite region in the shell outside the rail (`DndLive`), the visible strip and bell carry no live attribute, and the account page, which has no shell, has its own.
- [P14-01b Esc in a nested picker] The composer's emoji picker closes itself on Esc but the dialog's capture-phase Esc handler runs first and would close the whole dialog → the status dialog offers typed emoji, ten quick ones and ready-made statuses instead of the picker.
- [P14-01b review / a shared clock rule is not free per render] `useMyDnd` called the shared `dndState` on every render of five components; for a schedule that never stops (every hour of every day) it scans a whole week a minute at a time (about 75 ms a call) → work the state out once per clock tick with `useMemo`, and start no wake-up timer for a person whose settings are absent. Also: a blanket "any 4xx shows the server's words" branch placed before the 429 branch made the "too many changes" sentence unreachable → order the specific status first; and a settings section that draws a "Loading…" heading before the server has answered flashes on a server without the plugin → draw nothing until the first answer.
- [P10-11 desktop] A Tauri app command with no entry in `build.rs`'s app manifest is open to every page that has any capability, including the live server's page → list the commands in `AppManifest::commands` and grant them only in a `local: true` capability (checked: the server's page gets "not allowed" for `connect_server`). Remote pages can still call plugins through Tauri's `postMessage` fallback although the server's CSP blocks `ipc:` fetch. Also: never run `git stash` in a shared tree (done once by mistake, popped at once).
- [P10-11 review / desktop] (1) The opener plugin's default init script swallows every `target="_blank"` click and asks the app to open it; the server's page may not, so all external links did nothing → build it with `open_js_links_on_click(false)` and let `on_new_window` handle them. (2) Sending every other origin to the system browser broke OIDC sign-in (the server redirects to Google/Microsoft and back) → the window follows the provider only between the server's `/api/auth/oidc/<id>/start` and its next page (`track_sign_in`). (3) A package script called `build` runs in the root `pnpm build` → name toolchain-heavy scripts `bundle`. (4) Grant only the plugin commands the page really calls (three notification ones), not `notification:default`.
- [P14-01b e2e / time of day and a shared live region] Four specs of the full CI run failed only because the run happened after 23:00 or because the shell gained a permanent `role="status"` region (`dnd-live`): a 30-minute pause started at 23:3x reads "paused until tomorrow 00:0x", "In 1 hour" reads "Tomorrow at 00:36", a one-hour status "Clears tomorrow 00:45", and an unscoped `page.getByRole('status')` now matches two elements → an e2e spec must not assume the time of day: accept an optional `(tomorrow )?` (and "Today|Tomorrow", "at midnight") wherever a text names a clock time that is "now plus something"; and scope `getByRole('status')` / `getByRole('alert')` to the screen under test (`getByTestId('bot-page').getByRole('status')`) because the shell keeps live regions of its own. Unit tests use fixed local dates, so they already hold in any zone (checked under UTC, New York, Karachi, Lord Howe, Chatham, Cairo).
- [Install-fresh / first install on an empty cluster] Wiping k3s and running `tools/devbox/deploy.sh` hit two gaps: (1) the script pushes the database image to the in-cluster registry, which only the chart creates, so the push failed on a cluster without it; (2) `helm --wait` also waits for every claim to be Bound and the backups claim (WaitForFirstConsumer) stays Pending until the nightly job runs, so the install ran to its 10-minute timeout and the post-install seed hook never started, leaving a server with no workspace → the script now starts the registry alone from the chart's own template (objects labelled as the release's, so the install adopts them) and, with no release yet, installs without `--wait` and waits for the server and web rollouts itself. When you start a seed Job by hand from `helm template`, pass `-n manythreads`: the rendered object has no namespace and lands in `default`.
- [P10-11 desktop / alerts when minimised] WebView2 does not change `document.visibilityState` or `hasFocus()` when the window is minimised or another app is in front (still "visible" and true), so `raiseAlert` never fired for a minimised app; and CDP `Browser.setWindowBounds` does not minimise the Tauri window, so the old test only passed through a `hasFocus` override → the shell sets `window.__manythreadsDesktopFocused` from `WindowEvent::Focused` and after each page load, the web client prefers it inside the app, and the test minimises the real window with Win32 `ShowWindow` (`setWindowState` in `e2e/desktop/shell.ts`) and overrides nothing.
- [P10-09 / upgrades before the first nightly backup] The first-install fix dropped `helm --wait` only when no release existed, but the `manythreads-backups` claim stays Pending until the first nightly job for every upgrade too: the next deploy waited 10 minutes, ended "UPGRADE FAILED: context deadline exceeded" and left the release marked `failed` although the pods were fine → `deploy.sh` skips `--wait` whenever that claim is Pending (not only on a first install) and waits for the server and web itself. Check the release status (`helm list`) after a deploy, not just the smoke test.
- [bot roster / "idle" was never measured] The roster and the sidebar drew a green "idle" dot for every bot whose definition was loaded and on, so a bot with no agent behind it (no pod, no connection) looked ready → the state comes from `botAvailability`: "ready" (green) only when the agent connection passed its last check (or a rules bot), "agent not set up" / "agent not working" when it is missing or failed, and "active" with an open ring where the page cannot tell. A status that nothing measures must not look like a health signal.
- [provision-host-bots / profile edits lost, kept token unchecked] The first version of `tools/devbox/provision-host-bots.mjs` rewrote `config.yaml` and the whole `.env` on every run (dropping a hand edit or any extra variable), trusted a token that was merely present in the profile, wrote files in place (the gateway serves a half-built profile directory at once) and committed the placement before the profile existed → the profile is built as `.tmp-<name>-<pid>` (not a servable name) and renamed, files go through a temp file and a rename, `.env` is merged (three managed keys only), a `config.yaml` without the script's header is left alone unless `--force`, a kept token is probed at `/mcp` (401 means mint again), a mint whose write fails is revoked, and an existing bot's profile is written before its placement is committed.
- [P10 live host bots] Two team bots (support-responder, content-drafter) answered nothing: the live repo copy was the seeded "Placeholder definition" with no `capabilities.native`, so the loader compiled no grants, the closing reply was refused ("no grant for messages.post in the actor's allowlist"), and the failure notice went through the same broker door and was refused too — the person saw silence twice over. The templates were right; `provision-host-bots.mjs` kept the existing (placeholder) file. → the platform's own fixed failure/gate notices are not a model's call and no longer need the grant (a bot always says why it did not answer; new category `not_allowed`); the provision script replaces a placeholder with the template; after changing templates, check what the team repo really holds (`GET /api/teams/<team>/repo/blob?path=bots/<slug>/BOT.md`), never assume it matches.
- [P14-15 sidebar prefs / ties, enum list, default order] (1) Rows written in one statement share `created_at`, so "stars in the order they were added" came back ordered by id; order by `created_at, channel_id` on the server and sort in the client (tests sort too). (2) A new CHECK enum column (`sidebar_prefs.sort`) must also be added to `ENUM_COLUMNS` in `packages/kernel/test/schema-enums.test.ts` with its shared Zod enum, or that test fails. (3) Making "Recent activity" the default sort changes the default channel order for everyone and adds an options button to the first group heading: the visual baselines and e2e specs that assume the old order (`e2e/shell/sidebar-contract.spec.ts`, `new-channel.spec.ts`, specs masking `.nav > .grp`) must be re-run after such a change.
- [P14-15 review / full-list writes and one-shot stores] (1) A PUT that replaces a whole list (stars) loses another tab's change: send add/remove deltas, and refetch on focus and reconnect. (2) An external store needs a generation token (reset() while a request is in flight), a per-team pending count that cannot go below 0, and must drop a load that answers while a change is waiting; a failed load must be retryable, not "unavailable for the session"; do not blank the list while the first request runs. (3) A menu that lives inside the first group remounts when that group moves: keep it in a stable element. (4) Reordering the shown groups must refill only their own places in the full order, or groups that are not shown jump. (5) Moving a row between lists loses keyboard focus: restore it in an effect by a data- id, and never leave it on a button that just became disabled.

- A test that races two editors must not assert which one the server saw first. `packages/plugins/pages/test/live.test.ts` named Nadia the commit author while she and Rafi typed at the same moment; the server takes the first edit it sees, so CI failed twice on order alone. Assert that the author is one of the two and the other is the co-author.
- [P14-01c / the heartbeat was never started] `useHeartbeat` and the presence store's 30-second round of cards existed since P14-01 but nothing in the shell called them (only `useWatchMyPresence`, which reads the person's own settings), so every dot would have said Offline and docs/plugins/presence.md said "the web client does not send the heartbeat yet". Before drawing a state in the UI, check the thing that produces it runs in the live app (grep for the hook's callers), not only that the route and the store exist.
- [P14-01c / one store, many dots] A dot beside every message author and DM row must not draw again every 30 seconds just because the store replaced the whole card map. `usePresenceCard` reads one card by id (`useSyncExternalStore` with a selector) and the store keeps the old card object when a refreshed card is equal (`keepCards`), so only a person whose state changed re-renders. A dot also never calls the API itself: the shell fetches every card in one request. Bots and webhooks get no dot and no profile card (the roster lists people only, and a bot has no heartbeat).
- [P14-01c / a test that depends on locale data] `toLocaleDateString('en-GB', { weekday: 'short' ... })` gives `Mon 15 Jun` in one Node build and `Mon, 15 Jun` in another; assert with a pattern (`/^Back Mon,? 15 Jun$/`), not the literal text.
- [P14-01c review / "no card" is not "offline"] The first dots drew Offline whenever a person had no card, which is also what the store held before the first answer, after a failed fetch, and for every other person when the reader is a guest (the server serves a guest only their own card). A state drawn from "absent" needs a separate "loaded" flag (`cardsLoaded`), a staleness rule (3 failed rounds) and the reader's right to know. Also: a field that moves on every beat (`seenAt`) must not be part of the "did it change" comparison, or every active person redraws every 30 s; an async answer must carry a generation (`reset`) and a round number so a late older answer cannot replace a newer one or another person's data; and several tabs of one browser must not each say "away" (a short localStorage lease decides which tab may).
- [P14-01c review / accessibility of a clickable avatar] A decorative control must not be a focusable element under `aria-hidden` (a `button` with `tabIndex=-1` still is one): make it a plain wrapper with an `onClick` and keep the real button beside it. Render a dialog as a sibling of its opener, not a child: React events bubble through a portal to the opener's `onClick` and reopen it.
- [P14-15 live check / menus clipped, Esc order] (1) A menu anchored to a small button with a fixed 250 px width ran past the left edge of a 247 px sidebar, and the sidebar (`overflow: hidden`) cut its first letters: anchor a menu to the list it belongs to (left and right inset, `width: auto`, `max-width: calc(100% - 8px)`), never `100vw` or a pixel width, and check the box against the sidebar box in e2e. (2) Two `useDismiss` listeners (a drawer and a menu inside it) both sit on `document` in the capture phase, and the first registered wins: `useDismiss` now keeps a stack so Esc closes only the one opened last, and a menu inside the phone drawer closes when the drawer goes inert (MutationObserver on the `inert` attribute).
