(3) A child of an element with `pointer-events: none` inherits it: give the menu wrapper `pointer-events: auto`.
- [P14-01 CI red: later.spec phone "element was detached" / a row moved under the pointer] The presence dots were blamed (rows remounting when cards load) but a row's element and its action bar stay the same when cards arrive and refresh; the real cause was older: the channel drew its rows as soon as the messages came and put the "New" line in front of the first unread one only when the roster answered (the unread decision waits for it, P14-12), pushing every row down 36 px under the pointer. The hover action bar exists only while the pointer is over the row, so Playwright's click saw "not stable", then "detached", and there was no bar to wait for. A few more requests at start (presence cards, own settings, heartbeat) made the roster arrive after the rows often enough to fail one run in about fifteen → the rows are not drawn until the opening decision is made (`deciding` in `ChannelBody`, at most 2.5 s), so they are drawn where they stay; `e2e/channels/row-stability.spec.ts` holds the roster back and checks position and element identity, and the presence cards likewise. Also: a poll helper that asserts 200 (`StackApi.get`) aborts on the first 404 of a thing that is loaded a moment after a commit; poll with the raw request and treat other statuses as "not yet" (`evals/on-commit.spec.ts`).
- [P14-22 / Hermes keeps what it read when a profile was served] Edits to a bot's tools did not reach its Hermes until the gateway restarted. Measured 2026-11-01 (Hermes v0.21.5): `config.yaml` toolsets are read per request; `SOUL.md` is frozen per session (a thread with one turn keeps the old text); per-run `instructions` are applied every turn, also mid-thread; the MCP `tools/list` is read once at serve, and our endpoint says `listChanged: false`, so nothing pushes → never assume "Hermes re-reads its files". Put what must change live in `instructions`, and push tool changes with `notifications/tools/list_changed` (a `GET` event stream plus a session id works: Hermes re-lists in milliseconds, old and new threads both); a profile re-serve (`hermes -p <profile> gateway restart`) is the fallback. The pod sync's comment "prompts are re-read every turn" was wrong for `SOUL.md`.
- [P14-22 test hygiene] To test a toolset change I enabled `terminal` on a throwaway profile of the **host** gateway, then sent `/reload-mcp` as run input: the API server does not run slash commands, the model took it as a prompt and used the host's unsandboxed shell (it only `curl`ed public docs, but it could have done anything) → never enable a shell, file or browser toolset on a probe profile of the host gateway (flip `todo` against another harmless toolset instead), and never type a command as run input. Test restarts and reloads on a separate gateway with its own `HERMES_HOME` and loopback port, not on the live host service (a classifier correctly refused a restart of the live one). Also: `pkill -f <name>` matches your own shell when the name is in the command line → kill by pid from `ss -ltnp`.
- [P14-03 / a bot's `toPerson` was never enforced] The bot manifest accepted `handover.toPerson: [role:…]` and the docs described it, but nothing read it: only `mayTag` reached the `tasks.handoff` grant. Before relying on a manifest field, grep for its consumer. It is now compiled into the grant (`constraints.toPerson`) and resolved through the `role.groups` provider.
- [P14-03 / a tag that loses a handle collision is still a group] Dropping a colliding tag (`role:coder` when a person is called Coder) from the group list made `toPerson: [role:coder]` unresolvable. Keep every defined tag as a group with `mentionable: false`; only the `@` form is closed, and tag-name checks (hand-over, approvals) still resolve the people.
- [P14-03 / bots hold no role tags and resolve no groups] `app.role_members` holds people only, so a role mention can never wake a bot, and a bot's own messages reach no group (the same door as `@person`: `lookup_workspace_role()` is null for bots). Do not add a bot branch to the group expansion.
- [P14-03 / the `roles` table is hidden from bots] A provider that reads `app.roles` (or `team_role_tags` through it) returns nothing when the caller is a bot (`roles_select` wants a person's workspace role), so a bot's `handover.toPerson: [role:…]` resolved nobody while the same code worked for a person. Read tags through `app.teams_roster` (it answers a bot of the team) and test a provider as a bot as well as a person.
- [P14-03 review / an outside text cannot be assumed to reach groups] The brief and the first docs said alerts and webhooks reach role groups. They do not: the webhooks adapters break every `@` of outside text (`md.ts`), so only text the server writes itself (a system-role post under an actor of its own) can name a group. Check the real writer before documenting who can mention whom.
- [P14-03 review / a first name must not win a tag] The first collision rule closed a tag when any team member's first name equalled the word, so one new member called "Pager" killed `role:pager`. Only exact handles (email part, name slug) outrank a tag; and `matchHandle` has `exactOnly` so `@word` for a tag's word finds a person only by an exact handle.
- [P14-03 review / one event per person is a round trip each] A mention of a 500-person group emitted 500 events one by one (three statements each). Use `ctx.audit.emitMany` for any fan-out, and put a per-author budget (`mention-budget.ts`) in front of anything that can notify a whole team.
- [roster headers clipped at 1440 / person rows wide at 390] (1) The buttons of a block header ("Open", "Add person", "Invite") sat in `<span class="act">`; `.act` is also the 26x24 icon button of a message's hover toolbar (channels.css), so the group was 26 px wide and the card's `overflow: hidden` showed "Oper" and overlapped the next button. A shared class name in a header wrapper took another screen's fixed size: give a wrapper its own name (`.bh-act`, a wrapping flex group) and grep a class for every rule that sets it before reusing it. (2) `.mrow.person-row` (0,2,0) kept its five desktop columns in the phone block, which only set `.mrow` (0,1,0), so the tags cell was 640 px wide under the clip: a phone override of a row variant needs the variant's own selector. Both are asserted at 1440, 1024 and 390 px in `e2e/teams/roster-headers.spec.ts` (the seeded team has no template definition, so the spec injects one into the team response).

- [P14-05 / an exact-match test on a plugin's event list] `channels-events.test.ts` asserted the manifest emits exactly the ten known events, so adding an eleventh failed an old test that nothing in the new code touched. When adding an event to a plugin, grep its tests for the old list (`EMITTED`, the count in `docs/plugins/<name>.md`) and update them in the same change.
- [P14-05 / new shared schemas] Every new exported Zod schema and event needs its schema-compat snapshot (`env -u CI npx vitest run --project schema-compat` from the repo root writes them; from `packages/shared` it runs nothing) and, for a new SQL `CHECK` list, a line in `packages/kernel/test/schema-enums.test.ts`. Check that no existing snapshot was deleted or changed.
- [P14-05 / testing a rate-limited route in bulk] A test that needs 100 rows through a route that allows 60 a minute fails on the limit, not on the rule under test. Fill the rows with SQL as the system, and test the cap with that.
- [P14-05 / a class name used twice] I gave the dialog's text box and the strip's one-line preview the same class (`sch-text`), so the box styling (80 px minimum height, resize) would have hit the preview. Before writing the stylesheet, list the class names the components use and give each element its own.
- [P14-05 / a job that acts for a person] Do not post "as the author" with a system transaction and a hand-made check: open the transaction as the author (`ctx.db.as`) and call the same door the composer uses, so every rule (visibility, post permission, archived, thread root) is checked again at send time for free, and a refusal is just the door's own error. Do the post, the row delete and the event in one transaction so a crash cannot post twice or lose the message.
- [P14-05 / not run] The database tests for scheduled send (`test/scheduled-send.test.ts`, the RLS block, the events test) and the migration were written while CI held the dev database and were typechecked and linted but not run; run them first when the database is free.
- [P14-05 review / a draft the author can no longer reach] I made the list route ask for the right to read the channel, and drew the strip only in a readable channel: a draft refused because the author lost access (or a reply in a thread that cannot be opened) could not be seen, edited or dismissed, and still counted against the 100 limit. A "keep it, show the reason" design needs a door that does not depend on the thing that failed: list and cancel ask only for the person's own rows (the row-level policy already says that), and an account-wide "Not sent" list sits outside any channel.
- [P14-05 review / a job that does many things] A job that lists up to N rows in one run without a loop stalls everything past N; a loop without a cursor reads the same still-due rows again; a per-row step without its own try/catch lets one bad row block the rest; a long run inside one transaction pins a connection. Use a detached job (short transactions), a keyset cursor (`(send_at, id) >`), a time budget, one try/catch per row and `concurrency: 1`.
- [P14-05 review / "mark it failed" after a failure] The update that records a failure must say which version of the row it saw (`updated_at` read as text, so microseconds survive), or it overwrites an edit made in the meantime. Never store an empty reason: `reason || general sentence`.
- [P14-05 review / 429 is not a refusal] A "busy, try later" answer from a door a job calls is not the author's fault: wait for the `retry-after` it names without counting an attempt, and give up only after a long, stated time.
- [P14-05 review / events are read by more people than the row] The event log is readable by the team and its admins, so an event about a private draft must not carry its time or its refusal reason (the author reads those from their own row). Decide for each field of a new event who can read the log, not only whether it holds the text. Also keep the team id on the row: asking the channel for it fails exactly when the author lost access.
- [P14-05 review / a control that drops another control's setting] "Send later" beside Send ignored the "Also send to channel" tick in a thread, and Send and Send later could both fire while the request ran. When a second way to send is added to a composer, list every setting the first way reads (ticks, files, slash plans) and every in-flight guard, and either honour them or hide the new control.
- [P14-05 review / a time field with no seconds] An edit dialog that always sent its time moved a message by up to a minute even when only the text changed. Send a field only when the person changed it, comparing with what the record has.
- [P14-05 review / a read that is asked for while one is out] A store that returns the running request for a forced reload loses the push that caused the reload: mark the list dirty and read once more when the request ends.
- [P14-08 link preview / a count per trimmed character is quadratic, and the first one was in the shared parser] A link that is a run of 10,000 `)` took 0.5 s and 40,000 took 10 s: `trimUrl` in the shared `parseMarkup` recounted the brackets of the whole link (`url.slice` and `split`) for every character it trimmed, and my own `trimLink` copied the same shape. Found only by the `expectLinear` hostile case "unbalanced closing brackets" I wrote for the new plugin → keep running bracket counts (one pass, decrement as you trim), and add a hostile case for every loop that removes characters from the end of an input. The shared parser now has the test too.
- [P14-08 link preview / a query schema that transforms must parse its own output] The list route's query turned a comma string into an array (`transform` then `pipe`), the server validated the query once, and the handler parsed `req.query` again: the second parse got an array, failed, and every list call answered an error (seven DB-backed tests red on the first run). The files routes survived the same pattern only because `z.coerce.number()` accepts a number → a query schema used both by the server and again in a handler must accept its own output (`z.preprocess` that passes non-strings through), and the client sends the raw string without a request schema (the client's `call` would send the parsed array).
- [P14-08 link preview / an address check is a whole-string check] `isPublicAddress` first `trim()`med its input, so `" 8.8.8.8"`, `"8.8.8.8 "` and `"8.8.8.8\n"` were judged public while the caller would have connected to (or logged) the untrimmed text → a function that decides whether an address may be connected to takes the exact string it is given; the table test lists whitespace and newline variants next to the spellings that must not parse (octal, hex, integer, zone id).
- [P14-08 link preview / SSRF is a chain, test every link of it] The checks that matter are the ones after the first: a name with one private answer among public ones (refuse the whole name), a redirect to the metadata address or to a bare IP in another spelling, a redirect to another port or scheme, an `og:image` or oEmbed address on a private host (they come from the page, so they are as hostile as the link), a compressed answer that expands past the cap, one clock for the lookup, the redirects and the body → one function (`safeFetch`) is the only door; every hop goes through the same vetting and the caller's `check`; connect to the vetted IP with the name in `Host`/TLS server name (no second lookup); the tests use a fake resolver and a fake local server (`dial`), and assert that the server was never contacted for a refused hop.
- [P14-08 link preview / a cache keyed by address is a list of private links] The first sketch kept one cache row per address readable by the workspace; a listing of it would show every address pasted in a private channel → the cache table is system-only (no app grant, one `app.is_system()` policy), and the member-facing copy is the per-message card, readable with the message's channel. Same for the picture bytes: readable only through a card the caller can read, and served by a route that re-sniffs the bytes and sets `nosniff` plus a sandbox CSP, so a stored non-image can never be sent as one.
- [P14-08 link preview / never run a DB-backed test file by accident] Running `vitest --project unit packages/plugins/link-preview` (to run the DB-free files) also ran the DB-backed `link-preview-api.test.ts`, because every `test/*.test.ts` outside `test/rls`, `test/events` and `test/schema-compat` is in the `unit` project and the plugin's world test sits there → name the files when an agent has been told to stay off Postgres (`vitest run <file> <file>`), and keep DB-backed files obviously named so a glob cannot catch them.
- [P14-08 review / `dns.lookup` is not a network call] It runs on libuv's four shared threads and an abort signal cannot cancel it, so four dead names held every file read, password hash and zlib job of the whole server for the resolver's timeout while the "3 second" clock fired and changed nothing → resolve names with `dns.promises.Resolver` (c-ares; own sockets, a timeout and `tries: 1`), cap the lookups in flight, let a caller who gave up leave the line, remember a failed name for a short while; a test greps that the fetcher never imports `node:dns` for `lookup`.
- [P14-08 review / a database transaction around a network wait] The fetch job held a pooled transaction for up to 9 seconds per link, two at a time, with no fairness between workspaces → a `detached` job: a short transaction to look, none during the read, a short one to write; `serialKey` by workspace; a budget per author and per workspace; a breaker per host; one deadline for the whole job. A test makes the resolver assert that no transaction is open.
- [P14-08 review / write after slow work only if the world is the same] The card was inserted ~9 seconds after the message was read, with no check, so an edit or a delete in between still got a card → make the final write itself conditional (`INSERT ... SELECT ... FROM messages WHERE deleted_at IS NULL AND body = <the body that was read>`), not a read followed by a write.
- [P14-08 review / `recorded: true` with nobody recording] The caller said it records the refusal, but nothing did, so a refused link left no audit line → pass `recorded: false` so the guardrails plugins write their own, and give the caller's own state change (the settings save) its own event, mapper and fixture. Also pass `teamId`, or the team's own egress list is silently skipped.
- [P14-08 review / a rule that is only checked when something is created is not retroactive] A denied site kept its old cards, its kept reads and its pictures, and a cached read skipped the guard → on a rules change queue a sweep, filter by the rules at read time too (the list and the picture route), and re-run the chain for a cached read (only the network is saved).
- [P14-08 review / a prune that selects on the wrong thing deletes tombstones] `DELETE ... WHERE NOT (url = ANY(...))` also removed the row that records "the author removed this card", so an edit brought the card back → every prune of a table with tombstones says `removed_at IS NULL`; skip it altogether when the feature is off.
- [P14-08 review / reading a picture's size is not decoding it] Magic bytes say "PNG", not "4 bytes of header that claim 60000x60000"; the browser decodes it → read width and height from the header (PNG IHDR, GIF, JPEG SOF, WebP VP8/VP8L/VP8X, every AVIF `ispe`), refuse above a side and a pixel count, refuse when it cannot be read.
- [P14-08 review / a new background feature fires inside every old test] Posting a link in an existing test now queued a real fetch job → an environment switch (`MANYTHREADS_LINK_PREVIEW`) that is off under `NODE_ENV=test` and in the e2e stack, read at job time; the plugin's own tests turn it on for their run. Count the jobs a new subscriber adds to queues that other tests count (here none: the ops-health tests look at named queues).
- [P14-08 review / `deflate` is two formats] The standard says zlib, many servers send raw deflate; `createInflate` alone fails on those. Peek at the first two bytes (a zlib header is `0x?8` with a pair divisible by 31), and collect two bytes before deciding: the first network chunk can be one byte, which a test caught.
- [P14-07 / the brief said HEAD already had P14-03 and P14-05] They were uncommitted work in the main checkout; a worktree is plain HEAD and lacked migrations 0012 and 0013 and their code, so the merge back touches the same files (channels `index.ts`, `service.ts`, `rows.ts`, shared `events/registry.ts`, `ids.ts`, `api/channels/*`, the composer and message row). Check the commit history and that the files exist before trusting a brief about what is already in; and say in the report which hunks will collide.
- [P14-07 / anonymous votes leak by side doors] An anonymous poll is not anonymous if a count is derived from ballots a reader can see, if the event log names the voter and the time, or if an admin or the system role has a policy that reads ballots. Keep the totals on the poll row (updated by definer triggers), write no per-vote event for an anonymous poll, give the system role a policy for named ballots only, and test it with the admin, the author and the system role (the superuser database owner remains the one limit, say so in the doc).
- [P14-07 / a vote racing the close] Two voters and a closing job at once can let a vote in after the result was counted. Lock the poll row (`FOR UPDATE`) in the ballot trigger and in the close, and refuse a ballot once `closes_at` has passed even if the job has not run yet; do not rely on the job being on time.
- [P14-07 / adding a slash command breaks older specs] `e2e/channels/slash-commands.spec.ts` counted the menu (5 commands) and listed the `/help` usage; a new command needs those two updated and `docs/guide/first-week.md` ("the five that exist"). Search for the old count before finishing.
- [P14-07 / RLS test personas] Tariq is on Marketing and cannot read Engineering channels, so he cannot be the "second reader" of an Engineering poll; use Priya for another Engineering reader and keep Tariq as the outsider. Check who can read the channel before choosing the voters.
- [P14-07 / shell tool in a worktree] Heredocs written straight into files and compound `cd` commands were refused as too complex: use the Write and Edit tools for files and `python3 - <<'EOF'` for bulk edits.
- [P14-07 / live totals leak an anonymous vote] Hiding the ballots is not enough: in a DM or a tiny channel one count going up names the voter. Refuse anonymous polls under 5 readers and in DMs, and never push an anonymous poll's totals at the moment of a vote (one delayed push for all changes). Say it honestly in the dialog and the doc.
- [P14-07 / a definer function sees every channel as allowed] `app.channel_can` has an `is_system()` shortcut that is always true inside a definer function, so a permission check there passes for everyone; use `app.lookup_channel_can` (or test the caller's own rows) and test that an ordinary reader gets 42501.
- [P14-07 / test meta through SQL] `ChannelMessage.meta` is a Zod object that strips unknown keys, so `meta.poll` and `meta.pollResult` never show in a parsed message; read the marker with `SELECT meta FROM app.messages` in tests.
- [P14-07 / things a poll closes around] A poll must close even when its author lost the post right, the channel is archived or the message was deleted: fall back author, then admin, then a quiet system close, and test each. Check `messages.deleted_at` on every poll route and in the ballot trigger.
- [P14-07 / pushes arrive out of order] A page that applies "totals" pushes in arrival order can reopen a closed poll or show old counts; give the row a version the database raises on every update, drop older data on the client and refetch instead of restoring a snapshot after a refused vote.
- [P14-07 / radios vote on arrow keys] A real radio group casts a vote when the arrow key moves the focus. Use buttons with `aria-pressed` for a poll option, put the count in `aria-describedby`, and reserve a fixed slot for "Your vote" so it never overlaps the text at 390 px.
- [P14-07 / seeded channels are small] The seeded Engineering channels have only 4 readers, so an anonymous poll test needs a channel with at least 5 (a private room with added members, or ACL grants in the database test).
- [P14-07 / `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` obeys the UPDATE policy] The close route locked the poll row with `FOR UPDATE`, but the update policy is the author's alone, so for a team lead or an admin the row looked missing (404 "No such poll") although they may close it; no test had ever run against a database to say so → to lock a row for someone who may not update it, take the lock in a definer function that checks the permission itself (`app.polls_lock`), then read; and test an admin closing someone else's poll through the HTTP route, not only through the definer.
- [P14-07 / a system transaction has the nil workspace] The coalesced push read the poll in `ctx.db.system(...)` with no `workspaceId`, so `pushToPeople` addressed the nil workspace and nobody received `poll.changed`, with no error anywhere → any deferred push that runs outside the request opens its system transaction with `{ workspaceId }` of the row, and a live test waits long enough (anonymous totals come about 4 s after the last change) to see it arrive.
- [P14-07 / a job run closes every due poll, not only mine] A test that counted the channel's messages before and after the close job saw one more, because the same run closed another test's overdue poll and posted its result → assert on the thing that belongs to the poll under test (messages with its `meta.pollResult.id`), never on the length of a shared feed; and the creator of a shared DM must accept 200 as well as 201.
- [P14-07 e2e / a channel only takes people of its team] The poll spec added Tariq (Marketing) and Lena (a guest) to an Engineering private room and got 409 "that person is not on the channel's team"; the seeded Engineering team is only Omar, Nadia, Rafi and Priya → to get 6 readers, add Tariq and Sameera to the team first (`POST /api/teams/engineering/members`), and use a separate private room of two for "too small", because adding them makes the public #dev a 6-reader channel too.
- [P14-07 e2e / the toolbar is hidden at rest] The composer toolbar is `display: none` until the box has focus, so `getByTestId('poll-tool').click()` timed out with "element is not visible" → click the message box first, as the other composer specs do. A new last tool also moves End/ArrowLeft in composer-format.spec.ts (now "Ask a poll", not Emoji).
- [P14-07 e2e / a second click during a vote is dropped] PollCard ignores a click while its own vote request is in flight (`busy`), so two quick clicks on a multiple-choice poll left one choice → in a test wait for the first option to show `aria-pressed="true"` before the next click.
- [P14-05 e2e / a baseline went stale unseen] visual/channels/dm.visual.spec.ts was not re-run when P14-03/P14-05 added the header icons, the "Jump to first unread" chip, the channel filter icon and the Send later chevron → run the visual specs of the channel and DM screens in the same task that changes the composer or the channel header.
- [P14-07 live / a layout change between mousedown and mouseup eats the click] The composer's resting toolbar folds (`display: none`) on blur; a press on a poll option blurred the box, the feed grew by the toolbar's height under the pointer, and the click was lost (a second click worked) → anything that folds or opens on focus change must not move the content in the same press: keep it open from `pointerdown` until just after `pointerup` (the `holding` class), or reserve its space. Test a first click, not only a second.
- [P14-07 live / `busy` that drops a click] A button handler that returned early while its own request was in flight lost the user's newest choice → keep the newest wish in a ref, send it after the answer arrives (one request at a time, versions only go up), and drop it if the earlier request was refused.
- [P14-08 live / a client wait with no server answer for "never"] `pending` promised a card for links that could never get one (private address, denied host, remembered refusal), so the client held a blank slot for 20 s and then jumped → compute "will never get a card" on the server from what is known without a fetch (rules, address literal, unexpired non-ok cache row) and leave those messages out of `pending`. The cache is system-only: read it through `ctx.db.system` and return only a yes/no for addresses of messages the caller already reads.
- [P14-07 live / one error sentence for two cases] The anonymous-poll refusal said "...and a direct message is too small" in a channel too, which confused people → pick the sentence by the place (`pollAnonymousRefusal(kind)`), one constant for each case.
- [worktree / no node_modules] A fresh agent worktree has no `node_modules`; symlink the main checkout's root and package `node_modules` (untracked) to run vitest/tsc/eslint there.
- [P14-04 / replacing a shared SQL function forces plugin order] `app.message_authors` has now been replaced four times (channels, bots, webhooks, reminders), each CREATE OR REPLACE carrying the whole earlier body plus its own case; a plugin that replaces it must `dependsOn` the plugin whose version it extends, or its migration runs first and the later one silently drops its case → read the newest body before copying, add the dependency (reminders `dependsOn` channels and webhooks), and keep every earlier case in the new body.
- [P14-04 / notifications are anchored to a message] A notification row points at a message (`ref_id`); a reminder with no message has nothing to anchor to, and delivering by notification alone would have needed a new kind and a new place to open → the reminder is posted as a message from a system sender into a one-member direct message (`reminders:<person id>`), and the notification is made from that message. The normal new-message path must then skip that conversation, or the person is notified twice and the conversation's mute decides.
- [P14-04 / a direct message with one member looks like Notes to self] The page named any DM with only the signed-in person "Notes to self"; the Reminders conversation is such a DM → `dmLabel` reads the `reminders:` key first. Any new kind of DM-shaped channel needs its label and its notification rule decided together.
- [P14-04 / Postgres checks a row in a fixed order] A BEFORE INSERT trigger runs before the row-level-security WITH CHECK, and WITH CHECK runs before table CHECK constraints, so a test that expects a constraint error for a row the policy would also refuse gets 42501 (or the trigger's own error) instead → in a test, make the row pass the earlier checks, or assert only that the insert fails, not the code. The system role is not BYPASSRLS either: a table that the job writes needs its own system policy, and a trigger with a person's budget must let the system role through, or fixtures written by it are counted.
- [P14-04 / the schema-enums test does not load every migration] `packages/shared/test/schema-enums` builds its database from a fixed list of plugin migrations; the reminders migration is not in it, so a new enum or CHECK of the reminders tables is not compared with the Zod enum there → the RLS test of the plugin (`test/rls/reminders-rls.test.ts`) and the API test cover the states; add the migration to that list if the enum test is ever widened.
- [P14-04 / a regex time parser can be made quadratic] A "parse a time out of free text" function is the usual place for a backtracking pattern, and the text is typed by anyone → the parser (`commands/when.ts`) cuts the line into at most 7 words of at most 24 characters, reads each once with character codes, and slices the rest unread; the tests feed 100k-character hostile lines and assert the time stays linear (`expectLinear`). Resolve the zone from an IANA name against a passed-in `now`, never `new Date()` inside the parser.
- [P14-04 / a bare hour is two times] `/remind me at 5 …` means 05:00 or 17:00; guessing one is the exact mistake the command exists to avoid → an hour from 1 to 7 without am/pm is refused with a sentence that says what to write; 8 to 23 is that hour. A weekday means the next such day, never today, and a time already passed is refused, never rolled to tomorrow.
- [P14-06 / an unknown command used to be posted as text] The first version posted `/remnd me tomorrow x` to the channel as a message, so a typo became a public line, and the PLAN row says an unknown command is never posted as text → `parseSlash` answers `unknown` for a word shaped like a command (a slash, a letter, up to 32 letters, digits, `_` or `-`, then a space or the end), the page says so in a sentence and keeps the text, and `//word` posts it on purpose. What is not shaped like a command word (`/etc/hosts`, `/`, `/12 things`) is still plain text. Changing this made the older tests, docs/plugins/channels.md and docs/guide/first-week.md wrong: search them when a rule about what is posted changes.
- [P14-06 / a command list that grows breaks counts and a menu with no height limit] Adding nine commands moved the menu from 6 rows to 14 (the e2e `toHaveCount(6)`), made the `/help` dialog list and the first-week guide stale, and let the menu grow taller than a phone → search specs and docs for the old count, give the menu `max-height` with scrolling, and scroll the highlighted row into view.
- [P14-06 / a command must call the owner's route, and some state has no route] `/away` has no server state (presence knows only the heartbeat's `online` and `away`), and `/mute 1h` has no end time in the notification level → do not invent a table or a field to make a command work: `/away` is a flag in the browser that the heartbeat reads (a missing key in shared storage means off even when this tab last set it), and a timed `/mute` is refused with a sentence that suggests `/remind me in 1h unmute this channel`. Record such gaps in the plugin doc.
- [P14-06 / a hook's memo dependencies must not be a fresh object] `useSlashWho` listed the whole query result in its `useMemo` dependencies; the result object is new on every render, so the memo never held and every keystroke rebuilt `who` → depend on the data (`info.status === 'ok' ? info.data : null`), not on the wrapper.
- [P14-04 / a reminder for someone else reaches a person who muted you, with your own words] A person could make reminders for another person with no limit on what that one person receives, and the text was delivered as ordinary message text (links, mentions, emphasis, link previews) → cap what one person can be sent from all makers together (20 waiting, counted through a definer function because row-level security hides other makers' rows; lock maker and recipient in sorted order), and deliver other people's text as a code span (`plainCodeSpan`), which link previews and mentions skip; check the claim by parsing the delivered body with `parseMarkup`.
- [P14-04 / a plain-object lookup answers the language's own keys] `UNITS[word]`, `WEEKDAYS[word]` and `NUMBER_WORDS[word]` on a plain object read `constructor`, `__proto__`, `toString` from the prototype, so `/remind me constructor review` was taken as a weekday → look words up with `Object.hasOwn` (or a `Map`) and test `constructor`, `__proto__`, `toString` and `hasOwnProperty`.
- [P14-06 / a browser flag about a person must carry the person] The `/away` flag was stored as `'1'` under one key, so it stayed on after sign-out and showed the next person on that browser as away → store the person's id as the value and count it only while that person is the signed-in one (the session tells `setAwayOwner`); `onPersonChange` fires only when the person changes in a running tab, not on a fresh load, so it is not enough.
- [P14-04 / events written after switching the actor are not fanned out] After `app.reminders_act_as()` the session acts as the Reminders sender, and `app.enqueue_outbox` fans out only events whose actor is the session's actor; an event emitted through the original system transaction (`reminders.reminder.due`, `reminders.reminder.changed`) was written but reached no consumer, so no notification was raised. → emit through the transaction that carries the sender's actor (`sender.tx`), and test the effect (the inbox), not only the event row.
- [P14-06 e2e / a name typed last opens the people menu] `/invite @priya` then Enter picked the person from the @ menu (the right product behaviour, the same as any message) and ran nothing, so the notice never came → in a spec, close the menu with Esc before Enter when a command ends in an @name or #channel.
- [P14-06 e2e / the box reads the channel's facts after the first slash] The channel facts (member, manager) load when the first `/` is typed, so a spec that typed `/topic x` and Enter at once got "This conversation is still loading" → type `/`, wait for the menu row to stop saying "still loading", then type the rest. After `/leave` the page lands on the team's first screen (threads), not a fixed URL: assert it left the channel.
- [P14-23 / `pkill -f` kills your own shell] `pkill -f <pattern>` matched the shell running the command (exit 144) → stop a process by its pid, or `pgrep -fx` with the full command line.
- [P14-23 / vitest path filters also run `.claude/worktrees` copies] A filter such as `vitest run avatars` pulls in the stale copies under `.claude/worktrees/*`, which are slow and fail for unrelated reasons → read failures by path and ignore that folder.
- [P14-23 / `tools/devbox/deploy.sh` is not executable] Running it directly gives "Permission denied" → `bash tools/devbox/deploy.sh`.
- [P14-23 / a new CHECK column needs an enum mapping] `schema-enums.test.ts` fails for a column with a CHECK list that has no Zod enum → export the enum from shared, use it in the request schema, and map the column in the test.
- [P14-23 / live shots used a route that does not exist] The check went to `/t/engineering/roster`; the roster is `/settings/team/:slug/roster` → read the routes in `App.tsx` before writing a shots script.
- [P14-22 / an event subscriber is not a broadcast] A plugin's `ctx.events.subscribe` handler runs on ONE replica (the outbox consumer holds a lease), so "push list_changed from the handler" would reach only the streams that replica holds → the handler writes the new digest and bumps a counter in `app.mcp_bot_tools`; every replica that holds streams polls the counter (and wakes at once for a change it wrote itself). Never keep the only copy of a change in process memory.
- [P14-22 / the tools list depends on who asks] `tools/list` differs by run trigger and by the open run, so a digest taken from "the list of the last call" flaps between runs → digest the bot's own listing (the conversation handle, no run) in one place (`offeredTools`), and compare the page's "last listed" against that same digest.
- [P14-22 / a stream route returns a Readable] A plugin route that returns `{ body: Readable }` has its transaction committed when the handler returns, so nothing inside the stream may use that `tx` → open the stream after the reads it needs, and let the hub use `ctx.db.system` on its own timer.
- [P14-22 / a stream's close is asynchronous] A test that destroyed a stream and asserted the hub's count at once saw the old count → `await` a few milliseconds (the `close` event) before asserting the cap is free again.
- [P14-22 / a fresh worktree has no node_modules] `pnpm exec tsc` said "not found" in a new worktree → `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` (about 2 s) before the first gate.
- [P14-22 / Hermes freezes SOUL.md per thread] The pod wrote the `BOT.md` body as `SOUL.md`, so a thread that already had a turn kept the old text for its life (and the Secret took up to 70 s to arrive) → the body travels as `instructions` on every run on every connection; `SOUL.md` is one fixed identity line. Measure with `tools/devbox/probe-hermes-reload.mjs` after a Hermes upgrade, not from memory.
- [P14-22 / a byte limit never trips on tiny frames] The slow-reader drop only counted unread bytes over 16 KB, but a frame is about 90 bytes and a keepalive 14, so a stalled client would have held a slot for hours → also drop a stream that has anything unread at two keepalive times in a row, and test it at the real timings (fake timers), not with shrunk limits.
- [P14-22 / two refreshes of one digest race] Two transactions that each read the tool list and then upserted could write the older digest last → take a per-bot advisory lock first and read the list after it is held, so a waiting refresh sees what the one before it committed.
- [P14-22 / `hermes gateway run` is a foreground gateway] The probe tried to attach its throwaway profile with `hermes -p <p> gateway run`, which starts a whole gateway and fails here → on a multiplexing host gateway write the profile directory and wait (up to ~30 s) until `hermes gateway list` says "served by the default multiplexer"; the gateway API is at API_SERVER_HOST:API_SERVER_PORT of `~/.hermes/.env`, not necessarily 127.0.0.1:8642.
- [P14-22 / a name prefix is not a team] `--reserve eng` matched `mt-eng-ops-*` through `startsWith('mt-eng-')` → build the team's profile names from its bots with the one naming function and match them exactly.
- [P14-02 / a worktree can start on an older commit] The agent worktree began at ef9c3be, not the commit the task named, and lacked the P14-04/06 work it had to build on → fast-forward it to main and check the head commit before the first edit.
- [P14-02 / ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE still fires the BEFORE INSERT trigger] The draft cap trigger counted a re-save of an existing box as a new row and pushed the oldest draft out → the trigger first checks whether the slot already exists and does nothing then; a test saves a full box again and counts.
- [P14-02 / a generated column is not computed in a BEFORE trigger] `thread_key` (the thread or the nil uuid) is NULL inside a BEFORE INSERT trigger → a trigger that needs it uses `coalesce(NEW.thread_root_id, nil)` itself.
- [P14-02 / a system insert may not pass a person policy] The 200-drafts test seeded rows as the system role and failed the INSERT policy (it wants a person and a channel they may post in) → seed through the person (`as(nadia, ...)`), which also runs the cap trigger the way the app does.
- [P14-02 / the audience functions answer to a person only] `app.channel_audience` and `app.channel_role_audience` return nothing for the system role, so a test that computed the expected recipients as the system found 0 → ask through the route as a person (`broadcast-reach`), or count rows.
- [P14-02 / clearing a draft belongs in the route] Putting the draft delete into `postChannelMessage` would let the scheduled-send job erase text a person is typing now → the post route clears it, the shared write path does not.
- [P14-02 / a client DELETE has no body] The api client sends a JSON body for every non-GET call, so a DELETE that needs `?threadRootId=` must put it in the path (`deleteDraft`) → there is no query parameter for DELETE in `call`.
- [P14-02 / a draft box that switches must compare with the new key's own text] The first version of `useServerDraft` compared the text at open with the previous conversation's text (the composer swaps the text in an effect), so the server draft was never applied after a switch → compare with `readDraft(newKey)`.
- [P14-02 / reserved words are not mentions until someone builds them] `@here` and `@channel` were only reserved so no tag could take them; the mention code treated them as unknown handles. Making them real needed them filtered out of `handlesOf` first, or the group lookup would try them as tags → the two words are removed there and handled by `broadcast.ts`.
- [P14-02 / never compare a device clock with a server time] The first draft sync stamped every open and compared `Date.now()` with the server's `updatedAt`, so a stale local copy could overwrite a newer server draft → keep the server's own `updatedAt` as a "synced version" per box, stamp nothing on open, and skip a push of the text the box opened with until the server has answered.
- [P14-02 / a synced copy that the server no longer has is a sent message] A draft sent on one device came back on another from its local copy → remember per box what the server last held; if the server has nothing and the local text is that text, drop it.
- [P14-02 / a job cannot ask, so the agreement is taken when scheduling] A scheduled `@channel` in a big room could only fail at send time → the schedule route returns the same 409, the agreement is stored on the row (`broadcast_confirmed`), and the job passes it on.
- [P14-02 / a retry that cannot answer a 409 is a dead end] "Not sent · Retry" resent the same request and was refused again → detect `broadcast_needs_confirmation` on the pending item and offer "Send anyway", which sets `confirmBroadcast`.
- [P14-02 / never pick a badge from a display name] `authorMark` matched the name "Reminders", so a webhook with that name looked like the system → the server sends `author.sender` and the badge keys on it.
- [P14-02 / `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` cannot change the returned columns] Adding `sender` to `app.message_authors` needed `DROP FUNCTION` first (and the grants again).
- [P14-02 / hand-built image bytes in tests] JPEG height and width sit at bytes 7 and 9 after the marker, and a GIF header is 13 bytes; wrong offsets in the test made the parser look wrong → write the test header from the format table.
- [P14-02 / a shared store hook needs a server snapshot] `useCustomEmoji` read its store with `useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, getSnapshot)` and no third argument; the markdown and Answer tests render to a string on the server, where React 19 throws "Missing getServerSnapshot", so 61 tests broke although the coder had run only the files it knew → pass `() => state` as the server snapshot, and run the whole `clients/web` suite after touching a component that every message uses.
- [Marketing bots silent] A person off the bot's team (an Engineering lead naming `@content-drafter` in #content) got no answer and no error: `runs.open` refuses an asker who is not on the team, and the run job only logged it → every "no run" exit that a person caused must say so in the thread (`postNotOnTeamNotice`); and check WHO verified before blaming the bot (the live check was run as Omar, who is not on Marketing; Tariq and Priya were answered in ~19 s).
- [P14-13 / compound shell commands are refused in the worktree] A heredoc `cat > file` and `cd && ...` chains were refused as too complex → write files with the Write/Edit tools, keep each shell call to one plain command, use `python3 - <<'EOF'` for bulk edits.
- [P14-13 / which zip guard fires first] A test for "an entry over the size cap" used a repeated pattern and got `compression_ratio`, not `entry_too_large`, because the ratio guard runs first → use random bytes (hex of randomBytes) when a test must hit the size cap, and a repeated pattern only for the ratio guard.
- [P14-13 / tests that depend on claim order] The Mattermost expectations changed after the claim test had turned placeholders into people → put every "before claim" describe first in the file and let the claim test handle every placeholder of the same email.
- [P14-13 / fixtures go stale] The committed Slack and Mattermost fixtures are made by test/fixtures.ts; a test fails when they differ → after any edit of fixtures.ts run `pnpm exec tsx packages/plugins/import/test/generate-fixtures.ts` and commit the result.
- [P14-13 / replacing app.message_authors needs the dependsOn order] The import migration replaces `app.message_authors`, which bots and webhooks also extend → import must depend on webhooks (which depends on bots), so the replacement carries every join; any later plugin that extends the function must do the same.
- [P14-13 / control characters in a regex] `no-control-regex` fails on a character class that spans the control range in the name checks → keep the check (it is the point) and put `// eslint-disable-next-line no-control-regex` above it.
- [P14-13 e2e / report wording] The dry-run report counts messages of the whole archive ("212 messages"), not the 200 of the fixture's channels, and the channel may become `#general-2` when the name is taken → assert on the report text, and read the made channel's name from the run's channelLines instead of guessing it.
- [P14-13b / a unique-key insert breaks the retry] The import made its placeholders with a plain INSERT into `import_people` (unique per workspace, source, source id), so a run cut off between batches failed on the rerun → look the row up first and insert only when it is missing; test a crash between batches and a second run.
- [P14-13b / a flag only helps if something reads it] Cancel set `cancel_requested` for an analyse job that never reads it, and a crash left the run `analysing` for ever (so "another import is running") → a run that no job holds ends from the request itself, and a sweep fails any run with no sign of life for 30 minutes.
- [P14-13b / read then write races a cancel] The run job read the run as queued, a cancel ended it, and the job's unconditional UPDATE made it running again → repeat the status test in the UPDATE itself and look at the returned rows.
- [P14-13b / keys of one server are not unique across servers] Mattermost channel names and usernames were used as the archive's ids, so two servers' `general` became one channel, and the chosen team was ignored by the map → put an archive fingerprint in the key and the team in the map key.
- [P14-13b / clamped time must not feed a key] A future `create_at` was cut to now for the stored time and the same cut fed the message key, so the key changed with every run → build the key from the raw value, clamp only what is stored.
- [P14-13b / a refused upload still has to be read a little] Closing the socket at once makes the client see a reset instead of the 403, and reading it to the end takes a gigabyte → read up to 1 MiB, answer, cut the connection a second later.
- [P14-09 / a public link must live under /api] The unsubscribe page was planned at `/unsubscribe/...`, but nginx proxies only `/api`, `/hooks` and a few more paths, so the link in a mail would have hit the web app's 404 → put every public server route under `/api/...` and check the nginx config first.
- [P14-09 / opening a link must not change anything] Mail scanners and previews open every link in a mail, so a GET that unsubscribed would turn the digest off for people who never asked → GET shows a page with a button, only POST changes the setting.
- [P14-09 / the audience view needs a workspace] `app.channel_audience` answers nothing unless `app.workspace_id()` is set, and a detached cron job has no workspace, so the digest found no one → loop over `app.workspaces` and run each with `ctx.db.system(fn, { workspaceId })`.
- [P14-09 / one clock for one decision] The "never twice" watermark was compared with the injected clock while the stored time was the database's, so a skewed test clock sent twice or never → store and compare the watermark in database time (cut to milliseconds so a put-back matches exactly) and use the injected clock only for the away check.
- [P14-09 / the same bytes, a different spelling] The last character of a base64url signature carries spare bits, so four different strings decode to one signature and a tampered link still verified → check that re-encoding the decoded bytes gives the same text, then compare in constant time.
- [P14-09 / hide a secret column with a grant] The per-person unsubscribe key sits next to columns the person may edit → column grants give the person role `enabled` and `delay_minutes` only, so the key cannot be read and the watermark cannot be moved.
- [P14-09 / the edit tool turns escapes into characters] A regex written with `\u2028` in a file came out with the real character and the file no longer parsed → build such characters with `String.fromCharCode` or `chr()` in a script, and read the file back.
- [P14-09 / compound shell commands are refused here] Heredocs that write files and `cd && ...` chains were refused as too complex in the worktree → write files with Write or Edit, or a script file in the scratchpad run with `python3`, and keep shell calls single.
- [P14-14 / moving shared code breaks tests that read its source] Link-preview tests read `src/resolver.ts` and `safe-fetch.ts` as text to check what they import; after the fetcher moved to the SDK they read a two-line shim and failed → point such source-reading tests at the new file when code moves, and keep a thin re-export in the old place so imports do not break.
- [P14-14 / one fetcher, two verbs] Adding POST to the guarded fetch must not mean "follow redirects as before": a 301/302/303 turns a POST into a GET in a browser → a POST follows only 307/308 (same body, target vetted again) and anything else comes back as `bad_status` carrying its status, so a retry rule can read the number instead of parsing a message.
- [P14-14 / Slack's rate limit is a 429] The brief said Slack-style errors are HTTP 200, but Slack client libraries retry on a 429 with `Retry-After` and ignore the header on a 200 → answer `rate_limited` as `429` + `Retry-After` + `{ok:false,error:"rate_limited"}`; every other Slack error stays `200`.
- [P14-14 / two messages, one ts] A Slack `ts` derived from a UUIDv7 (48-bit ms + 12-bit fraction) has microsecond resolution, so two messages created in the same microsecond share it → the lookup prefers the integration's own message, `chat.update` re-checks the author, and the doc says so; test 20 parallel posts for distinct values.
- [P14-14 / a loop needs three guards, not one] A reply that says the trigger word again could trigger its own hook → only a person's message triggers (the reply is written by the integration's system actor), the `(hook, message)` delivery row is the once-only guard, and the reply is posted in the same transaction that marks the delivery done.
- [P14-14 / lookup by hash needs an index and a constant-time check] A token has no id in it, so the call is found by the SHA-256 of the whole token → index `secret_hash`, re-check with `timingSafeEqual`, and make revoked, rotated and unknown answer the same word.
- [P14-14 / the limit of 20 per channel bites tests] Every test that made a token filled the same channel and the 21st got `409` → one fresh channel per group of tests.
- [P14-14 / a browser spec cannot reach a private receiver] The server (rightly) refuses 127.0.0.1, so a spec's local receiver was unreachable → a `NODE_ENV=test`-only seam (`MANYTHREADS_STACK_WEBHOOK_RECEIVER`) maps `*.fake.example` to a public number and dials the receiver; every other rule still runs.
- [P14-14 / lint reads `#C123` as a colour] A Slack channel mention `<#C123|general>` in a test failed the raw-hex rule → write `<#CHAN1|general>`; use `good` instead of `#36a64f` for an attachment colour.
- [P14-14 / emoji joiners in a character class] A regex class holding ZWJ, variation selector and keycap characters trips `no-misleading-character-class` → write them as an alternation of `‍|️|⃣`.
- [P14-14 / a dirty worktree blocks `git merge --ff-only`] The merge refused because main touched files I had edited → `git stash push -u -m <unique tag>`, merge, `git stash apply <sha>`, then drop that entry by its tag; never a bare stash.
- [P14-09b / one transaction for a whole round] The digest claimed every person of a batch in one transaction, so one failing row rolled back the whole workspace and a failing audit write after the mail released nothing, nothing was recorded, and a hung mail server stalled the loop → claim, mail and record per person, each in its own try/catch; release the claim only when the mail did NOT go out, log (without addresses) when only the record failed, and give the mail send a timeout.
- [P14-09b / a mailer that resolves is not a mailer that sent] The unconfigured mailer resolves without sending, so the digest moved watermarks and wrote "Sent" rows for mail that never left → a mail service says `configured`, and a job that marks work as done checks it first and logs once per process.
- [P14-09b / scan the people who can act, not everyone with a row] The candidate query read every person with unread items and dropped the online ones afterwards, so online people filled each 200-person batch every minute and a cap starved higher ids → hand the away filter to SQL (people seen lately, from the provider) and resume after the last person reached.
- [P14-09b / a per-row function in a lateral] `channel_audience(c.id)` was called for each item, building the channel's whole audience each time → ask once per channel per run and check membership in code.
- [P14-09b / words frozen when the thing was made] The mail used the preview stored with the notification, so an edit was ignored and a deleted message still showed → read the message's current text when the mail is made, still under the reader's access check.
- [P14-09b / copy that promises more than the code] The footer said the mail "never repeats until you have been away again" and the subject said "unread" for a count of "new since the last digest" → say what the rule is ("at most one email every <delay>", "N new messages").
- [P14-09b / an UNLOGGED table lies after a crash] Everyone looks away until the tabs beat again → keep an UNLOGGED marker beside the heartbeats; its absence says the data restarted, and the job waits five minutes after making it.
- [P14-09b / disabled drops keyboard focus] A switch that was `disabled` while saving lost focus for keyboard users → keep it focusable with `aria-busy`, ignore clicks while busy, and announce the result in a polite live region.
- [P14-14b / a credential in the query string reaches the access log] The Slack-shaped API read `token` from the query as well as the header and body, and the request logger writes the whole URL → read the token from the header or the body only, and make `redactUrl` hide `token=` values and any `mtat_...` text as a second line of defence.
- [P14-14b / a maker must be able to read what the hook sends out] An outgoing webhook only asked "can the caller manage the channel?", so a lead or admin who was not in a private channel could send all of its messages to an outside address → `app.integrations_create` also requires read access for an outgoing hook, and turning a hook on, changing its address, words or secret needs the same; switching it off stays open to every manager.
- [P14-14b / a per-address counter on a shared address] The Slack API route counted every call per address (600 a minute) before the handler, so junk from a shared office address could 429 valid scripts → list such routes as `failures`, count only unknown-token calls (the lockout), and keep the per-token limit for valid tokens. `/hooks/:id/:secret` still has the same coarse 600 a minute per address on all calls: left as it is, to look at later.
- [P14-14b / a secret that could not be read must not become no secret] `ctx.secrets.get(...).catch(() => null)` made a hook that should be signed go out unsigned → `sendCall` is the one choke point: a hook with a signing secret id and no readable secret throws (a retryable `internal_error`) before anything is sent.
- [P14-14b / a repeated POST carries the first host's secrets] The guarded fetch replayed a POST body and signature headers on a 307/308 to any public host, even from https to http, and the data-loss check had run for the first host only → a POST is repeated only at the same host and never https to http (`postRedirectRefusal`); a moved receiver is a `bad_redirect` and the lead sets the new address.
- [P14-14b / a label can name another site than its link] `<https://evil.com|https://bank.com>` became `[https://bank.com](https://evil.com)` with nothing to show where it goes → a sender-written label that names a host other than the target's is followed by the real host (`linkWithHost`); ordinary labels are unchanged.
- An event subscriber must not call anything that reads on a connection of its own (the capability broker, `ctx.runs.may`, `offeredTools`) while its transaction is open: it sees none of the rows the transaction wrote, and a template that loads many bots at once holds every pooled system connection while each waits for one more, so the server never closes (templates.test and teams-api.test hung at `server.close()`). Do that work after the transaction ended, one bot at a time (gateway-mcp `refreshAfterCommit`).
- [P14-19 / one tick rule, two places] The chat, a card and a page each counted "box number n" their own way, so a tick could land on another line → one pure `setTaskChecked` / `taskItemAt` in `@manythreads/shared`, used by the client and by the tick route; the route also takes the box's text and answers 409 when box n now says something else.
- [P14-19 / `.` does not match a carriage return] A box regex ending in `(.*)$` found no box on a Windows line (`- [ ] a\r`) → end it with `([\s\S]*)$`, and test a CRLF text.
- [P14-19 / a tick must not overwrite a bot's rewrite] Writing the whole description from the page would drop a bot's change made while the card was open → the door `app.tasks_tick` compares the old text (compare-and-set) and answers `changed`; the person reads the card again.
- [P14-19 / the editor ticks, then the whole page is re-serialised] Letting Tiptap tick and Save rewrote the whole page in the commit → intercept the checkbox click, change the one character in the source text, save that, and leave the click to the editor only when the source and the editor count the boxes differently.
- [P14-19 / a worktree has no node_modules] `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` in the worktree takes seconds and is needed before vitest or typecheck.
- [P14-21 / a stored layout has more readers than its writer] A token kept once under an owner changes what the Secret holds, so the pod's sync script name check, the garbage collection (prune and remove) and the publish script all had to learn it, not only the provider → list every reader of a store (scripts, jobs, tests) before changing its shape, and test each one.
- [P14-21 / a background job has no author] A job cannot post a channel message: messages need an author actor, and only a plugin with its own sender actor (like reminders, with a `message_authors` migration chain) has one → when a plan says "the server posts one message", check for an author first; otherwise let the bot that met the problem say it, and write the deviation in the plan row.
- [P14-21 / a rate limit counts the whole test file] One test file made 20 saves of the push credential before the new tests ran, so the new ones got 429 → put a new flow in its own test file (its own server) instead of at the end of a long one.
- [P14-21 / a worktree has no packages] A fresh agent worktree has no `node_modules`, so `pnpm typecheck` said `tsc` was not found → run `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` first.
- [P14-21 / a refusal remembered in one place] A 401 seen by the sync, the publish script or the job must reach the other two, so each marks the token in the pod (`refused`) and the job turns that mark into the project's status → keep one mark that all of them read, and clear it only when a new token is saved.
- [P14-21 / one good answer is not a verdict on the others] An "ok" for one project of a shared token flipped every project on it back to good, including one marked because the token cannot write to its repository, and rewrote rows (and `updated_at`) that had not changed → an ok answer only touches the project that was asked, plus the expiry of mates that are good already; write rows only when something changes, and keep a separate "last looked at" column so a batch still rotates.
- [P14-21 / a mark is a claim, not a fact] The pod's "refused" mark was turned straight into "needs replacing", so one stray 401 could switch an owner off for good → ask GitHub (`GET /user`) before acting on a mark, clear the mark if it answers well, and treat only a 401 as a refusal (404, 410 and 5xx say nothing about the token).
- [P14-21 / two writes that must go together] Writing the shared token and then removing other projects' tokens in separate calls could leave pod and rows out of step → give the provider one call that does both (`putShared(…, drop)`) and add the failing-Secret test.
- [P14-21 / locks after a slow call] The shared save asked GitHub for each project and only then wrote, so a project renamed or given its own token in between was overwritten → select the rows again `FOR UPDATE` after the slow calls and compare the address; never overwrite a token a project already has of its own.
- [P14-19c / a selector copied from the library's docs, not from the page] The page viewer looked for `li[data-type="taskItem"]`; the Tiptap version in use renders `li[data-checked]` with no `data-type`, so a tick never reached the save and fell back to the editor: found only by the live check (`e2e/screens/task.verify-p14-19-21.shots.ts`), because the pure function was tested and the DOM was not → when a handler reads the DOM, check the selector against the live page.
- [P19-02 / event names] A two-part event name (`bot_runner.created`) fails the registry, which wants at least three parts → name events `<area>.<thing>.<what happened>` (`bot_runner.runner.created`) and say so in the plan row.
- [P19-02 / one plugin needing another's code] The Hermes outbound guard lived in runtime-hermes, and the new plugin needed it; plugins may import only the SDK and shared → move the shared code to an SDK subpath (`@manythreads/sdk/hermes-outbound`) and leave a one-line re-export where it was; reach other behaviour through a provider (`bot.runners`), never an import.
- [P19-02 / reading a secret] `ctx.secrets.get` was called inside an admin's transaction and failed with a 500 (only the system actor may open a secret) → read keys in `ctx.db.system(fn, { workspaceId })` and use the admin's transaction only for `put` and `delete`.
- [P19-02 / a policy that lets the wrong role write] The old `hermes_connections` write policies allowed a team lead, so closing the lead's routes with a 403 would have left the table open below the API → replace the policy in a new forward-only migration (workspace admin and system only) and add an RLS test that a lead's INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE changes nothing.
- [P19-02 / failure notices are chosen by words] The failure notice for a run the platform refused is picked by looking for words in the message; a refusal that says "runner" shows the "runner offline" notice → write refusal messages with that in mind, and assert the notice the thread shows, not an assumed category.
- [P19-02 / long shell commands] Heredocs and long compound `cd … && …` commands were refused in the sandbox → write a small script to the scratchpad with the Write tool and run it as one command.
- [P19-02 / a secret in a list] A list that an admin and a lead both read must be built per role: the connections list showed the address to everyone → compute the role once per request and send `baseUrl: null` unless the caller is a workspace admin; test the lead's view of the list and of every error text.
- [P19-04 / a shut card must stay reachable] A kind that cannot be chosen yet (Claude Code, Codex) and the default star of a runner that cannot be the default were first plain `disabled` buttons, which drop out of the Tab order so a keyboard user never learns why → use `aria-disabled="true"` with the reason as text, and ignore the press.
- [P19-04 / testing a gateway needs a profile] The stand-in gateway (and a real one with no default profile) answers 404 on `/v1/models`, so a Test with no profile always shows "did not answer as a Hermes gateway" → the Test dialog has an optional "Profile to ask for" field; specs type `probe`.
- [P19-04 / a word test matched a product name] A copy test for "no plan or phase wording" used `/PLAN/i` and failed on "Claude plan" → match the internal words case-sensitively (`\bPLAN\b`, `P19`, `\bphase\b`).
- [P19-04 / branded ids in a pure model] The teams list sent plain strings where the schema wants `TeamId` and `tsc` refused → cast once where the id comes from the server's own list, with a comment saying so.
- [P19-04 / a fresh worktree has no node_modules] Typecheck and vitest failed with missing modules in a new worktree → run `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` first.
- [P19-04 / e2e delete guard] The "bots still placed" refusal counts `placement.gateway.connection` through an adopted connection, so the spec has to set the bot's placement and the old connection by SQL before adopting, then clear the placement to prove the delete goes through.
- [P19-04 / shell] Heredocs mixed with other commands and compound `cd … &&` lines were refused again → use the Write and Edit tools, or one plain command.
- [P19-04 / a failed send kept the secret] A failed add or save left the typed key in the field and in state → clear the key field in the catch branch, use `autoComplete="new-password"` (not `off`) so the browser does not offer to store it, and guard a double submit with a ref (state updates after the second press).
- [P19-04 / one busy flag for the whole list] A single "busy" string made one slow row block the next press on another row → keep a set of busy row keys.
- [P19-04 / a locked switch] A `disabled` switch cannot be focused, so the reason it is locked is never read → `aria-disabled` plus `aria-describedby` on the reason; a switch label names the thing ("Use X"), not its state, because the switch already says on/off.
- [P19-04 / teams the list leaves out] A runner can be shared with an archived team that the live team list does not return; the teams control then hid it and a save dropped it → offer the union of the live teams and the teams already on the runner.
- [P19-04 / mock drift] The mock transport's messages and rules had drifted from the real routes (delete wording, default switch-off wording, no-change patch, Claude text, LLM status) → copy each wording from the route file when adding a mock route.
- [P19-04 / Playwright and aria-disabled] Playwright treats `aria-disabled="true"` as not enabled, so `click()` on a shut card waits until the test times out with a confusing "context closed" error → use `click({ force: true })` when the test means to press a shut control.
- [P19-04 / focus after a row vanishes] Moving focus to the page heading right after a dialog closed did nothing: the closing dialog gives focus back to the button that opened it, and that button only disappears when the list reloads → move focus once the reloaded list arrives (compare with the list you had when the action finished).
- [P19-03 / a check in the loader] The bot loader runs inside the outbox consumer's own transaction; asking the runner catalog on another connection would not see rows of that transaction and can block → give the provider a method that takes the caller's `tx` (`checkPlacement`) and use only that from the loader.
- [P19-03 / failure notice by words] A run refused by a runner would again have been shown the notice picked by words in the message → carry a typed `failure: 'runner_refused'` with the runner and a reason word through the run result, and post the runner's own sentence (built from the label, never from a remote answer); assert the line the thread shows.
- [P19-03 / the same placement rule in two places] The managed pod's profile list selected bots by the SQL `placement->gateway->>connection = 'managed'`, so a bot on a pod runner (`runs_on`) or on a default pod runner got no profile and every run failed with "no profile" → keep that rule in one function (`podPlacementWhere`) and use it wherever bots are picked for a pod.
- [P19-03 / a DB test run during CI] A DB-backed vitest was started while a CI run held the shared dev database (the pgrep check was skipped because the file name looked like a unit test) → many runtime-hermes tests build a repo world; run `pgrep -fx 'bash tools/devbox/ci.sh.*'` before every vitest that is not one of the pure unit files, and stop the run at once if CI is up.
- [P19-03 / a screen that does not exist] The plan names a "create-a-bot flow", but at this base bots only come from templates, proposals and Files → grep the web for the screen before promising a place in it; build the picker as one component on the Placement tab and say in the ledger where else it goes when the screen exists.
- [P19 / a pinned image digest the registry did not hold] The chart pins the Postgres image by digest (`…@sha256:2bad…`), but the in-cluster registry held the same build under another digest (`bbbc…`, the index of the image pushed after the 2026-10-31 reinstall); it only ran because the node's own image store still had the old one. A disk-pressure event (2026-11-02 07:15) made the kubelet clean the node's images, the registry pod was evicted too, and Postgres, LiteLLM and Hindsight could not start (`ImagePullBackOff`, "not found") → pin the digest the registry answers for (`curl -H 'Accept: application/vnd.oci.image.index.v1+json' http://127.0.0.1:30500/v2/platform/postgres-cnpg/manifests/<tag>`), patch the Cluster `imageName` and delete the pod; watch the disk (`df -h /`) before long builds.
- [P19-10 / tool names a provider rejects] The tools the MCP gateway offers are capability names with a dot (`memory.recall`), and OpenAI and Anthropic accept only letters, digits, underscore and hyphen, so the first real call would have failed while every stand-in test passed → send the model a wire name (`memory_recall`) and keep a table back to the real tool; test that every offered name matches `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$` and that two names that collapse stay apart.
- [P19-10 / the unmetered check had nothing to check] The plan said "a pod run leaves a spend line", but no Hermes run wrote a line in `llm_spend` at all (only alias test, classify and eval did), so a check alone would have fired on every run → read the code that writes the thing you are going to check before writing the check; here the run job now writes one line from the tokens Hermes reports, and says so as a limit.
- [P19-10 / a scripted insert landed in the wrong function] A Python patch that inserted a new method before the first `  };\n}\n` put it inside `fetchTransport` → anchor inserts on a line unique to the target function (here the end of `complete`) and grep the result for the order of the definitions.
- [P19-10 / a runtime must not touch the database] The model runtime needs a model call and a tool call, which need transactions → the run job hands the provider two doors in its scope (`scope.model`, `scope.tools`) and the provider stays a loop that tests with stubs; do not give a `bot_runtime` provider a pool.
- [P19-10 / a typed failure, not a word] A new cause (the step limit) would have been picked as "other" by reading the error text → add the typed `failure` value and a fixed sentence first; the text is only for the log.
- [P19-10 / one rule for where a bot runs] The provider is chosen by the runner's kind (`kindOf`), not by the `bots.runtime` column (CHECK-limited to hermes and rules) → a new kind of place to run means one question to the catalog and no new column; a bot with no `runs_on` stays on the old path untouched.
- [P19-10 / a picker and a loader must say the same sentence] The web picker and the loader both refuse a model-only runner for a bot that asks for a shell, files or a browser → write the sentence once on the server and copy its wording into the pure web helper with a test on the key phrases, so a bot is never selectable in the picker and then invalid on save.
- [P19-10 / the shared dev database during CI] CI started while the DB-backed tests of this change were written but not yet run → check `pgrep -af 'tools/devbox/ci.sh'` before every DB-backed run, write the tests first and run them when the check is empty.
- [P19-10 / a list of notice categories in two places] A new failure category (`steps`) was added to the notice text and the run job but not to the `runFailure` list that the message meta check in channels uses, so the notice was refused at post time and only a log line said so → grep for an existing category (`hermes_unreachable`) across all packages when adding one, and test the notice end to end in the thread, not just its text.
- [P19-10 / redacting a copy of the newest message] The model call redacted only the newest user or tool message, in a copy, while the loop kept the original history, so turn two sent a secret from the prompt in clear text; with parallel tool calls only the last tool answer was checked → check every user and tool message added since the last turn, return the redacted history from the call, and make the caller replace its own history with it. A test that pins "earlier messages are sent as they were" pins the bug: assert that no later turn carries the secret.
- [P19-10 / a transaction held across a model call] The run job wrapped the whole chat call in one system transaction, so a connection stayed busy for the minutes a proxy could take → a turn is a short check transaction, the HTTP call with none open, and a short spend-line transaction; the test reads the pool's in-use count from inside the proxy stand-in.
- [P19-10 / a tool that never answers] A loop that awaits a tool call has no end if the tool hangs, and the bot's one-run-at-a-time lock holds with it → race every model and tool call against the remaining run budget and a stop signal, check the deadline before each call, and test with a tool that never resolves. A tool that throws is that tool's failure (a fixed answer to the model), not the run's.
- [P19-10 / steps are not tool calls] A step limit counts model turns, but one turn may ask for any number of tools → also cap tool calls per turn and per run; an answer cut off at the length limit is a failure, never a reply that looks complete.
- [P19-10 / a canary test that proves nothing] A test that put canary keys in the process environment and checked the rendered pod objects passed because the renderer never reads that environment → feed the canary through what the code does read, use the real renderer, or drop the test.
- [P19-10c / a runner check that only looked at one kind of need] The model runner refused a bot that asked for a shell, files or a browser, but not one that had no way to post its reply, so such a bot loaded as Ready and every question ended in "not set up to post messages" → when a runner replaces a part of the run (here the platform posts the reply), list everything the bot must bring for that part and refuse its absence at placement, in one shared function the loader, the proposal check and the picker all call; read the code that makes the late failure (`CapabilityRefusedError` on `messages.post`) to get the exact capability names.
- [P19-10c / a check that cannot read what it checks] The proposal check would have run the placement question on the proposer's transaction, but the runner catalog is readable only by workspace admins, so a team lead's proposal would be told "no such runner" → ask it on a system transaction (read only, and only when the file names a runner); test it as a lead who is not an admin, as well as an admin.
- [P19-10c / a name on a report that someone else's rules may hide] The spend table showed a bot id because the ledger holds ids; resolving names with a join on the caller's own transaction lets row-level security decide who sees a name, and a deleted or unreadable bot stays a bare id → keep the fallback and the whole id as the title, and test the fallback with a deleted bot and a non-admin viewer.
- [P19-10c / scripted edits refused] Multi-line python heredocs and `sed` with a shell variable as the file were refused in the worktree → write the script with the Write tool into the scratchpad and run it as one plain command, or use the Edit tool.
- [P19-08 / a column that was open before its route existed] `bot_runners.node` already existed, and the generic create and patch routes accepted any string for it, so the operator's allowlist and the Ready check would have been bypassed by a plain PATCH → when a new route owns a field, take the field out of the generic request schemas (strict objects then refuse it) and put a database check on its shape; a test sends the field through the old routes and expects a refusal.
- [P19-08 / a plugin cannot read the cluster] The runner catalog is a plugin and holds no cluster rights, and plugins do not import each other → the plugin that owns the client (hermes-pods) registers a provider (`runner.nodes`), the catalog looks it up by name at request time, and an absent provider is a plain answer (`available: false`, naming a machine is a `409`), not a crash. A new provider kind needs three lists changed together: `PROVIDER_KINDS` (sdk), the manifest `extends` enum (shared) and the kernel registries.
