- [P19-08 / free CPU is a bigger permission than it looks] "Free CPU and memory" needs a read of every pod in the cluster, a second cluster-wide right, to subtract what runs → report what the machine offers (allocatable), say "offers" on the page, and write the deviation in the row's Gap column instead of widening the role quietly.
- [P19-08 / a local volume is tied to one machine] Adding `nodeSelector` to a pod whose `local-path` claim is already bound elsewhere leaves the pod Pending forever; the claim is the thing to move → a move is scale to zero, wait until no pod is left, delete the stale claims, and let the next pass make them new; judge a claim stale only from an annotation that names another machine (never on a guess), and keep every Secret. Say in the dialog, in plain words, that both volumes start empty.
- [P19-08 / an event that names the machine] The first draft of `bot_runner.node.changed` carried the node name, and an event and audit row are read by more people than the admin who chose it → carry booleans (`wasPinned`, `isPinned`, `recreatesWorkVolume`) and the runner name; a test greps the stored event for the machine names it just used.
- [P19-08 / two answers that tell names apart] A node outside the operator's list and a name that is no machine at all must get the same answer, or the difference tells which machines exist → judge the allowlist on the cluster's own list and answer both with one sentence; a test compares the two bodies.
- [P19-08 / the cluster's own words] A Pending pod's condition message can hold addresses and taints → map it to a fixed reason (`node_not_ready`, `not_placed`, `starting`) and test that a marker string placed in the message never reaches the page or the API answer.
- [P19-08 / the shell in a worktree agent] A worktree-isolated agent's shell refused heredocs, `git -C`, `awk` programs and compound commands, and `--set key={a,b}` in Helm → write a script file with the Write tool and run it as one plain `python3 <path>` command, use `--set key[0]=…`, and keep each Bash call to a single plain command.
- [P19-08 / a node has two names] The scheduler writes the Node object's name into `volume.kubernetes.io/selected-node`; the picker, the `nodeSelector` and the database use the `kubernetes.io/hostname` label. Where they differ (a hostname override, some clouds) a claim made on the right machine looked stale on the next pass, and the controller would have deleted both claims every 30 seconds → trust this controller's own annotation (it holds the hostname); map a scheduler name through the node list to the hostname label before comparing; a name that cannot be mapped is unknown and never stale; a test uses a node whose object name differs from its hostname.
- [P19-08 / a computed value must not drive a delete] The machine of a team's pod is worked out from several runners (the default, enabled, the Teams list, the name order), so Make default, Enable or Disable and a Teams edit changed it with no admin move and the controller deleted the volumes → the controller moves only on an explicit confirmation written by the one route that asks the admin; any other change is held (the pod stays where its volumes are, nothing is deleted) and shown as "Waiting … confirm the move". Test every path that changes the computed value, not only the route that was meant to.
- [P19-08 / a confirmation needs a shape and an end] A bare "confirmed" flag would stay true for ever and cover a later, unrelated change → the mark names the machine and the time (`worker-2@<epoch ms>`), counts only for that machine and for 15 minutes, must be on every claim of the team, and a move already under way is finished without it.
- [P19-08 / verify the target before the irreversible step] The value in the database is not proof of a machine → before deleting a volume re-read the cluster's nodes and require an existing, Ready node on the operator's list; if it cannot be checked, hold or stay stopped and delete nothing. Delete both claims or neither, and only a claim that carries this team's id label.
- [P19-08 / NOT VALID still checks on update] A `NOT VALID` check skips old rows when it is added, but an UPDATE of such a row is checked again (23514), so a legacy bad value would make an unrelated edit fail for ever → clear the old values in the same migration before adding the check, and test it by dropping the checks, writing bad rows as the table owner, and running the migration file.
- [P19-08 / a test that hides its own failure] `.catch(() => [])` on the audit query hid that the table was not even named right (`audit_log`, not `audit_events`), so the assertion about the audit row proved nothing → never swallow the error in a test's query; poll for the row and assert its fields.
- [P19-08 / a polling page] A 8-second reload kept running in a hidden tab and for a pod that was only waiting for the admin → poll only while something changes by itself and the tab is visible, and reload once when the tab comes back.
- [P7-02 / a fixed address and a free name] The Add a bot page lives at `/t/:team/bots/new`, the same shape as a bot's page `/t/:team/bots/:botSlug`, so a bot called `new` could never be opened → the setup policy refuses the slug `new`, with a test; when a screen takes a static segment beside a user-chosen name, refuse that name where names are made.
- [P7-02 / the model chooses, the code grades] A grade the model writes is a grade the model can talk itself into → the helper only lists what the brief needs (kind and name); the grade, the question for each gap, the refusals of `propose_config` and "what it won't be able to do" come from code (`grade.ts`, `policy.ts`) that has unit tests for every kind and every refusal.
- [P7-02 / a session that borrows a bot] The model runtime and Hermes both need a bot, a runner and a run, and none exists while a bot is being set up; borrowing a throwaway bot would put an unapproved actor in the roster → a small loop of its own over `provider.llm.chat` with `botId: null`, so the same guard, budgets and spend line apply and no actor is made.
- [P7-02 / the wide door for a narrow need] The helper must call `knowledge.search` and `proposals.propose` as the person, and the only door that called capabilities was `tool.invoke`, which acts as any run's bot → a narrow `capability.call` door (call a capability in the caller's own transaction, nothing else) with a test that every other member throws.
- [P7-02 / the end of a turn is not the end of the row] After `propose_config` the row is `proposed`, not `working`, so the loop's "is it still mine" check said cancelled and threw away a good proposal → a turn a tool ended is final whatever the check says; a unit test covers it.
- [P7-02 / tools run as the person] A tool that reads the inventory as the system would show other teams' names → every tool runs in the asker's own transaction, the team comes from the session and never from the model's arguments, and a test lists what exists only in other teams and asserts none of it (and no key, address or token) is in the `describe_self` answer.
- [P7-04 / a request parsed in the argument list] `startSetup` calls `Request.parse(...)` while building the call, so a bad brief throws before the promise exists and `expect(...).rejects` fails → the screen calls it inside `try`, and the test uses `expect(() => ...).toThrow()`.
- [P7-04 / a poll that never stops] A page that polls a conversation must stop for a gone or foreign one (404, 403) and not poll a hidden tab → stop on those two answers, skip the fetch while `document.hidden`, read once when the tab comes back.
- [P7-04 / the lockfile in a worktree] `pnpm install --offline` added unrelated `@tauri-apps/cli-*` entries to `pnpm-lock.yaml` → revert the file, add only the new package's importer block by hand, and check with `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile`.
- [P7-02 / a typo that loosens a limit] A `TEAM.md` default block that could not be read fell back to `standard`, the loosest guard, so a typo such as `guardPreset: lax` widened what the helper may propose → a key that is absent takes the documented default, but one that is present and cannot be read reads as the strictest (or refuses); test the typo case.
- [P7-02 / a later answer must not loosen an earlier one] The requirement merge let a second listing replace the kind or name of an id already graded as a gap, and a gap could turn `covered` → merge by (id, kind, name), keep the stricter grade, put a different thing under a fresh id; and make `propose_config` refuse until `ask_clarification` ran once, with a test that scripts a hostile model.
- [P7-02 / leftover jobs eat scripted answers] A test that cancelled sessions left their queued jobs, which then took the next scripted model answers and made a later test fail at random → wait for the job queue to be idle (`jobsIdle`) after a cancel instead of sleeping; never a timer.
- [P7-02 / do not cut a JSON answer in the middle] `describe_self` cut its text at the length limit, so the policy fields (what the team allows, the default guard) could be lost on a big team → put the policy fields first, halve the long lists until it fits and say what was left out.
- [P7-02 / a count in one place only] The PLAN headline, per-phase table, order-table row and phase header each carried a count, and an edit changed one of them; a spec row was also overwritten by a ledger row → sum the table to get the headline, and keep the spec rows (Task/Where/Spec/Agent) apart from the ledger rows.
- [P7-02 / a check then an insert is two steps] The limit of three open conversations was counted, then inserted, so parallel starts passed it → take a transaction advisory lock keyed on the person before counting; test with parallel requests.
- [P7-02 / a narrow door still needs a list] `capability.call` could call any capability another plugin registered → the manifest names the ones it may call (`calls`), the kernel refuses the rest, and the door cannot be combined with the run-closing or bot-acting ones.
- [P7-04 / a test persona that is not the role it names] The "lead" e2e test signed in as Omar, who is also a workspace admin, so it proved the admin path → check which persona holds only the role in the test name (Tariq leads Marketing and is a plain member) or rename the test.
- [P7-02 / a front-matter line pasted from the model] The helper's BOT.md had `role: Reads posts in #dev and replies` written unquoted, so YAML read `#` as a comment and stored the role cut at `#`; `: `, a leading `-`, `*`, `>`, `&`, `!`, `%`, `@`, quotes and a line break break a plain scalar the same way → generate the `name` and `role` lines in code (double-quoted, from plain arguments), read the file back with `parseBotMd` and refuse when the parsed value differs; test each of those characters and unicode.
- [P7-02 / the author cannot decline, only withdraw] The proposals service lets a person reject only what somebody else wrote, and the helper files in the asker's name, so the asker had no button to take a proposal back → show Withdraw (the proposals' own route) to the author, announce it, move focus to the heading, and test that Reject is absent.
- [P7-02 / an answer must not depend on the model's list] The limits list said "Not settled" for a gap the person had answered whenever the model listed the requirement again under a new id → an answer counts only when the platform recorded it against the requirement the platform asked; a hostile re-listing stays open; show "You decided: <answer>".
- [P7-02 / internal names in front of people] Reasons and questions showed tool, trigger and requirement ids (`messages.post`, `needs smart-and-slow`) → map to plain labels in code, check a model-worded question for a question mark, a short choice list and no internal name, ask the model once to reword it, then use the platform's own question.
- [P7-02 / a time limit set from a guess] Two minutes per model call ended sessions that were still working, because real turns took 80 to 230 seconds → measure on the real model, default 240 seconds, make it configurable with a clamp, document it.
- [P7-02 / a failure that throws the work away] A failed setup conversation could only be started again, losing every answer → keep the conversation and answers on the row and offer Try again (same session id, last round from stored data, same limits and owner checks).
- [P7-05 / a spend line with no reason] The ledger showed a bot name or nothing, so setup, Workshop and rehearsal calls looked like mystery rows → write a `purpose` on every line, name it on the Spend page, and show "Removed bot" when a bot id no longer resolves.
- [P7-05 / a handle from the caller] The sandbox of a rehearsal takes a run handle from the workshop plugin; a plain object can be made up or kept past the run's end → each member reloads the run's row and refuses anything that is not a running rehearsal of the same bot, team and asker; a held write is decided in the broker, never by the prompt.
- [P7-06 / the model decides it was asked for a rule] A rule is a standing instruction and must come from the person's own words → the platform looks for "make that a rule" in the person's latest message; the model's argument alone files no rule. The same for "this lesson repeats": a deterministic word-set similarity in code (Jaccard 0.6), documented, not the model's opinion.
- [P7-05 / a shell that refuses the long command] A worktree agent's shell refused compound `cd ... &&` and large heredoc commands as too complex → write a script file to the scratchpad and run it as one simple command, or use the Edit tool.
- [P7-06 / a word check that says yes to what it cannot read] The lesson check looked for English words, so a lesson in another script, or with a look-alike letter in the middle of "ignore", passed silently, and "never skip the review" was refused as if it skipped it → a check on words must refuse text it cannot read (with the reason in plain words), treat a negation just before the word as the opposite, and its docs must say it is a first filter and not the guard (the approval is).
- [P7-06 / similarity that ignores "not"] "Use metric units" and "Never use metric units" shared every word that counted, so they merged, repeated as one lesson and shared one test case → a negation is a marker that is part of the comparison: different polarity is never the same, and the case id is a hash of the whole normalised text.
- [P7-06 / a rewrite that deletes what it cannot read] Compaction rebuilt lessons.md from the `- ` lines only, so a hand-written section vanished without a word → a rewrite of a file a person also edits refuses when anything is not understood, and names every line it drops in the proposal.
- [P7-06 / a doc that promises another service's guarantee] The doc said "the proposals service allows one open proposal per file"; it does not, so two conversations could file on the same bot at once → check and insert under a lock the caller itself takes (an advisory lock on team and path), prove it with a parallel test that fails without the lock, and never write down a guarantee that was not read in that service's code.
- [P7-05 / a record written before the commit] The transcript said "proposal filed" before the batch committed, so a failure half way, or End during a filing tool, left lines for proposals that did not exist → write such entries after the commit and patch them when the conversation is ended mid-tool.
- [P7-05 / a cleanup that outruns a slow job] The hourly reaper uses the run's own short limit, so a rehearsal turn that is simply slow could be taken mid-turn → give rehearsal runs a floor (90 minutes) before the reaper takes them, and say so in the bots doc.
- [P7-05 / "counted apart" that is not] The doc said rehearsal spend is counted apart; it is only shown apart, and it still counts toward the bot's own budget (lines with a bot id) while Build and Live lines (no bot id) count only for team and workspace budgets → say which budget counts what, and group the Spend table by bot and purpose so the row reads "Rehearsal of <bot>".
- [P7-05 / aria-disabled and Playwright] Playwright treats `aria-disabled` as not clickable and waits for the whole test timeout → a test that clicks a disabled-looking tab on purpose uses `click({ force: true })`; and a tab row that moves between two screens must put focus on the chosen tab after the switch, name the panel with `aria-controls` and `aria-labelledby`, and have one live region, not two.
- [P7-05 / regex `.` and U+2028] In JavaScript `.` does not match U+2028 or U+2029, so a line separator hid the second half of a front-matter value from a check that read line by line → use `[^\n]` where every character must be read, refuse block scalars (`>`, `|`) and those separators in generated front matter, and note that the editor tools turn a `\uXXXX` written in content into the raw character: build such a regex with `String.fromCharCode` in a script.
- [P7-04 / a retry with no end] Try again on a failed setup conversation could be pressed forever on a model that always fails → count tries on the row (migration 0003) and refuse after three with a plain sentence.
- [P7-07 / `SET` in a plugin] The free replay opened with `SET LOCAL transaction_read_only = on` to make "cannot write" a database fact; the SDK's SQL guard refuses session settings from a plugin, so the route answered 500 → a plugin cannot set anything on its transaction: ask the server for it with the route option `readOnly: true` (`BEGIN READ ONLY` in `withActor`), and test that the database refuses a write even for the system role.
- [P7-07 / a lead reads less than "everything"] The replay test assumed a team lead sees every run of the bot, but a conversation run is its asker's alone (the `bot_runs` policy) → before writing "a lead sees all", read the table's policy; a feature built on a table inherits its reach, and the docs must say who is left out (here: other people's private conversations, `404` on a full replay).
- [P7-07 / no record to replay] The platform kept the held decisions and the refused calls but nothing for a decision that passed, and no input → "re-check the past" needs a record of the decision first; keep it content-blind (kind, stage, outcome, a message id read again with the viewer's own access), cap it per run, and say plainly what cannot be asked again (a local model's answer, a content scan, budgets).
- [P7-07 / a name that the lint bans] A variable called `wasModel` failed the rule that bans names ending in Model → run eslint on every new file before the first full test run; the ban applies to locals too.
- [P7-07 / `dollars()` rounding] A cost of $0.0124 printed as $0.01, which hides the very thing an estimate is for → show four decimals under ten cents.
- [P7-10 / a seed that rewrites a file] Seed steps are idempotent by commit message, so changing the text of an old step never reaches a repository seeded before → add a new step with its own message (and keep the old one as it was); a test that counts the files of a bot folder for every team must say which team gained files.
- [worktree shell] A worktree-isolated agent's shell refuses a compound `cd ... && ...` with a heredoc, a `$(...)` or `$(cat file)` inside a `pnpm` command, and `flock ... -t "a|b"`; write the script with the Write tool into the scratchpad and run it as one plain command, pass file lists as literal arguments, use patterns without `|`.
- [P7-07 review / a record of what a model wrote is not content-blind] The tool decision kept `path`/`target` straight from the model's arguments, so a secret the model put in a path argument would have been stored → keep a place only if it has the shape of one (a known first folder, plain characters, no long opaque run; a UUID or slug for ids) and mark the line `truncated` otherwise; test with a secret-looking path.
- [P7-07 review / compare like with like] The eval-drop notice compared a score with the last run of any set and any mode, and filed one for a first run under the minimum → compare only with the previous finished run of the same set and the same mode, and file nothing for a first run.
- [P7-07 review / a cap that hides is a lie] The 6000-decision read and the 80-question limit cut results silently, and the 81st question read as "permissions could not be checked" → return a `decisionsCapped` flag, say it in a note and on the screen, and give the over-limit case its own words.
- [P7-07 review / a confirmation of 0] The full replay's confirmation was the token count, so a zero-token estimate was confirmed by sending `0` → bind the confirmation to the run and the person with a short server hash and require it as well as the number.
- [P7-07 review / "lists none" means all] A bot with an empty `knowledge:` list searches every base of its team, so a base created or deleted changes its answers; the notice skipped those bots, and a silent 200-bot cap skipped others → match empty lists too and page through bots with no cap.
- [P7-07 review / a plugin test that sends many requests] The full replay is limited to 10 a minute per person; a test that asks 14 times gets `429` → wait out the window in the test helper, never raise the limit for the test.
- [P7-09 / a socket route that no one checked] Plugin WebSocket routes had only the cookie check at the upgrade: no `Origin` check, no way to refuse before the upgrade with a real status, and no link to the session, so a signed-out person kept the socket → one place in the server (`mountPluginRoutes`) now refuses a foreign `Origin` with 403, runs the route's `guard` as the caller before the upgrade, and closes the socket when the session ends; a new socket route gets those for free, but still needs its own role check in the `guard`.
- [P7-09 / a run that needs a person who is not there] A sandbox run opened with the admin as its asker failed ("the asker is not a person of that team") when the admin was not on the bot's team → a run made for a terminal has no asker; do not put a person on a run the person did not make.
- [P7-09 / a credential revoked "somewhere" is not proved] Closing the run was trusted to revoke its tokens → the function that opens the sandbox run checks, before it answers, that no token row is live, the run is not running and the minted token is refused by `resolve`, and throws (no terminal) otherwise; the test keeps the token and tries it, and a stub that closes without revoking must fail closed.
- [P7-09 / a flag that cannot be stored] A test tried a rehearsal flag on a webhook run to prove it is refused; the table's own check (`bot_runs_rehearsal_conversation`) already refuses the row, so the case cannot be built → test the shared predicate for it and the real runs for the rest.
- [P7-09 / node `http` upgrade and keep-alive] A hand-made upgrade request in a test failed with "socket hang up" because the default agent reused a connection → pass `agent: false` for upgrade requests in tests.
- [P7-09 / `pnpm add` in a worktree] `pnpm add` rewrote unrelated parts of the lockfile (extra desktop entries) → revert it and add only the importer lines for the new dependency by hand, then check `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` accepts it.
- [P7-09 / a count over a shared table] An audit assertion counted rows written by earlier tests and earlier runs for the same bot (the database outlives a run, and a later request sweeps stale sessions) → filter by time and compare what this session adds (one opened, at least one closed with its own words), not the table's total.
- [P7-09 / the shell sees what it should not] A jail that mounts `/proc` shows the root-owned helper that started it → mount it with `hidepid=2`, and test that the shell sees only its own processes and cannot signal the server's.
- [P7-09 / complex shell commands refused] In a worktree-isolated session a command with heredocs, `$(...)` or the word "eval" in a string was refused → write edit scripts with the file tools and run `python3 <file>`, and keep each command plain.
- [P7-06 / a repeat written as a duplicate] A lesson told again in other words was appended as a second line, though it counted as a repeat → the line that already says it gets the `(told N times)` count, no second copy is written, and the rule logic still runs.
- [P7-05 / a title cut in the middle of a word] A conversation's title was the first 80 characters, so the list showed "Then cr" → cut after a whole word and add an ellipsis (`titleOf`); and a page address that asks for a mode the person may not use lands on the mode they may, so no intro for the refused mode shows beside its refusal.
- [P7-05 / autofocus wins over the tab row] Going back to Chat with the arrow keys or Home put focus in the message box, not on the Chat tab → the message box does not take focus when the page was reached from the tab row.
- [P7-09 / a chroot is not a jail] The shell's root was entered with `chroot`; a process that can make a user namespace (`unshare -Ur`) or `chroot` again walks back out → `pivot_root` into the tmpfs and unmount the old root, drop the bounding set, and filter the system calls (seccomp) that make namespaces, mount, chroot or trace; the test runs `unshare -Ur` and a chroot escape inside the real jail.
- [P7-09 / an insert with no cap] The terminal text builder let `ESC[200@` insert 200 blanks with no limit, so a hostile shell printing it 100000 times never finished (and was fed synchronously) → cap the line, budget the bulk edits of one line, and test the timing of hostile input (100k inserts, long blank runs), each well under a second.
- [P7-09 / "plain mode" as the server's own user] The no-isolation mode ran bash as the server's uid when the server was not root → the helper refuses a mode that is not exactly `jail` or `plain`, and refuses to run a shell unless it is root (a test runs both as a non-root user).
- [P7-09 / a failure message on the shell's own terminal] The helper's fatal message went to stderr, which is the shell's terminal, so the shell could print "fatal:" and look like a failure → send it on a pipe of its own.
- [P7-09 / the parent-death signal is cleared by setuid] `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` is reset when the uid changes → set it after `setuid` (and check the parent is still the right one); and resume a paused output stream before killing, or a shell stuck on a full pipe is never reaped.
- [P7-09 / a refusal that depends on the machine] "This is a real run" came after the "can this machine run a shell" check, so on a machine that could not run one the answer was 409 and the test returned early → refuse by the data first (403), and write tests that do not return early when the machine cannot run the feature.
- [P7-09 / a cursor move that writes what it skips] The text builder wrote out the blanks between the text and the cursor when the next letter came, so `ESC[3999C` + `x` in a loop made 4000 cells from 8 bytes (10 s for each MiB; one 64 KB chunk blocked the event loop for a second) → the cursor may be moved at most 8 cells past the written text (also checked when a letter is put, so erasing a line under a far cursor does not bring it back), and the hostile timing tests cover moves, column jumps, erase-then-write and mixes; the list of kept lines is trimmed in steps, not by one splice per newline.
- [P7-07 / a deploy that leaves a bot without a host placement] The seed refresh at deploy rewrote Content drafter's `BOT.md` without its `placement:` block, so its real runs failed ("something went wrong on our side") while Brain answered → after every deploy run the FULL `node tools/devbox/provision-host-bots.mjs engineering marketing customer-support` (it sets the placement back; `--reserve` only serves profiles again), then ask each team's bot a real question.
- [coders / a worktree on an old commit] A coder's worktree started from an older commit and had no code to edit → tell the coder to check `git log` for the named commit first and merge it; the orchestrator takes only the coder's own files with `git diff <commit> -- <files>` (after `git add -N` for new files).
- [P8-11 / a test builds a table by hand and fails the lock-down check] A partition test made its old month with `CREATE TABLE … PARTITION OF` and the RLS harness then flagged the detached table (no RLS, no policy) → make a partition through the one function the code uses (`app.analytics_ensure_month`) so the test checks what production makes; and an `ON CONFLICT` on a `NULLS NOT DISTINCT` unique index is inferred from the same column list, so name every column of the index.
- [P8-01 / `app.is_system()` inside a definer function] `app.is_system()` reads `current_user`, and in a SECURITY DEFINER function owned by `manythreads_system` that is always `manythreads_system`, so a guard written with it let a workspace admin insert `has_credentials = true` → inside a definer function ask `session_user = 'manythreads_system'` (the connection's login) for "is the caller the system", and test the guard as an ordinary admin, not only as the system.
- [P8-01 / a tool that is both a handler and a source] Two places can answer one tool name (a capability handler and a tool source) and the call then depends on which is asked first → the kernel refuses the second registration in either order, a source may only offer tools its own manifest declares (the broker needs the name and the destructive tag), and the registry keeps a frozen copy so a later push to the list adds nothing.
- [P8-28 / a source reached outside a run] A tool source's `call` is a door to credentials; routing it by the transaction's actor alone would let any bot transaction call it with a run id it does not own → the run service reads the run from its own row (live, token not revoked), requires the transaction's actor to be that run's bot or its asker, and throws when there is no run; the source then re-checks the grant under the caller's own row-level rules.
- [P8-28 / a listing that trusts one side] A tool must be offered only when both the broker (the grant) and the source (a usable connection) say yes, and a source that throws must offer nothing → fail closed in `offeredTools`, and run `describeToolSourceIntersection` (packages/test-utils) for every new source: granted/not x asker sees/does not.
- [P8-28 / the same tool in two lists] A BOT.md that names `email.send` under `native` and under `connections` would give two grant rows for one tool → the BOT.md schema refuses it (also through a `ns.*` wildcard) and names the field; the compile writes one connection per tool per bot.
- [P8-01 / secrets in a table the app role can read] A sealed secret is still a credential: `connection_secrets` has no GRANT to `manythreads_app`, forced RLS for the system only, a guard that refuses a secret in another workspace, and a test that the app role gets 42501 → give a credential table no grant first, add policies second.
- [P8-01 / a view that is the real object] `ctx.toolSources.list()` and `.find()` returned the registry's own source, so every plugin with `tool.invoke` held `call`, the door to a connection's credentials, and could skip the run service's checks → hand out a fresh frozen view with only `id`, `tools` and `offered`; the real `call` stays in the registry for the run service; a kernel test lists the keys and tries to add `call`. When a seam hands a looking plugin an object, give it only what looking needs.
- [P8-28 / a read that logs only what the tool says] A source's `sourcesFrom` could answer an empty list, so a read of outside data left no line in the run's source log and the retention rule never saw it → the gateway always writes a `connection` line (narrowest scope) for a source tool's read, beside what `sourcesFrom` returns.
- [P8-28 / a wildcard compared as a plain name] The loader matched `approvals.needsApproval` against a connection's tools with `Set.has`, so `email.*` covered nothing and `email.send` ran with no approval → compare with `nativeCoversTool`, the one rule for names and `namespace.*`, and test the wildcard.
- [P8-28 / a schema shown but not enforced] The JSON Schema a model is shown for a source's tool was never checked on a call → define the input once as a strict zod object, generate the JSON Schema from it and parse every call before a kind sees it; refusals name fields and never repeat a value.
- [P8-01 / a pointer table that owns sealed data] Cascades (team, owner, workspace) deleted `connection_secrets` rows but left the ciphertext in `app.secrets` → an AFTER DELETE definer trigger (pinned search_path, system-owned) removes the sealed row; test the direct, connection, team and workspace paths.

- [P8-13 / a space and a hash in a plain YAML line] An example question written as `What is waiting in #escalations that nobody has picked up?` is stored by YAML as `What is waiting in` (a space and a hash start a comment), so the bot would have shown a cut-off chip; the editor-form test (render the parsed file and compare bytes) caught it, nothing else would → in `conversation.prompts`, `role` and `name` never write a channel name in plain text; say "the escalations channel", and run the template tests after every new BOT.md.
- [P8-13 / a handover with only people also compiles a grant] The bots loader compiles `tasks.handoff` from `handover.mayTag` OR `handover.toPerson`, but the template test expected the grant only when `mayTag` was set, so a bot with only `toPerson` would fail the roster test while the loader was right → the test follows the loader now; and a bot that never hands a card over (KB gardener) gets no `handover` block, so it holds no grant it never uses.
- [P8-13 / a template bot's tool list must be checked against what exists] The spec's roles (Social scheduler publishes, Figma watcher reads Figma, Literature scout searches the web) name tools no connection provides yet; declaring them would be inventing tools → give each only the native tools that exist, say what is not built in a `What is not built yet` section of the body, and let a test pin that no new bot declares a connection or a runtime tool. Experiment tracker first listed `tasks.describe`, which needs a live claim the bot never takes → read a tool's rule in docs/plugins/tasks.md before putting it in a list.
- [P8-13 env] A fresh worktree has no `node_modules` (`vitest: not found`) → `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` there takes seconds. Free-mode check of one bot's scenario set: `pnpm -s test:evals -- --bot=<slug>`.
- [P8-06 / a credential put in the same transaction as its connection] The sealed credential is written by `putConnectionSecret` in its own system transaction, which cannot see a connection the request has not committed, so a "create with credential" route fails with a foreign-key error → create the connection first, then put the credential in a second call, then switch it on (three calls from the client); a switch-on with nothing stored answers 409 in words.
- [P8-06 / a change that leaves no line] The audit event for a credential write must be emitted before the sealed write and carry no value and no display name, or a failed write leaves no trace and a successful one leaks a name → emit first, ids and the kind of change only, and have a test search the events table and the server log for a sentinel credential.
- [P8-10 / a setup prompt in a bot folder] The template loader allows only `BOT.md` and `evals/<set>.yaml` in a bot folder, and the folder must be named like the slug in `template.yaml` (`enquiry-bot`, not `enquiry`) → keep setup prompts in `templates/<template>/setup/<slug>.md`, outside the bot folder, and check the slug before creating the folder. The Coder bot has no scenario set, so a test that asks for one on every bot of every template fails.

- [P8-30 / a stamp that needed a thread] The plan said to link a mail to the bot's post "through `meta.runId`", but the `messages.post` capability stamped `meta.runId` only when the run had a reply root, and an `inbox` (or `routine`) run has none, so the post carried no run and the link was never made (the claim stayed unlinked, no error anywhere) → read the code that writes a field before building on it; the stamp now needs only the run's channel, and a test follows a real run from the delivered mail to the linked post.
- [P8-30 / a run that fails before it starts says "something went wrong"] A test bot without `model.aliases` failed in the profile renderer with the generic notice and no log line in the test output → when a run fails at once, add a temporary `console.log` of the error in the run job's catch (remove it), and give every hermes test bot `model: aliases: [smart]`.
- [P8-29 / the recipient of a send] A send that takes the address from the model (even checked afterwards) lets a mail body steer who is written to → `email.send` takes only the text; the tool takes the recipient from the stored first inbound mail of the thread, and the database function that writes the row takes it from the stored mail again and ignores the argument. A later mail in the thread, from anyone, never changes who a thread belongs to.
- [P8-30 / two bots, one old mail] A bot loaded after mail arrived claims the mail still inside the 48-hour look-back, so a test that picks "the bot's linked claim" without naming the thread sends the approved reply to an older thread → select the claim by thread key in tests, and say the look-back in the docs.
- [P8-29 / test order in one RLS file] "A bot with no grant" was tested with a bot an earlier test in the same file had granted that tool to → make a fresh bot actor in the test that needs one with no grants.
- [P8-29 / `a@b.c` is not an address] zod's `email()` wants a two-letter top level; test fixtures use `a@b.co`.
- [P8-16 / worker-thread files are erasable TypeScript only] A worker file written with a constructor parameter property (`constructor(private x)`) failed to load under Node's own type stripping, which loads the worker without the build tool → worker files (and everything they import) use only syntax that disappears when types are removed: no parameter properties, enums or namespaces; the extractor test loads the real worker.
- [P8-16 / move shared code once, keep the old path] The extractor lived in the knowledge plugin and the inbox needed it, but a plugin may not import another plugin → move it to the SDK (`@manythreads/sdk/extract`) and leave a one-line re-export where the old path was, so the knowledge plugin's imports and tests do not change.
- [P8-16 / test helper signatures] `expectLinear(prepare, run, { n, label })` is synchronous; awaiting it in a test hid a failure as a hang → read the helper's signature before using it.
- [P8-14 / a definer function that trusts `is_system()`] Inside a definer function `app.is_system()` is true for everyone, because the function runs as the system role → check the caller with `app.actor()` (kind and id) and test it as a person, as another bot, and as a bot of another run.
- [P8-17 / a job that dedupes on its own key] A step that re-enqueued itself with the dedupe key it was running under got its own running job back, so the retry was never queued → a "look again later" uses a plain `runAt` with no dedupe key; only the first run of a step is deduped.
- [P8-17 / cron handlers return nothing] A cron handler that returned the count it moved failed the type check (`void | Promise<void>` is required) → wrap the helper in an `async` handler that awaits it, and keep the counting function exported for tests.
- [P8-14 / RLS tests are their own project] `vitest --project unit` found no test in `test/rls/` (the unit project excludes it) and looked like a pass → run RLS files with `--project rls`; event contract tests with `--project events`.
- [P8-14 / people and actors are hidden from other people] A list cannot join to `actors` or `people` to show who dropped something, because a caller sees only their own row → store the dropper's display name on the item when it is dropped; and a live push to a person works only from system code (a route cannot look the person up), so the pipeline sends pushes and the lead-driven routes do not.
- [P8-15 / a stalled upload ends with a closed socket] The idle timer destroys the connection (so the rest of the body is not read), so a test that waits for a 400 hangs with "socket hang up" → accept status 0 or 400 and assert that no row and no blob remain.
- [P8-15 / per-person rate limits and a test file] A test file that drops more than the per-minute limit in total started getting 429 only when the machine was loaded (the minute window held more tests) → reset the in-process limiter before each test and test the limit in one test of its own.
- [P8-17 / a stand-in context needs a clean table] Pipeline step tests share one database, and the two-at-once rule counts the team's other running items, so an earlier test's `filing` item made a later step wait → delete the table's rows before each test, and seed rows that satisfy the table's checks (a filed item needs a commit hash, a held one a reason).
- [P8-14 / a test that fakes a clock column] A guard trigger sets `updated_at` on every update, so a test cannot age a row by updating it → set `updated_at` (and `not_before`) in the INSERT.
- [P8-29/P8-30 review] A tool whose recipient was "platform-owned" still let the model steer it: the link between a mail and the bot's post took any post (an explicit `threadRootId` bound the claim to someone else's thread), the definer writer accepted any inbound mail of the connection, `email.read` read the whole mailbox and `email.send` needed no approval, and the "which mail is behind this run" answer depended on a race (the run had to post first) → bind every platform-owned fact to the transaction's own run in ONE definer function (`app.connection_mail_claims_of_run`), link only what the platform can prove (a top-level post), narrow reads to the conversation's thread, and hold the irreversible tool for a person (a source can now do that itself: `ToolSource.approval`). Also: `meta.runId` on every top-level post made a routine run look as if it had answered; restrict a stamp to the runs that need it, and test with a run of each trigger.
- [P8-21 / the OAuth error redirect names no provider] A sentence for `?connect_error=` that was built with the provider's name could never be filled, because the callback's error redirect carries only the code → write each error sentence without a provider name, and name the provider only for `?connected=<provider>`.
- [P8-21 / a web screen's stylesheet and the shared classes] A `.x input` rule placed after a shared form rule such as `.cn-form .fld.mono input` failed stylelint `no-descending-specificity` → give the element its own class (`.cp-box`) instead of styling it by tag, and write the lower-specificity rule first.

- [P8-19 / a header proves nothing] The plan said the door passes a mail when "SPF/DKIM pass", and `sampleEml()` carries an `Authentication-Results: ... spf=pass; dkim=pass` header, so reading it would let anyone forge a pass → the verdict comes only from the mail transport, out of band (the query of the delivery); no header inside a message is ever trusted, no result counts as a failed check, and a test sends a forged `Authentication-Results` and expects the item to be held.
- [P8-19 / a forged mail carries a real name] Crediting the item to the person whose address is in `From` before the check is known puts a stranger's mail under a colleague's name → credit the dropper only when the check passed and the person belongs to the team; every other mail has no dropper.
- [P8-19, P8-20 / a table that points at another plugin's table] The RLS test database holds only the plugin's own migrations and the ones it lists, so a foreign key to `app.connections` or `app.tasks` makes the migration fail there → keep such a column a plain uuid, and check the link in the code that writes it (the card must exist, be this team's and be made by this person).
- [P8-18 / key order in BOT.md] `renderBotMd` writes the frontmatter in a fixed order, and a template test compares each file byte for byte → put `contract:` after `placement:` and before `evals:`, and run the templates test after adding any key.
- [P8-35 / a `#` in a plain YAML value] A bot `role: Answers each email in #support-inbox ...` was read as "Answers each email in" followed by a comment, and the BOT.md editor round-trip test failed → a front-matter value that holds ` #` (a channel name, say) goes in double quotes.
- [P8-34 / a refusal the page must read] The web client drops the body of an error answer, so a deploy refused with a 409 would show no reason → the Deploy route answers 200 with three outcomes and the refusal is one of them; only a request that is wrong as a whole is an HTTP error.
- [P8-18 / a second bot with the `drop` trigger] The inbox asks for "the team's Archivist" by trigger, so a bot made in the setup helper with `triggers: [{ type: drop }]` would have competed with it for every dropped item → the helper's policy refuses a `drop` trigger (like `inbox` and `webhook`), and the drop run picks a bot called `archivist` first.
- [P8-18 / two drop runs of one bot] A gateway profile is addressed by the bot, not the run (`openRunOf`), and the inbox works two items of a team at once, so two runs of one Archivist could overlap → open the run under a transaction advisory lock on the bot and, if a run is open, answer `budget` (try again in 30 seconds) instead of starting a second.
- [P8-18 / a stand-in script that matches names] A stand-in model script that chose its answer by `name.includes('empty')` never matched a note called "Empty one" and every case filed the same concept → lower-case the name before matching, and give each case a visible marker in the test.
- [P8-21 / the mock keeps its state per transport] A web test that called `as('nadia')` after switching the mailbox on as an admin found it off again, because `createMockTransport` starts a new mock world → do the whole flow as one person (an admin who is also a team lead), and only switch person for a separate test.
- [P8-20 / a card made from the Inbox needs the tasks create call in the mock] The mock transport had no tasks routes, so "Add to board" had nothing to call offline → the inbox mock also answers `POST /api/teams/:slug/tasks` (the one task call the Inbox makes) with a schema-valid card (`state: 'open'`, branded ids made with `TaskId.parse`).
- [P8-19 / the email address is not in the item list] The list answer used to be read for an `emailAddress` the server never sent; the address comes only from `GET /api/teams/:slug/inbox/email` (with `canSetUp`) → read it there, and never invent a field on another route's answer (the e2e check read the old field too).
- [P8-36 / who may approve a mail-backed send] A journey spec let "whoever sees the card" approve it, but seeing is not deciding: with no `approvals.approvers` on the bot, only team leads and workspace admins decide, so a member such as Sameera gets 403 → assert the 403 for the member, then approve as the admin; and when a spec edits a file by splicing on a comment, use the last match before the marker (`rindex`), because the same comment can occur earlier in the file.
- [P8-22/P8-23 / a team a person cannot see has no name for them] A share's page shows the destination teams, but a person who is not in a team (and is not an admin) cannot read its row, so a join to `teams` would show nothing for the team that is getting the digest → store the destination's slug and name on the link row when the file is loaded.
- [P8-23 / the bundle check is the P5-24 parsers, composed] A second set of rules for "is this digest well formed" would drift from what the receiving side accepts → `checkOkfBundle` in `shared/okf/conformance.ts` only calls the existing parsers (concept, log, index) and adds the digest rules (every footnote has a source, every source a footnote, no `verified`, at most one source, no way into `references/`); the receiving task runs the same function. Test every pattern in it with `expectLinear`.
- [P8-23 / a settle step that must set the approval's outcome] An approved hand-back left `queued` is swept to `failed` after 15 minutes, which would have shown the owner a failed Approvals row for a digest that is fine → the settle function sets `outcome = 'ran'` on approve.
- [P8-23 review / a `[^...]` pattern under a global regex is quadratic] The footnote finder `/\[\^([^\]\s]+)\]/g` took a second on 80 KB of `[^[^[^...` because every `[^` opener scanned to the end before failing → scan with `indexOf('[^')` that stops at the first `]` or whitespace and moves on past it; test every pattern with `expectLinear` using the shape that opens but never closes.
- [P8-23 review / copy fields that skip the guard] The data-rules pass covered title, description and body, while tags, resource, type, stale_after and the other copied frontmatter left unchecked → run the guard over every finished file of the bundle (and block on a stop or a redaction), not over the fields someone remembered; add a test that puts a secret in each copied field.
- [P8-23 review / a "no links into references/" rule written as one regex] The rule missed `](<../references/a b.pdf>)`, `/memory/references/`, `a/../references/`, `%2e%2e/`, `References/` and HTML href/src → one helper (`pointsIntoReferences`) normalises the address (angle brackets, percent-decoding, case, `..`, a `memory/` prefix, backslashes) and both the strip and the conformance check call it.
- [P8-23 review / a decision made once at load goes stale] `approval: auto` was decided when the file was loaded, so a lead who was later demoted or suspended still switched the review off → check the rule again at the moment it matters (`mayAutoApprove` at build time).
- [P8-23 review / a sent name is not today's name] A retired concept was named by its current title, which may now be private → send only what an earlier digest could have carried (a name derived from the path).
- [P8-18 / a bot's open run was only a job's run, live p0712] `openRunOf` (the run a gateway profile is attributed to when it calls with the bot's token) required `job_id IS NOT NULL`, and the drop run is opened in-process with no job, so every `inbox.read` / `memory.recall` of the Archivist was answered `no_open_run` (an `isError` with no log line, no decision row), the Archivist never read the item and answered with no list; the drop busy check, which also asks `openRunOf`, never saw an open drop run either. The drop test used a fake CLI Hermes, which calls no tool → a run that is opened without a job must be named in `openRunOf` (it now accepts `trigger_type = 'drop'`), every error answer the gateway makes itself is logged (`refusal()` in gateway-mcp `protocol.ts`), and a drop test calls the tools at `/mcp` with the bot token while the run is open.

- [P15-03 / a cron job that opens its own transactions] A sweep that was handed the job's transaction and then opened a per-workspace system transaction inside it could wait on rows the first transaction held → a cron job that needs more than one transaction is `detached: true`, takes `job.transaction()` for its first step and runs the next ones one after the other; never a second transaction inside a still-open first.
- [P15-02 / a signed notice that adds people] Handling a media server's "participant joined" notice by adding the person to the room would let anyone with an unexpired token back in after they were removed → a notice may only remove or re-kick; people enter the room through the join route (which checks access) and never through a webhook. Also compare room times with the database clock (`now()`), not the app's.
- [P15-03 / test-only plugins and `TEST_ONLY_PLUGIN_DIRS`] A fake plugin that the server finds by its folder loads in production unless it is listed in `TEST_ONLY_PLUGIN_DIRS` (`packages/server/src/start.ts`) → list every new fake there, and keep its handle on `globalThis` (`Symbol.for`) so the test and the plugin share one object even when the module is loaded twice.
- [P15-01 / an RLS test needs a real non-person actor] `INSERT ... SELECT ... WHERE kind = 'system' LIMIT 1` matched no row and so raised no error, and the test passed for the wrong reason (or failed with "undefined") → create the bot actor in the test and insert by its id.
- [P8-04 / a long-lived credential handed back by the other side] A Kubernetes `TokenRequest` answers with the token's real end, and a cluster that stretches token life gives a token valid for a year however short the request → read the answer's expiry, not the request's, and throw the token away past one hour (plus a minute of clock difference); the same for the certificate a CA signs (parse its `valid_before`), its key (must be the one made for the call) and its principals (only the one asked for).
- [P8-04 / a command tool is not destructive if the list is the administrator's] `ssh.run` could not be tagged destructive (the compile would skip it) yet runs commands → it accepts only lines the connection's administrator wrote, word for word, on listed hosts, and refuses before any certificate exists; a test sends near-misses (trailing space, upper case, a second command after `;` or a newline) and counts the CA's requests.
- [P9-03 / error boundaries and the server renderer] React's `renderToStaticMarkup` does not run error boundaries, so a test that throws inside a view fails instead of showing the fallback → test a boundary through `getDerivedStateFromError` and `render()`, and leave "a throwing view costs one part" for a browser check.
- [P9-01 / a title CHECK copied from another table] The line-break characters in a copied `title !~ '[...]'` pattern are real U+2028/U+2029 and become plain spaces when retyped, which would reject every title with a space → write them as `  ` in the regex.
- [P8-04 / a timeout that waits for the pipes] Killing `sh -c 'sleep 30'` left `sleep` holding the output pipes, so the `close` event (and the answer) came 30 s late → answer on the deadline, kill, and destroy the streams; do not wait for `close`.
- [P8-04 / a plugin cannot read a request's uncommitted row] A route that makes a connection and then compiles the bots that wait for it in a system transaction does not see the row (the request has not committed) → do the follow-up on the next request of the set-up (credentials), or from an event, never in the same handler.
- [P8-05 / checks need an app] GitHub lets only a GitHub App write a check run; a personal token gets 403 → the report goes out as a check run for an app token and as a commit status for any other.
- [P15-05 / a read route that is not read-only] The repo provider writes the repository on first use, so a route that reads `TEAM.md` through it failed in a read-only transaction and the cap came out as `off` → a route that reads a file through `ctx.providers.repo` is not `readOnly`; read the branch by name (`main`), not `HEAD`.
- [P15-05 / an enum order that must match the CHECK] The schema-enums test compares the Zod enum order with the SQL `CHECK (... IN (...))` order, so a new column whose CHECK listed the values in a different order failed it → write the CHECK in the Zod order and add the column to `ENUM_COLUMNS`.
- [P15-05 / a test that awaits a hand-stepped clock] A test awaited `rejoin()`, whose first step is a fake `setTimeout(0)`, and hung for 30 s because nothing advanced the clock → start the call without awaiting, advance the fake clock, then await it.

- [P16-02 / a client-secret table and the app role] Workspace OAuth app secrets are sealed through `ctx.secrets` with a pointer table the application role cannot read, and the app role has only SELECT on the apps table (UPDATE/DELETE answer 42501) → writes go through `deps.db.system`, and a system delete must name the workspace (`is_system` sees every workspace). A reap trigger removes the sealed row with its pointer; test replace, clear and workspace delete.
- [P16-02 / a "nothing new to ask" answer invented for a route] Answering 409 when an incremental connect named no new service broke the existing flow (reconnect of the same services) → with no delta, ask the named services as they are; only a dead sign-in unions old and new scopes.
- [P16-02 / typecheck reads `Record<string, unknown>`] A row type declared with `interface` does not satisfy the `query<T extends Record<string, unknown>>` constraint → use a `type` alias for row shapes; and `ctx.providers.get` may be undefined, so guard it.

- [P8-24 / a definer function checks its caller itself] A SECURITY DEFINER function runs as the system role, so `app.is_system()` and `is_workspace_admin()` are true inside it and say nothing about the caller → test `session_user` for the platform, otherwise `coalesce(app.lookup_can_team(team, 'manage'), false)` on the team the RUN belongs to (never the team named in the call), and answer a missing row and a forbidden row alike (42501) so ids cannot be probed; write the forged-caller tests (destination lead, sender member, third team, guest) with the function.
- [P8-24 / a queue belongs to its plugin] memory-share could not enqueue `memory-inbox.hold` for the item it delivered (queue names are `<own-plugin>.<name>`) → announce the fact (`memory.digest.delivered`) and let the owning plugin queue its own step from a subscriber.
- [P8-25 / a zip's file type is in the central directory] The inbox's zip reader hides the entry's attributes, so a symbolic link inside a bundle looks like a one-line text file → an importer reads `versionMadeBy >> 8 === 3` and the top 16 bits of the external attributes itself, refuses anything but a plain file, caps the inflate at the declared size, and compares the local header's name with the directory's.
- [P8-25 / the shared conformance check must not throw] `checkOkfBundle` called `toJS` on a root `index.md` that used a YAML alias and threw instead of reporting a problem, which would have failed an import job over and over → hostile input to a "never throws" function gets a test of its own.
- [P8-24 / a state that now moves on] Once delivery exists a `ready` run becomes `sent` within seconds, so tests that waited for `ready` and then asserted it were racing → wait for the end state (`sent`), not the one before it.
- [P16-03 / a Gmail metadata read answers only the headers asked for] `gmail.search` asked for `metadataHeaders=From` and showed `subject` and `date` as null against the real API (the fake answered every header, so the test passed) → ask for `From`, `Subject` and `Date` by name (the API helper repeats a list-valued query parameter), and make the fake filter headers the way Google does.
- [P16-03 / a send is not a draft] A tool that sends names recipients the model chose, so the approval card is the only guard against a mail that talks a bot into writing → build the card from the arguments that passed the schema (every recipient, the subject, the text), allow no `bcc`, refuse whitespace in addresses and subjects, and take reply headers only as `<id>`; test that a header-injection attempt is refused before any provider call.
- [P16-03 / a label tool that deletes] `TRASH` and `SPAM` are labels, so a "label" tool can delete or hide mail while the catalog says it is not destructive → the tool refuses to add them (and to remove `SENT`/`DRAFT`), and a test names each.
- [P16-03 / a trigger needs an identity] A new-mail trigger on a person's mailbox cannot be finished by polling alone: the run it starts has no asker and person tools need one → decide whose identity the run has before building the router, and keep the catalog trigger `available: false` until then.
- [P16-03 / a circular import between tool files] Moving the Google tools to their own file while they used helpers defined in the file that imports them would read constants before they exist → put the shared helpers in a third file (`person-kit.ts`) that both import.
- [P8-26 / accepting a share has no route of its own] The server has no accept route: a request to receive is answered by approving or denying its Approvals row (`approvalId` on the incoming link), and pause/resume/end are the only `/incoming/:linkId/:action` routes → the web page calls the Approvals decision for Accept and Decline; do not look for or invent an accept route. Also, a worktree can be behind main (the P8-24/25 routes were missing from what I first read) → `git merge --ff-only main` before reading routes.
- [P8-26 / a load that arrives later than the click] A share file is read by the loader after the proposal is applied, so the list does not show a new share at once → the page polls for about 20 seconds after New share instead of showing nothing; a mock that answers at once hides this, so say so in the verification steps.
- [P16-04 / Microsoft Graph rules that a fake can hide] Graph refuses `$search` with `$filter` (a 400), so a search with words applies the other filters itself; `sendMail` cannot keep a message in a conversation, so a reply goes `createReply` → PATCH the draft to the approved recipients (empty bcc and reply-to) → send; a PATCH of `categories` replaces the whole list, so read it first; free/busy needs `Calendars.ReadBasic` and `masterCategories` needs `MailboxSettings.Read`; Outlook cannot skip emailing guests → make the fake answer the same 400s and say so on the approval card.
- [P16-04 / the approval card is clipped] The approvals store keeps 300 characters of a string and ten items of a list in a preview (and flags it), so a card that "shows every recipient and 1,500 characters" shows less than the tool built → cap what must be read in full (ten recipients for a send), put every address in the one-line summary too, and test the stored card, not only the tool's. (Gmail's send, P16-03, allows 25 recipients and has the same clip: raise with the owner.)
- [P16-04 / a fake token must carry what the person agreed before] Microsoft's token holds every permission given to the app, but the fake issued only the scopes of the last consent, so a second sign-in broke the first service's tool with a 403 → the fake unions earlier consents for Microsoft.
- [P9-04 / no `max()` over a uuid] Postgres has no `max(uuid)`, so "newest line" written as `max(id)` failed the migration → use `ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1`. uuidv7 ids sort by time only roughly (two connections in the same millisecond are unordered), so "after the last update" by id order is safe only if the writers take turns: the click route reads the surface `FOR SHARE` and `surface.update` takes `FOR UPDATE`. Also: a bot in a test world needs `messages.post` or its run fails when the runtime posts the reply, even if the test only calls other tools.

- [P8-27 / a seed that goes through the routes must be rerunnable] A failed inbox item (the photo) is not a duplicate source, so dropping it again made a second item, and committing the same share file again answers 400 "does not change the file" → look the item up by name before dropping, and treat that 400 as done.
- [P8-27 / the person who wrote a share can never accept it] Seeding an accepted share through the real Approvals row is impossible when the only lead or admin of the destination is the writer (Omar) → settle the link and its accept row in the database (decision row, approved, outcome ran) and say so in the code and the docs.
- [P8-27 / an MCP run's `url` is no base for other routes] The cross-team tests appended `/close` to `new URL(run.url).pathname`, which 404s since the run URL became the per-team host form → use `/api/mcp/runs/<runId>/close`.
- [P8-27 / a share slug is in the digest] A test that looked for the destination team names in the bundle matched the share's own slug (`share:` in `digest.md`) → name test shares without team names.
- [P8-27 / a queue constant that no job uses] `EXPIRE_QUEUE` in memory-share `util.ts` is never registered (expiry happens in the tick); the docs test checks only queues that `jobs.ts` registers. Remove the constant when next in that file.
- [P18-01 / a new folder under templates/ is read as a team template] `loadTemplates` takes every folder of `templates/` for a team template, so `templates/recipes/` would have been loaded (and refused) as one → skip `recipes` next to `_*`, and test that the loader ignores it.
- [P18-01 / a rules bot cannot serve a routine] The routine runner writes the page from the bot's reply and a rules runtime has no reply → the recipe validator requires a Hermes bot, and a test names the rules case; do not ship a rules recipe.
- [P18-01 / a held approval fails a scheduled firing] A scheduled run that stops for approval fails its firing, so a recipe whose point is "ask first" (invoice approval) cannot rely on `needsApproval` → recipes keep `needsApproval: []`, write a page and cards, and leave the decision to people.
- [P18-01 / a shipped BOT.md must equal its render] `renderBotMd` is deterministic and the loader's tests compare it → generate recipe files from data and re-render, never hand-edit a BOT.md.
- [P18-01 / one schema for two readers] The rhythms scheduler and the recipe validator each needed the routine contract → move `RoutineContract` to shared and re-export it from the plugin, so a recipe that validates is one the scheduler accepts.
- [worktree agents / heredocs and a fresh worktree] The shell refused heredoc file writes (use the Write tool), a fresh worktree needs `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` before any test, and `node` needs `--experimental-transform-types` to run TypeScript scripts that import shared source.
- [P9-05 / a part's values share one budget] The checker counts every value of a part against 20,000 characters, so a Table of 50 rows by 10 cells of 300 characters is refused as `too_large` by the checker, not by the schema, and a DiffView cap near 20,000 would never be reachable → set each schema cap well under the budget and test the combined worst case through `parseSurface`.
- [P9-05 / a view with a lookup is a component] `SurfaceView` functions are called inside one `ViewHost`, so a hook in a view would be shared by whatever view that slot draws next while a surface streams → views that read the host context return a small component element (`<PersonChipView/>`) and keep the hook there.
- [P9-05 / a diff header is not a change] Reading every line that starts with `+` or `-` as added or removed counts `+++ b/x` and `--- a/x` as changes → lines before the first `@@` (and after a `diff ` line) are headers; only inside a hunk the first character decides.
- [P9-05 / a shared scratchpad file name] Helper scripts written to the session scratchpad with a common name (`fix4.py`) can belong to another agent working in the same scratchpad and be run by mistake → give helper scripts a task-specific name, and prefer the Edit tool for edits.

- [P16-06 / a provider id is also a connection slug] The first id for GitHub Issues, `github_issues`, passed every unit test and failed only in the database test: a connection's slug is its provider id and the slug check allows no underscore, so the sign-in failed after the tokens were traded → pick provider ids that are valid slugs (`githubissues`), and always run one sign-in through the real routes, not only the tools against a stub.
- [P16-06 / the read scopes of a write service] The Asana write service listed only the write scopes, so a write-only grant could not read the story it commented on → a write service lists the read scopes its tools need too; the catalog-against-tool test catches it.
- [P16-06 / an approval card is cut at 300 characters a string] A comment or issue body longer than that shows only its start, and a hostile text can push its point past the cut → lead the summary with tracker, act and target (never with model text), show the text from its start cut at 280 with its full length beside it, and test that no string of the card runs past 300.
- [P16-06 / GraphQL and search words] Putting the model's words into a query text lets them become part of the query (Linear, Jira JQL, GitHub qualifiers) → send values as GraphQL variables, turn Jira operators and quotes to spaces, drop colons from GitHub words, and test with a hostile string; a GraphQL `errors` list can quote the request, so never pass it on.
- [P16-06 / a state change on a number] GitHub treats a pull request as an issue, so an "issue close" tool can close a pull request → read the item first and refuse a pull request; test that nothing was changed.
- [P16-06 / ids that go into a path] An Atlassian site id comes from the API and goes into the next path → match it whole against its shape (a UUID) before use, like any other id.
- [P16-06 / one error class for a whole GraphQL errors list] Mapping every Linear error to "not found" hid a lost sign-in, a rate limit and a permission failure behind the wrong words and skipped the one token renewal → read the kind from the error's `extensions`, give each kind its own status, and let the api renew once on a lost sign-in found in a 200 body.
- [P16-06 / a transition's own name is not its destination] Matching a Jira step by its name as well as by the status it ends in let a step called "Done" move an issue somewhere else than the card said → match only the status a step ends in, and pick the lowest id when several end there.
- [P16-06 / silent cuts between the card and the call] Cutting a long text to 100 paragraphs or 5000 characters when building the Atlassian Document meant the text run was not the text approved → never cut after approval: the schema bounds the size, and the conversion keeps every character.
- [P18-01 web] The brief asked for a typed parameters form, but the recipe API has none (`InstallRecipeRequest` is an empty strict object; `RecipeParams` is only the route's `slug` and `recipeId`) → read the schema before designing the form, and show the facts a person must read (schedule, needs, abilities, files) instead of inventing fields. Also: the server lets any member who can post file the install proposals, while only a lead or admin can approve them; the gallery enables Install only for people who can approve, with the reason in plain text beside the off button (a test per reason in `recipes.test.ts`).
- [P16-07 / a per-customer host is built, never taken] Zendesk and Salesforce live at the customer's own address, so the admin's setting is only a subdomain label (letters, digits, single hyphens, at most 63), checked on the way in and again every time it is used, and the host is `https://<label><the provider's own suffix>`; a dot, slash, `@`, port, scheme or double hyphen builds nothing → test each of those and the fake base (which may carry no path), not only a good label. Re-checking at use matters because the column is shared with Microsoft's directory setting.
- [P16-07 / the approver must read all of the text] A reply or note up to 2,000 characters on a card that the store clips at 300 characters a string hides what goes out → keep the one-line summary under 300 (act and target first), put the whole text in the preview as pieces of at most 250 characters (eight at most, under the ten-item list clip), cap the text so it fits, and test the stored card (`app.approvals`), not the tool's card. A model-supplied field named like `private_note` is also refused by the store, so the tool has no such field at all: an internal or public note is a different tool.
- [P16-07 / text that renders as HTML] Intercom and HubSpot show a note as HTML, so what the approver read as `<b>x</b>` would render as bold → escape `& < > "` and turn line breaks into `<br>` before sending; reject control, line-separator and bidi-override characters up front, since they make the stored text read differently from how it is shown. Quote a search for its language (Zendesk phrase, SOQL literal with `LIKE` wildcards escaped) and make the fake refuse an unbalanced quote so a missed escape fails the test.
- [P16-07 / each provider's token is different] Salesforce answers a refresh without a new refresh token and without a lifetime, Zendesk wants JSON and PKCE, HubSpot rotates nothing but expires in 30 minutes, Intercom never refreshes and has no scope parameter (its scopes are our own pseudo-scopes) and refuses a call without its version header → one table of per-provider facts (`PROVIDERS`) and a fake that behaves each way, and a test per way (a 401 renews once; Intercom says "connect again").

- [P9-06 / a plugin cannot set a statement timeout] The plugin SQL guard forbids `SET`, `set_config` and transaction words, and the repo had no statement timeout anywhere, so "a timeout on the viewer's read" had no way to be written in plugin code → added the kernel options `readOnly` and `statementTimeoutMs` to `withActor` and `ctx.db.as` (an integer written into `SET LOCAL` after BEGIN, clamped 1 ms to 10 min); a provider that needs a bound opens its own transaction with them.
- [P9-06 / a provider member takes the transaction, not an id] A "read as this person" member that takes an actor id would let any caller name anyone → `readForViewer(tx, ...)` takes the caller's own `PluginTx` and refuses unless `tx.actor.kind` is `person`; then it opens its own read-only transaction for that same actor. Test it with a system and a bot transaction.
- [P9-06 / the bot's grants must not decide a viewer's read] Calling the normal `callTool` path for a bound part would ask the broker about the bot and run in a bot's run → the viewer path asks the broker about the person and has no run; the test uses a bot with no grant at all and a person of another team to prove both directions.
- [P9-06 / `bind` is positional, and the placeholder must pass the schema] The binding is the last value of the part, so the placeholders before it must be filled (`[]`, `null`) and still pass the part's schema; the binding itself is pulled out before the props schema runs (the schemas are strict, and a `.refine` on a schema also hides `.extend`), and a bind key a part does not list is refused, not dropped.
- [P9-06 / a tool's answer is not a part's value] `hits[].snippet` is a list, so it cannot fill a `Text`; a number cannot fill a text value without being turned into text; a table cell that is an object is not a cell → `applyBinding` turns numbers into text where the bot wrote text, makes non-plain cells empty, and then lets the part's own schema judge; a mismatch is told in one plain sentence that never echoes the values.
- [P9-06 / which events refresh] A "changed" event was redundant: every new version of a screen already re-reads all bound parts, so only `answered` is a named event. Do not add a name that the page cannot tell apart from what it already does.
- [P9-06 / a Table test cannot use a team channel for a second team] A channel of one team is not readable by a person of another team, so the "another person sees less" test could not use it → use a direct conversation (no team) with members from two teams; the data they read is then decided only by the tool's own row-level security.
- [P18-01/P9-05 polish] A worktree branch ref (`work`) was moved under a running agent by another session, leaving HEAD ahead of the index and tree (a staged "revert") → check `git status` after a long pause; if HEAD moved, point HEAD at a private branch with `git symbolic-ref` instead of resetting. Also: a dialog that focuses its footer button on open scrolls a tall phone sheet to the bottom → pass `startAtTop` to Dialog; and a screen must not repeat the shell title in `useHeaderTopic`.
- [P9-07 / registering a platform-written card as a surface part] A part in SURFACE_REGISTRY is also offered to every bot in the `surface.render` help (`describeSurfaceParts`), so a card that states provenance (Answer: "Answered for <person> · scoped", numbered sources) could be forged by a bot's screen. Mark such a part `platformOnly` (refused by `SURFACE_BOT_REGISTRY`, left out of the help, drawn only when the page lends `SurfaceHost.answer`); the surface tools must use the bot registry, not `SURFACE_REGISTRY`. In a worktree `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` takes seconds and is simpler than symlinking node_modules.
- [P21-26 / root stylelint glob hangs] Running stylelint with a root glob over the whole repo hangs → run `npx stylelint "clients/web/src/**/*.css"`.
- [P21-26 / touch taps snap to the nearest button] A touch tap next to a name button lands on the button instead of the row → tap away from name buttons in phone tests.
- [P21-26 / two rows share a message id] In serial specs a page-wide `[data-message-id]` matches both the channel row and the sheet row → scope the locator to the channel or the panel.
- [P21-27 / a resize squeeze corrupts the list] A ResizeObserver squeeze while the thread layout was hidden by a resize corrupted the VirtualList's measured row heights → render layout changes synchronously (flushSync) so the list never measures the squeezed frame.
- [P21-26 / vitest picks up worktrees] The vitest glob found tests under `.claude/worktrees` → `**/.claude/**` is now excluded in `vitest.config.ts`.
- [P21-26 / verify spec user] Rafi is not a team lead and gets 403 on creating a channel → the live verify spec signs in as Omar (VERIFY_AS=RAFI to change).
- [P21-01/03 / a continued row keeps its author] A run's continued rows hide the name with `sr-only`, they do not remove it, so tests that count visible names or `.who b` must allow the screen-reader copy (assert `not.toHaveClass(/sr-only/)` on the row that shows it). The reply button's name now ends with "unread" when a dot is shown, and the presence emoji moved from the row to the avatar corner → update locators, do not match old names.
- [P21-01/03 / two same-named controls break strict mode] A second control with the same accessible name (Show more on the clamp and on another part) makes `getByRole` fail under strict mode → scope the locator to the row.
- [P21-01/03 / scrolling to the top races the oldest page] On the phone a one-off `scrollTop = 0` before the oldest page was drawn left the row missing, a flaky pass → wrap scroll and visibility in `expect(...).toPass()`.
- [e2e / a second playwright run wipes test-results] Starting another run while you still need the first run's actual/diff images deletes them → finish reading images before launching the next run; a spec filter is a regex against absolute paths, so use `/e2e/(a|b)/`, not `^a/`.
- [P21-01/03 / `.av` also matches the reply-preview faces] A live probe counting `.av` in a row counted the reply preview's small avatars (`.rp-avs .av`) as the row's own avatar → count `.av:not(.rp-avs .av)`.
- [P21-01/03 / no dark theme to shoot] `emulateMedia({colorScheme:'dark'})` renders the same light page, the app has no dark theme yet → a "dark" shot proves nothing; do not list dark as checked.
- [P21-02 e2e] A spec checked a test id that exists nowhere (thread panel id), so its "not shown" check passed vacuously → first assert the id is visible in a positive case, then the negative one.
- [P21-02 toast] Toasts now stack: a spec reading one `quick-switcher-note` hit two elements (strict mode) → take `.last()` or wait for the earlier toast to go. Also keep the toast's fixed left offset clear of the sidebar and composer.
- [P21-05 Jump to latest] seenUpTo was seeded with the stored last-read id when nothing was unread (null for a never-read channel), so messages arriving while scrolled up were never counted → seed it with the newest loaded id when nothing is unread on open.
- [P21-02/05 live check / the Undo needs a real unread count] A verify script that posted as the same person, or read a channel the app had already read on open, got no Undo on the Esc toast and then hung on clicking it for the whole test timeout → make the other person post, use More > Mark unread from here on THEIR message, then press Esc; pass `--timeout=60000` so a missing button fails fast.
- [P21-02/05 live check / killing a run leaves probe channels] A killed Playwright run skips its afterAll, so verify-p21-02-* channels stay on the live site → archive them by the API; never `pkill -f playwright` from the same shell command.
- [P21-05 / day float pill overlaps Jump to first unread] Both pills are centred at the top of the list and collide (desktop and 390) → give the float pill and the button different offsets in `styles/shell.css`.
- [P21-06 live check / a menu inside an overflow-hidden title is clipped] The details menu lives in `.chead-title` (overflow hidden for the ellipsis), so on the live site only its first row shows; the e2e passes because `toBeVisible` and `getByRole` see clipped items → render the menu outside the title box, and check a menu with a screenshot or by `elementFromPoint` on its last item.
- [P21-06 live check / 390 header overflow] The header's tools overflow the screen when a channel has the voice button and members stack (bookmark, Find and bell past 390 px) and a long name pushes all tools off; the spec checked only the buttons it listed for a short name → measure every header control against the width with a long name and with members.
- [P21-06 live check / DM header facts need set data] Nadia has no status or time zone on the live site, so the DM status and local time could not be seen → set them first through the presence API for a verify run, or accept the gap.
- [P21-06 live check / a verify script waiting on an absent element] `innerText().catch()` waits the full action timeout for a missing element and used up the 60 s test budget → pass `{ timeout: 2000 }`. Also a channel made by the API has its creator outside the member list (the stack showed only the person who joined).
